The second was said when the driving session began to measure the size of the guides in order to point repairers at what had grown. So nothing in this document measures the guides or lists changed lines in order to point a repairer at what to cut. A repairer who holds guides reads them and judges each passage by what becomes of its reader's work without it: the work goes worse (the text is needed and stays), the same (it is unneeded and goes), or better (it is a net loss and goes or is rewritten); and a passage that can be made to serve its reader better is rewritten. The freeze document states the rule and how a judge checks it (목적 진술, 「가이드를 읽어서 판단한다」). It differs in one respect from the rule of the earlier rounds, which the earlier part of this section describes and under which a correction was rejected if it lost any detail a reader could use: in round 18 the measure is what the reader needs. That reading of the user's words is the driving session's, and the user was told of it.

For a repairer and a judge of round 18 three further things follow. Both changes entered the tree between rounds 17 and 18, each written by one agent, and no judge has read either: the user had them made at once so that this round would examine them. What each change is to achieve is the user's decision and is not reopened. How it was carried out, in the program, the tests and the guides, is examined like everything else in the product, and a defect in it is raised as any defect is. And since no round follows this one, what a repairer records without raising goes to the user in the closing report, where earlier rounds handed such things to the next round.

## 4. What the user has decided

This section lists what the user has laid down about how do-math works and how its documents are written, so far as the investigation found it. A correction that reverses or narrows one of these decisions is not a repair. Where a correction would need such a change, the matter is for the user.

The user's decisions for the fourth generation were first collected in an agreement of twenty-five decisions and a set of approved proposals, written in Korean before the rebuild: `임시/do-math-lean-설계/합의.md`. The design record `docs/do-math/lean-canon-design.md` restates them in English with their reasons. Both are among the sources of this run. Two things about them need to be known. First, the design record was deliberately not updated after 22 November; later decisions were written into sections of `docs/do-math/lean-canon-verification.md` and into memory notes, and the sessions adopted the convention that where the design record and the present guides and code differ, the guides and code are current. That convention is an assistant's choice, but the later decisions it serves are the user's and are listed below. Second, the agreement is an assistant's wording of what the user wanted, approved as a whole with the single word "승인" (approved). It binds. But the wording of an approved text has more than once been read more widely than the user meant, so wherever this section can give the user's own words it gives those.

An older source also still applies in part: a list of forty criteria for do-math, derived from the user's statements and approved by the user on 7 November, at `임시/do-math_검증_20261107/사용자기준.md`. Its criteria about mathematical rigor and evidence (group E) were written for the nodes, relations, glossary and record forms of the earlier generations, and were superseded where they name those objects. Its criteria about the purpose and identity of the system (A), a light runner and the sessions' own judgment (B), guides as knowledge and not rules (C), the attitude of research (D), the quality of documents and prose (F), fidelity to the scope of the user's intent (G), the substance of tests (H) and operation and running instances (I) still stand unless a later decision of the user changed the thing they speak of, and several are cited below by their letter and number. The same file lists directions the user rejected (R-1 and following).

Where a guide and the program disagree, neither is the standard by itself. The standard is what the user decided; where that is silent, the agreement and the design record so far as no later decision of the user changed them; and where these too are silent, the repairer judges from the purpose of the mechanism which side is wrong, and says why. Where a guide and the program agree with each other but both depart from the agreement or the design record, and no later decision of the user that accounts for the difference is found in this document, in the verification record, or in the message and the memory note of the commit that brought the behaviour in, the matter is put to the user and not turned back by a correction: a decision may have been made that the records do not show.

### 4.1 What the fourth generation is for

The user's brief for the rebuild (session `d778f997`, 21 November 15:14:03Z; the same text in session `e13e3c64`) asked for five things: that the problem and every proposition and relation around it be expressed in Lean and laid out, not only a few key statements; that the record of mathematical content, one per Lean element and native to Lean's structure, be separated from the record of process; that sessions be able to traverse the canon mechanically and deterministically, and be told to, so that repeated work drops; that a script calling AI sessions produce a paper and its PDF from the canon; and that the use of Lean be fused into the whole of the work, "다만 자원 소비가 효율적인 방식으로" (but in a way that consumes resources efficiently). The brief ends with a principle that governs how old and new are to be weighed:

> "만약 지금 기존의 절차와 이 새로운 제안이 서로 충돌한다 느낀다면 애시당초 기존의 절차를 개선하는 것이 목적이기 때문이야. 그러니 어떻게 하면 기존의 절차를 이 새로운 제안에도 불구하고 유지할 수 있을까, 는 애시당초 방향이 틀린 고민인거지."

(If the existing procedure and this proposal seem to conflict, that is because improving the existing procedure is the purpose; asking how to keep the old procedure in spite of the proposal is the wrong question from the start.)

Two requirements from the design conversation bear directly on bloat inside an instance. The user wanted records not to keep accumulating, "런타임이 길어져도 bloat이 최소화되도록" (so that bloat stays minimal even as the run grows long; 16:30:52Z), and wanted what sessions learn to accumulate by the object it concerns and not one record per session (16:34:29Z). And on unformalized results the user set a direction (17:37:23Z): the rule is not "discard what is not expressed in Lean" but "express it in Lean and keep it", and expressing things in Lean is to be a central step of the work from its start, "자연어 처리의 위에 더해지는 chore가 아니라" (not a chore added on top of work done in natural language).

The agreement, and the design record after it, list what the fourth generation left as it was, and the user approved that list with the rest: the six roles and the order in which the runner launches them; the way questions are proposed and admitted; concerns, findings and the comprehensive review; the two acceptance settings (section 4.5); that a session's lease and section hold until its work is published; the reconsideration of a session's ending, by which a session that ends is resumed to judge again whether it should; that the original material of a session is kept, and the limit on who reads it; English inside an instance; and the governing principles of the earlier design record `docs/do-math/rebuild-design.md`, which remains in force for whatever the new record does not change.

### 4.2 The sessions judge; the machine does not

The oldest and most often repeated of the user's requirements is that judgments of mathematical meaning belong to the sessions and that the program records and carries out what they decide (criteria B1 to B4). The program refuses only what a machine can decide: that Lean confirms a statement is the same as another or its negation, that something compiles, which axioms a proof depends on, names and hashes, the form of files, who holds a lease. A correction that makes the program judge mathematical content, the faithfulness of a statement, the sufficiency of an argument or the meaning of an overlap between sections goes against this.

There is no planning role and no assignment of work: exactly six roles, and attempts choose their own work (criterion A2).

The limits placed on sessions rest on their cooperation, and this is deliberate. When a comparison of the skill against outside standards reported that sessions can write to every file the checks rely on, the user answered (session `20ae7c1d`, 22 November 05:36:20Z): "restrictions are intentionally reliant on session's cooperation for effectiveness, efficiency, and simplicity." The absence of an enforcing mechanism is therefore not a defect, and a correction should not add enforcing wording or machinery. On 26 November, on reading an evaluation of the skill, the user said the same of the system as a whole (sections 3 and 21): it is "designed to rely mostly on the good behavior of models and their instruction following capability, rather than setting restrictions and blocks to go around, for simplicity of system".

The same holds for the operator. During a verification run on 10 November the user gave this instruction, which later freeze documents have carried forward (original in `임시/do-math_검증_20261110-2/동결.md:76-80`):

> "there is no need to consider a case of intentionally destructive and hostile operator or issues that would arise only in such a circumstance. on the contrary, if such considerations lead to rule-ification of what should be sessions' semantic judgement, restrict valuable degree of freedom, or overengineer the system into unnecessary complexity, or create rules that does not improve the effectiveness of research and/or decrease its effectiveness, such considerations are to be actively addressed"

So a problem that arises only when the operator deliberately breaks the system is not a defect, and code, text or tests that exist only to guard against such an operator, and that cost freedom or simplicity, are themselves defects.

On orchestration the user said (session `3171182f`, 23 November 00:34:54Z) that an orchestrating agent either knows the details of every area it delegates, in which case it need not delegate, or does not, in which case it gives directives that constrain the worker who meets the actual detail; "So, it is better to just set governance rules and tools that help agents orchestrate themselves, in my opinion." A correction that adds central direction of the sessions' work runs against this.

When sessions evaluated do-math this week the user corrected them more than once for judging by proxies and for treating deliberate positions as failures: evaluate "without mistaking any of its key qualities as failures or evaluating trade-offs as mistakes" (session `5172428b`, 22 November 14:18:33Z), and "Don't rely on proxies as sole basis of your evaluation; actually read what is there, what is implemented, and judge based on what actually is" (15:35:11Z). This document takes the same standard to apply to what a repairer calls a defect.

### 4.3 How the guides are written

**Knowledge for judgment, not rules.** The guides that worker sessions read (`roles/`, `resume/`, and the documents of `reference/` in their packs) are meant to give a session the understanding from which it judges, not conditions and commands (criteria C1 to C3, F1 to F5, G2). The operator's documents (`SKILL.md`, `reference/operations.md`) describe procedures and commands and are not under this limit. Nor are the method document of the paper command and the instruction files of the finalize pipeline: the first is the fixed way that the user asked a script to supply (section 4.10), and the second are the instructions of a program that holds the checks itself (section 4.11). A correction does not add if-then rules, lists of things to refuse, closed lists of considerations, mandatory fields for justification, or gates. A choice left to the session is written as reasons, costs and open examples. This limit does not apply to the contracts that a machine or another role consumes: the form of an output file, the arguments of a command.

On 21 November the user decided that the way of working and review's criteria for accepting a proof should nonetheless be written down explicitly, and in what manner (session `1f8bb240`, 14:37:22Z):

> "명시적 규범으로 적자, 다만 판단의 여지 없이 hard하게 적용하는 것이 아니라 soft하게 그를 guide하는 방식으로"

(Write them as explicit norms, but not applied hard with no room for judgment; in a way that softly guides it.) They are to be phrased directly: "the user asks ... 가 아니라 you should ... 식으로" (not "the user asks ..." but "you should ..."; 15:49:50Z). The essay `reference/what-research-is-for.md` was to be left as written, and its relation to the new norms explained in a paragraph of `reference/common.md` (14:44:01Z). That essay and `reference/user-writing-preferences.md` carry the user's own account of what research is for and of how the user wants things written. Earlier verification runs treated both as the user's texts and left them unchanged, and they are not among the files this run covers.

**English, with no reason given.** Everything inside an instance is written in English: "그냥 다 영어로 하자 인스턴스 내부 모든걸" (14:17:33Z). The user gave no reason and the guides state none; a correction should not supply one. The user's words reach an instance only as an English translation, and the operator keeps the original outside it (the same session, 14:30:50Z).

**What the user's conditions say, and nothing added.** In the parking instance, several things that review sessions and other sessions did wrong came from sentences the operator had added around the user's words: a reason the user had not given, a broader reading than the user meant. The operator guidance now tells the operator to convey what the user said and nothing more, and the same care applies to the guides themselves. In particular the reason for holding back a correct proof that can still be made simpler is that the simpler proof is more concise, and nothing else; the user rejected the reasons the operator had invented (cost of formalization, number of elements named). Section 4.5 gives the words.

**Definitions before use, and no private terms.** While the norms were being written the user had the session check its English against a rule that has since entered the repository's instructions: a sentence of the form "X is Y" burdens the reader when Y is a term the writer coined or one that needs context, so such a term is either avoided or defined before it is used (15:17:17Z). The session unpacked words it had been using as names, such as "room", "held back", "retreat", "indicator", "ledger".

**Generally applicable, not fitted to this machine.** When a session wrote into a guide where the imported library's sources lie on this computer, the user had it rewritten (session `54d4d2f6`, 22 November 20:05:47Z): "rather than saying something that is tailored to this local environment, say something more generally applicable like, check input.json or whatever". Guides state what follows from the runner's contract with a session, not the layout of the machine on which they were written.

**A question may or may not have the form of a statement.** The guides once defined a question as something "that has no form as a statement". The user corrected the reading (session `0860eb60`, 23 November 00:17:50Z): the phrase "was intended to be "inclusive" to even those without a statement, not to be "exclusive" only to those". The three definitions were reworded, and one sentence was added with the user's approval: "A question can also be about a statement, such as whether a conjecture left as a lead holds." The commit that made this change (`80625feb`) also added two paragraphs to `reference/common.md` on where sessions meet the canon, at the user's request ("do that passage explaining which places sessions are led to", 00:23:49Z). That the passage describes and does not prescribe is the session's choice, made in keeping with the first paragraph of this section.

**"No" is an answer.** Looking at explorations that kept working without closing their questions, the user said (session `ce7d1dca`, 22 November 06:03:00Z): "그러면 각 탐구들은 질문에 답한거 아닌가? No 도 답이잖아" (Then didn't each exploration answer its question? "No" is an answer too). The criterion for resolving a question was rewritten across the guides with the user's approval (06:46:53Z), including a paragraph giving the case. The user also preferred input that helps explorers work better in general over input that says "do this specifically" (06:15:55Z).

**Research conduct the user asked the guides to convey.** Sessions are to search actively for prior work and cite it "모든 것을 직접 재발명하는 대신" (instead of reinventing everything themselves; session `1f8bb240`, 15:02:47Z); an attempt is strongly encouraged to search and judges what it finds. The literature server is attached to every instance by default, opened only to sources that need no login (the user's choice among three options, 15:25:06Z; made a default of the code on 22 November). A finite extension of the range over which something has been checked is not in itself an achievement; closing what remained, or gaining structure, pattern, insight or a lead while extending, is, and the examples are an open list whose common feature is that they change what is known (decision of 10 November, memory note `M-0687`; a correction that turns the examples into a list of conditions narrows the principle).

### 4.4 What counts as proved

Three things are fixed by the agreement and by criterion A3. The default allowed axioms are `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`, and `sorryAx` is never allowed. A complete Lean proof brought by a session is confirmed only after review has compiled the candidate itself. And the standard for the instance's completion, a formal proof of the root or of its negation, is not to be lowered.

This week the question of what must be trusted beyond Lean was decided. A second check of formal proofs outside the program's own index, here called the formal judge, had been added on 22 November with the user's approval: the compiled declarations were exported and compared by an external program, `lake comparator`. On 24 November, after it had stopped a live runner, the user asked what it was for and then decided (session `20538f79`, 12:42:15Z and 12:46:54Z):

> "is that really necessary? if statement is true and if lean is compiled then it is proved, as long as we do it right. It feels wasteful to create this elaborate setup to prepare for a case where it is not done right, instead of just doing it right"
>
> "I feel we are very much overcomplicating a very much simple issue, on the issue of lean's reliability"

The formal judge was removed, and the program now takes the axioms a declaration depends on from Lean's own report. A correction should not reintroduce a formal judge in any form: an export of the compiled declarations, a second program that checks them again, or a comparison by an external comparator. The program still compares the root's statement and the definitions under it with the instance's first commit when the root becomes formal (the "root check"). That check sees less than the formal judge did: it does not see a private helper of a definition, nor a definition whose unchanged text Lean now reads as a different term. The user was told, and on 27 November decided that the modules of the root's definitions are locked against publications and that the check compares their files (section 16.28, carried out in round 14). What the check still does not see after that is a known limit, not a defect to repair by rebuilding what was removed.

Review compiles a candidate itself without the cache although the peer has compiled it already. The over-engineering audit listed this as duplication; the user kept it: "review recompile is planned redundancy, it ensures that they compile" (13:46:18Z).

The user considered replacing the Lean broker's batch compiles with a live Lean server and decided against it: "I think it would be better to just keep the broker as is then" (session `ecf4e723`, 22 November 17:26:40Z).

### 4.5 When review accepts a correct proof

Review asks two things of a proof it has found correct before accepting it: whether the proof can still clearly be made simpler, and whether it closes part of the statement by large computation done without understanding. The guides call these the two considerations; the settings call them the acceptance settings (`acceptance.decline_reducible_proofs` and `acceptance.decline_uncomprehended_computation`, both true by default); section 4.3 called them review's criteria for accepting a proof. The user decided two separate things about them (session `1f8bb240`, 21 November 14:34:36Z and 14:42:44Z). They apply in every instance unless a setting turns one off, which is what the user's "기본값은 강하게 적용" (the default applies strongly) referred to, as the user explained when the session had read it otherwise (14:42:44Z: "기본값이 강하게 적용이라 말했을 때는 범위를 말한 것이었고", when I said the default applies strongly I was speaking of the scope). And their content is guidance for review's judgment, with no thresholds.

These considerations began as conditions the user gave to the parking instance, and the user corrected the operator's rendering of them three times. The corrections fix their meaning (session `58a24e38`):

- The reason for the first (21 November 03:14:03Z): "선언된 의존이 형식화 비용이라서 줄이라 한게 아니야. 그냥 줄일 수 있으면 그게 더 간결하니까 줄이라 한거지." (I did not say to reduce because declared dependencies cost formalization effort. I said to reduce because, if it can be reduced, that is more concise.)
- On computation (the same day, 03:14:03Z and 03:32:48Z): the problem is not that a proof relies on computed results; "내가 바라는 것은 계산 결과로 당장 그것이 참이란 것을 보일 수 있다 해도 가능하면 왜 참인지를 설명해주는 보다 수학적인 연구를 계속 수행하란 것" (what I want is that, even if computation can show at once that it is true, the more mathematical research that explains why it is true continues where possible). And: "내가 말한건, 무식하게 이해 없이 큰 단위 계산을 하는 것이었고 모든 계산을 금지한게 아니야" (what I meant was large-scale computation done crudely, without understanding; I did not forbid all computation). A case the user judged not to be large-scale: reading a few dozen facts and a few thousand entries from a table already published (21 November 09:01:48Z, "이 정도는 큰 단위 계산이 아니라 생각해").
- On citing part of a published result (03:04:42Z): "일부라고 해도 써야 하면 안 쓸 이유는 없잖아?" (even if only part of it is used, if it is needed there is no reason not to use it).
- The same criterion applies to a proof that derives one statement from another (20 November 23:18:25Z).
- Every session is to judge by reading the actual texts, "단순 피상적인 지표들을 계산해 그를 통해 프록시로 판단을 내리는게 아니라" (not by computing superficial indicators and judging through them as proxies; 20 November 16:35:13Z).

One request from the same session reached the guides only on 25 November. When a review session rejected a correct result because of one wrong side sentence, the user asked that review be able to support the result and leave a note, added this as a condition of the run, and said (22 November 04:26:56Z): "상시 조건으로 넣자, 그리고 검토자 지침에도 반영해줘 정본에" (put it in as a standing condition, and also reflect it in the guidance for review, in the canonical tree). The scope the user finally set was narrow (04:34:38Z): "지금 문제가 되었던 것 하나만. 사소하고 검토자가 어떤 점에서 왜 다르게 보아야 하는지를 의견으로 남기는 것 만으로 차후 그 지지된 결과를 읽을 이들이 올바르게 문제 없이 결과를 받아 정확하게 사용할 수 있는 경우 vs 아닌 경우" (only the one thing that was the problem now: the case where the flaw is minor, and where the reviewer's leaving a note of what is to be read differently and why is enough for those who later read the result to take it correctly and use it accurately, as against the case where that is not so). In the third generation a result that review found sound was "supported"; the request is that review, in the first case, not reject the result for the flaw. The session that received the request added only a standing input to the instance then running. The verification round of the morning of 25 November carried it into `roles/review.md`, as a paragraph that gives the user's distinction as the thing to judge by. That paragraph can be corrected like any other passage; what it has to convey is fixed by the user's words above. Whether the other guides that a review session receives agree with it has not been checked by anyone.

### 4.6 Mechanisms the user kept, removed or settled

**Kept, after the over-engineering audit of 24 November.** The audit listed things it thought excessive, and the user answered item by item (session `20538f79`, 13:46:18Z): "1. keep real paper read. 2. review recompile is planned redundancy, it ensures that they compile. 3. compile service feature. 4. intended. 5. intended. 6. fix subreviewer write report stuff. 7. have state file be written once every 10 seconds or on request. 8. if elk is not used and is worse, why is it there? if it really is not needed then clear." The numbers follow the audit's own list. That the first five answers mean that the thing is kept is how the session restated them to the user in its next message, and the user did not object; for the third the user's three words say no more by themselves. The five are: (1) the paper command's eight readings of the manuscript beside real papers; (2) review's own uncached compile; (3) the Lean broker's calibration of memory and its statistical rule for admitting compiles; (4) the `.agents` mirror; (5) the guides as each session receives them. The audit's item spoke of the size of a pack, of one guide that goes to all roles, and of twelve rules stated in several documents (section 3 quotes it in full). The run read the answer, up to its round 1, as keeping both which guides each role receives and the statement of one rule in several documents. On 26 November the user said they were not sure the answer had applied to the restated rules, and gave the way in which repetition is to be restructured (section 3). What stands of this answer is which guides each role receives. A proposal to cut any of these five goes against an explicit decision. Padding in the sense of section 3 was not part of what the audit put to the user. For the third, the audit separately asked whether the broker should exist as a build system of its own beside Lake, and that question the user did not answer (section 4.13).

**Removed, and not to be brought back without a new decision.**
- *Reuse of Lean's state after import ("header snapshots")* in the broker. It was an approved proposal of the agreement; it acquired an exception during implementation, and the user removed it altogether (session `d778f997`, 22 November 04:31:28Z): "그냥 스냅숏을 쓰지 말자 그럼. 예외 경우로 인해 실익이 떨어지면서 복잡성은 추가되는거 같아" (then let's just not use snapshots; the exception case lowers the benefit while adding complexity).
- *The formal judge* (section 4.4).
- *ELK*, an optional layout engine for the dependency map: "쓰이지 않고 더 나쁘면 치우라" as the note records it; the user's words are item 8 above.
- *Report files of sub-reviewers.* A sub-reviewer's report is its final message; item 6 above.

**Settled this week.**
- *The broker writes its state file at most every ten seconds or on request* (item 7 above). The agent that implemented this kept two immediate writes and gave its reasons: one before a compile starts, without which a broker started next could launch a second Lean for the same job, and one after a compile ends, without which a broker started next would finish the job again with a stale measurement of memory. Those two are an assistant's choice, made within the decision as the agent read it.
- *A refuted root is a result.* "루트가 반박으로 끝났으면 그건 루트가 반증되었단 결과를 얻고 끝나야지." (If the root ended in refutation, the research should end with the result that the root was disproved; `d778f997`, 22 November 04:17:56Z.) The dependency map was changed to show it; the particular drawing is the assistant's.
- *Replacing the proof of a settled statement.* The agreement had said that when a better statement supersedes an old one, the old statement's proof is replaced by a one-line derivation from the new statement. That was found to be blocked when the old statement is settled, and the user set the rule: the proof of a settled statement is replaced only by a proof under which the statement stays settled. So the old statement keeps its proof until the new statement is itself settled, because (04:17:56Z) "애시당초 해결되지 않은 새 진술이라면 그게 해결된 진술보다 더 나은 진술일 수 없지 않나?" (a new statement that is not settled cannot be a better statement than a settled one). The four detailed conditions in `runtime/check.py` are the assistant's implementation of it.
- *How long the CLI waits for a session's background work.* Two hours, 7,200,000 ms: "yeah, let's place a limit of 2 hours" (session `0860eb60`, 22 November 20:22:27Z).
- *A publication build that fails for a reason of execution.* A session evaluating the skill reported as its fourth defect that such a failure (a lost host, a restart, a cancellation) is charged to the candidate, which then goes to a replacement peer and a new review, while `reference/operations.md` says the same publication continues. Asked how to dispose of six defects, the user answered for this one "4) pass" (session `3750b5d4`, 22 November 18:29:45Z), beside "apply" for the others. The session took this to mean that the behaviour is left as it is, and the user did not contradict it. Whether the sentence of the document still disagrees with it was not checked; making a document say what the program does is a separate matter and does not decide the behaviour. Changing the behaviour itself would decide what the user's word left standing, and is the user's to decide.
- *Attempts are not held back while a peer or a review waits* (decision after the verification run of 10 November, memory note `M-0698`), and *the operator can post questions with `questions --file`*, which is an official source of questions beside those review admits (decision during the same run).
- *The index extraction runs without Lean's default limit on work* (`set_option maxHeartbeats 0`), approved by the user after a large canon stopped at the limit.
- *The paper does not mention Lean* (section 4.10).
- *The user had the running sessions told not to waste processor and memory on polling* ("tell sessions to not waste cpu and memory on things like polling and stuff", sessions `a494bae3` and `335efa8d`, 22 November 15:48Z to 15:50Z). The changes to waiting described in section 5.2 followed from it.

### 4.7 How a defect is to be repaired

The user has said a good deal this week about what a good repair is, mostly in response to repairs that were not.

**Find the cause and repair that.** Dispatching an agent on an incident, the user asked for "the real, root cause and full solution, not something shallow and surface, something that is simple, effective, and robust, and accurate" (session `0860eb60`, 24 November 11:07:50Z).

**Do not build a safeguard for a state the system should not reach; prevent the state.** The agent's repair for that incident included a path that recovered when a folder the runner needed had been deleted. The user refused it (11:49:12Z):

> "so the issue is that it may be removed, right? and it is a case that should not happen in the first place. I think building a failsafe for that exception is the wrong path to take. the better path is to just avoid it, plain and simple, I think"

The only thing that could delete the folder was the system's own clean-up rule, so the rule was changed and the recovery path, its test and its sentence in the guide were taken out. The note of that session states the lesson as a question to ask of any failure state: who creates this state, and if the system does, repair the rule first.

**Simplicity.** "Simplicity is best." and "If a solution for any problem can be implemented before any runs, at this time now, then just be repeated reliably without much thoughts, that is the best." (session `20538f79`, 24 November 14:32:28Z). That is: a solution that can be put in place once, ahead of time, and then simply relied on is better than one that needs judgment each time it is used. The formal judge was removed on the same ground.

**Delete what is not used.** "delete what is not used" (session `20538f79`, 24 November 11:53:14Z); "delete leftovers." (the same session, 10:40:29Z). The earlier verification runs of this skill, on the user's instruction, treated unused elements and copies of one rule in the program and its tests as damage to remove, and held that "the behaviour is the same" is not by itself a reason to leave them; for repetition in the guides section 3 applies. A file that the program writes into an instance and that nothing reads is a different matter, because removing it changes what a live instance holds: the audit raised three such files, and the user has not answered (section 4.13).

**Repair the instruction, not the agent's freedom.** When agents duplicated work because their briefs told them to, and a session then began building a guard that forbade it, the user said (session `20538f79`, 23 November 01:43:46Z): "you told duplicate workers to do duplicate work, then when they began doing it you tried to add a new ban that would forbid it, when you could simply have told them to just do what they should do in the first place?"

**No compatibility for earlier generations.** The skill does not carry branches for the formats of the third generation and before, migration code or preserved aliases (criterion A4). Section 5.4 describes code that reads what instances of the fourth generation, created earlier this week, still hold. That this code is outside the criterion is this document's reading; it rests on the user having had that code installed together with the removal of the formal judge on 25 November.

**A failure ends the runner; it is not isolated, and nothing restarts the runner.** The program does not pass through an undefined state. A refusal becomes a bounded wait; a failure of the runner's own operation ends the runner with the error recorded, and the operator starts it again after the repair. Isolating the failing piece of work and carrying on, and a watchdog that restarts the runner, are among the directions the user rejected (criteria B5 and B6, and rejected direction R-19 of the criteria file). Sessions that evaluate the skill keep reporting this as a gap; it is a decision.

**The later decision governs** (criterion G4), and a decided matter is not reopened by a correction. When a mechanism the user approved has since acquired exceptions, a repairer who writes a proposal for the user about it should include removing the mechanism among the options; twice this month the user chose a removal that the assistant's options had not offered.

### 4.8 Settings whose values the user chose

The values of these settings are the user's decisions, and a correction that would change one goes to the user: the default number of concurrent sessions; the order of models for each role, including that peer and review prefer a different model from the producing roles; the request for a context window of one million tokens and the threshold at which a session's context is compacted; the limit on a Claude worker's output; the interval between launches of attempts and explorations; the limit on resumptions of a session that ends (`max_end_resumptions`, 3); how often an exploration and an organizer are requested; the two acceptance settings; the two-hour wait for background work; and the default attachment of the literature server in its login-free mode. At commit `162b9e5e` the values, unchanged by the last two commits, are: at most 8 sessions at once; a new attempt or exploration no sooner than 180 seconds after the last; an exploration requested with every attempt launch and an organizer after every 4 publications; the producing roles on `codex/gpt-6.1-sol` and then `claude/opus`, peer and review on `claude/opus` and then `codex/gpt-6.1-sol`, all at the highest effort; a context window of 1,000,000 tokens with compaction at 900,000; an output limit of 128,000 tokens; at most 3 resumptions; both acceptance settings on; 7,200,000 ms of waiting for background work. They stand in `runtime/settings.py` and `runtime/launch.py` and are described in `reference/operations.md`. On 26 November the user raised the compaction window to 950,000 tokens and had a message sent to a session whose context reaches 800,000 (sections 16.17 and 22), and then decided that automatic compaction is switched off. Since round 13 a Claude worker that receives messages is launched with it off, and every other session keeps the window of 950,000 tokens (section 16.24).

### 4.9 The mirror and the running research

The mirror `.agents/skills/do-math` is kept (item 4 of the over-engineering audit). It is produced only by the generator script, never edited by hand (criterion I2); several commits of this week did edit it by hand on the user's instruction while an instance of the earlier generation was running from it, and it was regenerated in full on 23 November at the user's word. Since then commits to do-math regenerate it.

Running research is protected (criterion I1), and it is not paused to make room for other work. When a session paused the sunflower instance to free memory the user had it undone (session `20538f79`, 23 November 09:59:40Z, "undo the pause"). Long runs of a command, such as a paper run, have been started by the user from the user's own terminal; when a session was about to start one and raise its priority, the user said that they would start it themselves (recorded in memory note `M-0765`).

### 4.10 The paper command

The paper command comes from the same brief as the fourth generation (section 4.1). What the user asked of it there (session `d778f997`, 21 November 18:32:49Z):

> "명령 하나로 끝까지, 다만 모든걸 스스로 정하는게 아니라 어떤 방식을 따라야 할지는 사전에 정해져서 스크립트에 의해 동봉. 목표는 root을 해결하는 lean 증명으로부터 … 표준적인 수학 논문의 형식, 방식, 구조, 표현, 작문 … 에 부합하는 방식으로 영어 논문을 생성하는 것. … 구현 세부사항들에 대응해서(예:// 증명 A가 증명 B로 대체시 A는 B로 증명된다로 갈음) 효과적으로 군더더기 없이 깔끔하고 정확하고 간결하게 작성되어야 함(예:// 정리 1. A는 B로 증명 된다. 정리 2. B는 ..., 이런 것은 피해야 해)"

(One command to the end; the command does not decide everything by itself, the way to follow is fixed in advance and supplied by the script. The aim is to produce, from the Lean proof that settles the root, an English paper that conforms to the form, manner, structure, expression and writing of a standard mathematical paper. It is to deal with the details of implementation, for instance by writing that A is proved by B where proof A was replaced by proof B, and to be clean, exact and concise without padding; "Theorem 1. A is proved by B. Theorem 2. B is ..." is the kind of thing to avoid.) The fixed way is `reference/paper-method.md`, which the program hands to its sessions.

The decisions that followed, each in the user's words. Those of 21 and 22 November are from session `d778f997`, those of 23 and 24 November from session `20538f79`:

- **Real papers are models of style, not sources.** The user asked that the writing sessions actually consult many real mathematical papers and that repeated independent readings verify that the manuscript reads like them (22 November 07:02Z). When the session then spoke of citing them, the user corrected it (07:23:58Z): "인용? 내가 바란 것은 인용할 문서가 아니라(그건 do-math 연구가 어차피 하니까) '이런게 실제 논문 스타일이니 이 방식으로 해라' 라는걸 알려줄 대상이었는데" (Citation? What I wanted was not documents to cite, the research does that anyway, but something to show "this is what real paper style is, do it this way"). The collection under `exemplars/` and the readings of the manuscript beside real papers come from this.
- **Meaning is not checked by script.** The first version had a script verify quotations that readers gave. The user asked (07:26:59Z): "의미론적 내용을 담은걸 스크립트로 확인해서 무엇을 얻지?" (What do we gain by checking, with a script, something that carries semantic content?) The program now checks only the form of what a reading session returns.
- **The paper does not mention Lean.** "내 생각에 lean에 대한 설명과 논문은 별도로 분리하는게 나을 것 같아. 지금은 논문만 작성하고 그건 lean에 대한 언급 없이 자연어적으로 성립되어 있게 하자" (I think the explanation of Lean and the paper are better kept apart; for now write only the paper, and let it stand in natural language with no mention of Lean; 08:41:03Z).
- **At most eight readings beside real papers.** The user raised the limit from four to eight on 22 November ("독해 차이는 네 번의 시도가 상한인건가? 만약 그렇다면 여덟번까지 높이고 싶어", 09:40:39Z) and kept the eight readings when the over-engineering audit questioned them ("1. keep real paper read.", session `20538f79`, 24 November 13:46:18Z).
- **The redesign of 23 November.** After a run had spent 105 minutes on a plan and four of fifteen sections, the user said (session `20538f79`, 12:05:23Z to 12:11:34Z): "I feel that it is better to rethink how paper.py works from the drawing board", with the aim "maintain quality while increasing efficiency". Planning from a digest, a brief per section, sections written side by side and resumption came out of it. An interrupted run's old plan is not reused ("let's just start anew", 12:15:52Z).
- **Findings are acted on.** On 24 November the user asked "why is paper.py setup to find issues and not act on them?" (04:52:11Z). Of three changes the session offered, the user answered "not 1, but do 2 and 3" (04:53:38Z). Rejected: giving later readers the list of departures that earlier repairs had declined. Adopted: a narrow repair after the last reading, whose changes are read and undone when they are unsound; and a way to take up the open findings of a finished run (`--open-findings`).
- **A proof contains no plan of itself.** "yes, change paper-method.md to the first paper's rule, and have it be a system rule in general for paper generation" (24 November 10:01:45Z). The rule is that a proof begins with its first step. The user wanted no machine check for it.
- **The draft before the freeze, and the author line**, come from the interview for the finalize pipeline (section 4.11): the paper's statements are drafted before the library is frozen, and the author line comes from configuration and is empty by default.

Three things in the present command were derived by the assistant and accepted by the user through the approval of the finalize agreement as a whole. They bind as part of that approved whole; they are singled out here because the user did not word them: that a result from the literature is cited in the paper and not proved again; that the running text of the paper is compared with the library (the stage `coverage`); and that findings left open after the readings are resolved inside the command (the stage `resolve`).

### 4.11 The finalize pipeline

The pipeline was asked for on 24 November, after the user and a session had packaged the finished parking research by hand into `solved/parking-unimodality/`. The order of that work kept sending it back to products it had already made. The user described what had gone wrong (session `20538f79`, 11:58:09Z, "edit A, which leads to editing B, which requires updating C, then we edit A again, so on") and drew the line between what is acceptable and what is not (12:02:32Z):

> "fixing issues is fine. issues have to be fixed even later if discovered. what is not okay is having issues in later stages that needs to be fixed to begin with. what is even not okay is setting up workflow dependencies and sequence such that prior work having to be updated after the later work in an avoidable way(e.g. could have been avoided by just setting up a better workflow)"

The request for the pipeline followed (14:32:28Z). Its central sentences:

> "I don't want a pipeline that would repeat back into its previous stages like we often did; I want lean proof selection and preparing, proof writing, document organizing and validating, etc, to all be self contained and dependable, such that I can be assured that whenever any step is finished, we can just rely on it to be good and move on without thinking more about it. I also want the process to be efficient while retaining value and effectiveness. ... I want something that can just be run and waited till it finishes, and I want it do be done very good while being as fast and efficient as possible. ... If a solution for any problem can be implemented before any runs, at this time now, then just be repeated reliably without much thoughts, that is the best. Simplicity is best."

In the interview that followed the user answered eight questions and added a ninth point, and then approved the whole with the word "approved" (15:59:58Z). The user's words, with what the assistant derived from each and showed to the user before the approval:

| Matter | The user's words (session `20538f79`, 24 November, UTC) | What the assistant derived from them and the user approved with the whole |
|---|---|---|
| The package | "it should contain the full package; dependency map, history, lean, knowledge, paper, records, readme, etc etc, and I want it to be something that I can just zip and upload for distribution(e.g. in zenodo)" (14:48:37Z) | The folder stands alone: nothing in it points outside itself or says that something exists only on this machine, and its build instructions work from the unpacked archive. |
| The library | "done what we tried to do(in different forms through different attempts, but reference them to make something good and coherent) this session" (15:00:22Z) | Seven steps: take what the chosen proof needs, review, simplify, merge, arrange, records, freeze. |
| The freeze | "the frozen library should already start with all that the paper would need inside as written, it should be worked on such that the paper will not face a situation where it has to write something within it outside of what frozen library contains" (15:06:32Z) | The paper's statements are drafted before the freeze and the gaps are closed in the library; after the freeze the paper states only what the library has, and the library is never reopened. |
| Which proof | "I want its decision to be part of the pipeline; taking a deep, good look at what proof to build, among options, then picking the one that is best" (15:30:11Z) | One proof per package. The selection ends in a written decision that fixes the main theorem before any library work. |
| What "best" means | "the staircase is a better one. but the direct proof is also quite okay. insight is good. elegance and conciseness is also good. following mathematical research standards is required" (15:35:45Z) | Research standards are a threshold; above it insight comes first, then elegance and conciseness. A result already in the literature is cited in the paper and not proved again there, and every cited source is found and checked. |
| The history | "a somewhat more digestible version, than just reading them directly, of the research records timeline" (15:39:53Z) | Written from the research's reviewed record only, never from transcripts; each entry points to its record; it makes no claims of its own and does not retell the mathematics. |
| Author and licence | "have a configurable name and others, with default name as empty(as in, empty empty), and license as cc by 4.0" (15:45:29Z) | No placeholder is printed: without a name the paper has no author line and no author appears anywhere else. The run does not stop to ask the user anything. Findings still open after the paper's eight readings are resolved inside the pipeline. |
| The end | "keep it in solved, update README to list it, clean any unneeded temporary files created, and be done" (15:50:06Z) | No commit and no push by the run. |
| The registry | "+ there should also be a prepared palomar submission stuff(https://palomar-registry.org/how-to-submit)" (15:55:09Z) | The registry's rules become requirements of the library stage; its four files are made at the freeze; the author fields feed them; AI and human review are stated as they were, the default saying that no person reviewed the proof. The pipeline prepares and does not submit. The comparator (the registry's checking program, `lake comparator`) was to be run once while the pipeline was built, and a pipeline run does not repeat it. |

The summary the user approved also carried five choices that the assistant marked as its own and put up for confirmation: that a program, and no chat session, conducts the stages; that every loop has a fixed number of passes; "No port to a newer Lean unless you configure one"; that results from the literature are cited in the paper; and that the knowledge archive holds the research's library and derived views "as of the commit that settled the root, without session transcripts".

Earlier decisions of the user bind the pipeline as they bind the rest of the skill, and four of them shape it visibly. The paper does not mention Lean, so it is the package's README and its table of correspondence, and not the paper, that say what is formally proved (section 4.10). The paper's eight readings beside real papers stay (section 4.6). A library is verified by compiling it and reading Lean's own list of axioms, so the pipeline has no second check of the frozen library: no other program checks the proofs again, there is no formal judge, and the comparator is not run in each pipeline run (section 4.4). And of a second proof of a result the user said (24 November 10:40:29Z): "on second proof, the goal is to create an independent new proof, so mentioning first proof in second proof is to be avoided." From this comes the rule in `finalize/common.md` that every text of a package presents the chosen proof by itself, without mention of other proofs, of the research, of its sessions or of the run.

What follows was fixed by the design that the assistant coordinating the build checked against the agreement before anything was built. A reader of the code could take any of it for an accident, and a correction that "simplifies" one of these undoes a deliberate arrangement. Where an item rests on a decision of the user the table above shows it. The rest are the builder's and the coordinator's choices, made to keep the finalize agreement: a correction may change one of those where it shows that the reason given for it does not hold, and not because the arrangement looks unnecessary.

- **The program holds the order of stages and every exit check; no session decides either.** The operator's guide counts seven stages: the start, the selection, the library, the history with the archive, the map with the paper, the documents, and the final check. The library stage has seven steps of its own, the last of which is the freeze. The loops of the hand packaging came from a session deciding the order as it went.
- **The order.** The selection comes first and fixes the main theorem. The history and the archive are made beside the library stage, because they need only the research's record. The paper's draft is made inside the library stage, before the freeze, and its gaps feed a single fold-back into the library. The map comes before the paper. The documents come after the paper, because every figure in them is read by script from finished products. The final check runs once.
- **A product is written once.** A later step compares a product's hash with the run's state before reading it; a difference stops the run and names the product, and nothing is silently made again. "Frozen" means that after the freeze no Lean file and no record changes, and the final check compares their hashes again. When the paper wants a sentence the library does not cover, the sentence is cut or reworded.
- **Only simplifications may be discarded.** A part of the simplifying, arranging or fold-back steps that fails its check is dropped and keeps its previous form. Every other failed exit check stops the run with exit code 2, and the same command continues it. Every count of passes is fixed, with no "repeat until clean", because eight readings of the first paper never converged.
- **The pipeline's and the paper command's sessions cannot schedule themselves.** They are launched without the tools `ScheduleWakeup`, `CronCreate`, `CronDelete`, `CronList` and `Monitor`. The program reads a session's output only after its CLI process has ended, and the CLI does not end while a wake-up is pending: a reading session of a paper run that had set one held the run idle for eleven minutes. The coordinator had this closed at the cause and refused a watchdog that would end a session from outside. Research sessions keep those tools, deliberately: seventeen of 273 sunflower sessions had used them.
- **The first step takes whole elements**, keeps every element of a definition module, everything that carries an attribute or is an `instance`, and everything that is not an element (private declarations, notations, `open` and `variable`), and the definition modules are compared by hash with the instance's first commit after every step that writes Lean. The hand packaging had used a pruning tool that worked on terms and deleted a definition, an `rfl` lemma that `simp` needed, and a local notation.
- **The selection may choose a theorem that the root does not yet rest on.** It then writes the derivation of the root from that theorem as a Lean module, and the program compiles it. The first design had allowed only what the root's existing proof rests on, which would have excluded the user's own example of the better choice. The decision is read once by a second session against the canon.
- **The run takes its settings from the instance's working `settings.json`**, reduced for Lean to what a compile needs, and compiles through a broker of its own, seeded from the instance's cache; there is exactly one Lake build, at the freeze, after a check that every package the build names is present locally at its revision. A Lake build had once fetched Mathlib from the network and compiled 203 of its modules.
- **The form of a history entry is held by the program**: at most 120 words, at most 30 per list item, a heading of number, date and kind, and a line naming the record. The first trial's entries ran to 957 and 236 words and retold the mathematics, against the user's decision on the history.
- **`finalize/` is not under `reference/`** because every file of `reference/` is copied into every research session.
- **PyYAML is required**, and the command refuses at its start without it, because the freeze reads one of the registry's files back.
- **Deliberately not built**: any second check of the frozen library; options for model, effort, output folder or single stages; a README inside the package's `lean/` folder; a check of the classification codes against the registry's list; any submission.

After the build the user said three things that bear on this run (25 November). "do the pipeline yourself" (00:53:30Z) answered three items the coordinator had been holding for the user: a fresh trial, the two comparator commands, and whether the form of the history was acceptable. "apply pipeline now and commit push" (02:22:56Z) had the pipeline installed before the staircase paper run (section 6.2) had ended; the installation is commit `162b9e5e`. And "yeah do that" (02:44:38Z) approved the coordinator's plan to give three observations from a real registry submission to the builder once the trial has ended, and to commit a second time. That change is commit `e08aac4d` (section 6.3).

A further decision was made while this run was in progress. The second paper, the one written by the paper run of section 6.2, had ten findings open when its statements had been compared, and two records that said more than their Lean statements. The user asked whether the new pipeline, used from the start, would have avoided or resolved these, and the session answered case by case that three kinds would have come back. The user then said (25 November, recorded in memory note `M-0783`, which gives no time for the message): "yeah do that and update pipeline, and ideally library should already be stating that stronger thing". Three changes of commit `a8df6b22` follow from it, and their direction is the user's decision. A record whose statement in words says something that its Lean statement does not is corrected in the step whose comparison finds it: the statement is brought to the Lean statement, and a remark that is true goes to the record's explanation. An entry of the bibliography holds only what a reference list prints, and a remark about editions or about the numbering of a source's theorems belongs in the record's `## Literature`. And where a source states a stronger or a more general form of a result than the library has, the library is given the chance to state and prove that form before the freeze: if it succeeds the paper cites the source, and if it does not the library keeps its form, the paper proves the result, and `decisions.md` says which source states what and why the library was not raised. How the third is carried out, by a step `2.6-sources` before the fold-back and a report `stronger` from the session that searches for sources, is the builder's arrangement. The other direction stays closed: the paper does not cite a source for a statement stronger than the library proves.

### 4.12 Choices that are the assistant's, not the user's

The following stand in the tree and in its documents, were made by AI sessions, and were not ruled on by the user. They may be right; a correction may also revisit one where it has a reason, without that being a change to a user decision. Revisiting one remains bound by everything else in this section, and two entries below say of a part of themselves that it is for the user.

- That an approach entry's `sessions` line always names the producing session and its peer (the session said so itself: "follows my recommendation, not a decision of yours").
- That `init` refuses a literature-server command it cannot find on the machine (added "outside the user's request"; the user was told and went on).
- That commands a session abandons are not stopped by code but only discouraged in the guides (the note says the user did not approve this judgment).
- That whether a declaration's proof is complete is recorded per declaration and not through what it uses, and that about twenty lines remain in `runtime/canon.py` to read an index written before the formal judge was removed (taking those lines out would change which existing instances can be read, and is for the user; section 6.1).
- The wording of how long the CLI waits ("ten minutes in past sessions"; that the longer limit is honoured "has not been observed"), and the decision to keep out of the worker guides the observation that a background shell command kept a review session alive.
- That a guide, and not a field of `input.json`, tells a session how to find the imported library's sources from the settings it is given.
- The 180-second limit for starting the literature server; the particular checks the paper command makes of a manuscript; the way the dependency map draws a refuted root.
- The convention that the design record is not updated.
- In the finalize pipeline, several arrangements that its agreement file (`.skill-workspace/finalize-pipeline/agreement.md`, sections 5 and 6) calls "confirmed with the whole" were chosen by the coordinating assistant forty minutes after the user's approval, in a message the user did not answer. Two of them replaced wording the user had approved. The library, the history and the archive are all read from the instance's latest commit when the command starts, where the approved summary said the archive is taken "as of the commit that settled the root". And the pipeline has no way at all to move a library to another version of Lean, where the approved summary said "No port to a newer Lean unless you configure one". The others: a refuted root is refused at the start; the account name is replaced by `<account>` in the archive while other machine paths in the research's own records stay; the registry files compare the main theorem only when its statement names nothing outside the definition modules; a rule of the registry that the research itself cannot meet is reported and does not stop the package; after a successful run the work folder keeps only its report, its decisions file and its log; the default of eight sessions side by side; that a record the pipeline did not touch counts as compared because the research's own review compared it; and the clause of the default note on human review saying that nobody read the Lean statement against the proposition (the user approved only a default saying that no person reviewed the proof). For the two arrangements that replaced approved wording the rule stated before section 4.1, for a guide and a program that agree with each other and depart from an approved text, applies with the finalize agreement in the place of the agreement of twenty-five decisions: the program and its guide agree with each other and depart from a text the user approved, and no later decision of the user was found. A correction neither turns them back to the approved wording nor treats them as decided; a defect whose repair depends on one of them is put to the user.
- In the pipeline's third change (commit `6d353943`, section 6.3), five things that its memory note `M-0780` lists as decisions of the building agent, which the coordinating session accepted and was to report to the user; no answer of the user is on record. A finding of the package's reader that lies outside the README's two prose sections does not stop the run: it is written into `decisions.md` and `report.md`, and the run goes on. A numbered statement of the paper that still differs from its declaration after it was restated does not stop the run and is recorded in `decisions.md`, the report, the README and the table of correspondence. A line of the paper that is still wider than the text block does not stop the run and is recorded. The README prints the sentence of the selection and the statement of the root's record, and not `root.md`. And the README gives no address from which to install Lean. The first of the five replaced wording of the finalize agreement, whose table of stages gives the check of the documents stage as "One reader who has only the package has read them, and what the reader found is corrected" (`.skill-workspace/finalize-pipeline/agreement.md:41`), and the note says so itself. It is treated like the two arrangements of the entry above: a correction neither turns it back to the approved wording nor treats it as decided, and a defect whose repair depends on it is put to the user.
- That the form of a history entry, as the builder made it after the first trial, is acceptable. The coordinator judged this itself after the user's "do the pipeline yourself".

### 4.13 Matters the user has been asked and has not answered

A correction cannot decide these on the user's behalf. Where a repairer finds a defect in the files that can only be repaired by deciding one of them, the repairer writes a proposal for the user that states the defect and the choices. Three entries below are exceptions and say so: the first group, for which the request for the third run gives a direction; one matter that the user has since answered; and one group that belonged to another session, which has since carried it out.

- The remaining items of the over-engineering audit of 24 November, beyond the eight the user answered: files that are written and that nothing reads (the state file `knowledge/state.json`, which is committed at each publication, one-sentence inbox notices, a table of hashes). For the first of these the user has since answered. Round 1 of this run put three options to the user (leave it, write it without committing it, stop writing it), and the user said "leave it as is" (26 November, 15:46:47Z). The file stays written and committed, and the matter is not raised again; leases on questions, whose waiting mechanism the audit says was never used; options with no or one use in 636 sessions (the audit's notes name them); whether three sub-reviewers each need to judge every element; the broker as a build system of its own beside Lake; and the tests' reliance on a hand-written imitation of Lean. The audit's notes are in `.skill-workspace/do-math-review/` (not tracked). The request for the third run asks that over-engineering be simplified "without losing actual needed value or causing issues" (section 3). That gives a direction for the items of this entry that concern something unused or more general than its use: a repairer may correct them where the change does not touch what a running instance depends on (section 6.1), and writes a proposal for the user where it does. For a file that the program writes into an instance that condition usually fails, because the instance's older runner or broker may read it (section 6.1), so removing such a file is a proposal for the user. The questions of this entry that ask whether a whole mechanism should exist (three sub-reviewers who each judge every element, the broker as a build system beside Lake, the hand-written imitation of Lean in the tests) are not answered by that request and stay with the user. Section 15 says what has become of each item.
- Whether `max_end_resumptions` should change.
- Whether the repository's `README.md` should again say that an element's being formal holds only if compilation was done honestly, a sentence lost when the formal judge was removed.
- Whether a "revise" verdict on a proposed question should have an owner. The guide says a later peer proposes it again; in two runs none of seven such proposals was made again.
- For the paper command: whether a repair session may change the title; what to do with findings about citations when the canon has no bibliography; the composition of the collection of model papers; and the user's confirmation of `reference/paper-method.md` itself, which a session asked for three times on 22 November and never received. Of the paper items of the over-engineering audit the user answered only the first (keep the eight readings); the audit's doubts about the machinery that undoes a narrow repair, about `--open-findings`, about `--typeset-only` and about the check that a proof follows its statement directly are unanswered.
- How a process outside the runner should read `settings.json`. The morning's round found that `status`, the commands a session calls and the check of a finished session's output read that file raw, so that while it does not parse they fail, finished work is discarded and retried, and the runner ends at its next publication, although `reference/operations.md` promises that a rejected edit leaves the last valid settings in force. Four repairers raised it, and it was put to the user with five options and a recommendation (`임시/do-math_검증_20261125/종료보고.md`, section 3). The user answered on 25 November, when the second run reported it again as the first of three matters: "1. nah" (`임시/do-math_검증_20261125-2/종료보고.md`, 덧붙임 2). The driving session read that as: none of the five options is to be carried out, and the code and the guide stay as they are. A repairer does not raise the matter again, as a correction or as a proposal.
- For the finalize pipeline: whether its first complete run is to be made on an existing instance or on the next research that finishes. What the session that built the pipeline had in hand during the second run came into the tree with its commits `6d353943` and `a8df6b22` (section 6.3 says what it is preparing now). The user's decision that a sentence of the paper which stays uncovered by any declaration is cut, and that the run goes on to its end without a person, binds like the decisions of section 4.11; the five further decisions that the building agent made in commit `6d353943` are in section 4.12.

## 5. How the tree came to be what it is

The files this run covers were written, for the most part, in four days. Knowing how they were written tells a repairer where the tree is likely to be uneven, and which of its oddities are deliberate.

### 5.1 The rebuild of 22 November

Until 21 November the skill was in its third generation. In the days before, the user had been watching a third-generation instance work on a conjecture about parking functions (the records call it 주차함수, or "parking"), and had corrected its sessions through a series of standing inputs: a proof that can still be reduced is not yet accepted; judge from the text and not from counts; do not close a statement by large computation done without understanding; and others. In the night of 21 to 22 November one session wrote the substance of those conditions into the guides, made English the language of everything inside an instance, and attached the literature server by default (commit `a36c55e6`). That text was written in the vocabulary of the third generation, with its nodes and solutions and relations.

The same evening, in another session, the user and an assistant designed the fourth generation in a long interview. The assistant's understanding of the existing design was wrong at several points, and the user stopped the interview to say so ("네가 지금 생산 세션과 peer 사이의 관계를 잘못 이해하고 있는 것 같아", you seem to misunderstand the relation between a producing session and its peer). The assistant had not read the earlier design record and had filled the gap with a generic picture of a software pipeline in which review is a gate that work must pass. It re-read the record and corrected the design: a peer is a second researcher who continues the same mathematics and writes the record; review judges and does not gate; useful incomplete work is published with review's comments. The agreement of twenty-five decisions came out of the corrected interview.

The user approved the agreement and went to bed with the instruction to continue to the end without asking for approval. Overnight the assistant wrote the design record and then the fourth generation itself, using six sub-agents at once for the common guide, the role guides, two groups of tests, the dependency map and the paper command, while writing the core of the program itself. A small real instance (that the sum of the first n odd numbers is n squared) ran from start to a formally settled root. The result was committed as `ceb38646` at 07:33 KST on 22 November.

Two things follow from this for a repairer. The guides and the program were written by different agents at the same time, and four independent readings of the drafts, made before the commit, found mostly disagreements between what a guide promised and what the program did. And the text carried over from `a36c55e6` was moved into a design that no longer had the objects it spoke of; it was adapted, but nobody has since read the adapted text against the new model of standings as a whole.

A first verification run followed at once, with the same procedure as the present one. It ran two rounds (29 proposals of which 25 were applied, then 21 of which 19) and was stopped by the user's instruction to finish after the round in progress. Its final report says: "이 런은 적용 0건인 라운드에 이르지 못한 채 끝났다. 라운드 2도 동작을 바꾸는 결함을 찾았으므로 남은 결함이 있을 것으로 본다." (This run ended without reaching a round in which nothing was applied. Round 2 still found defects that change behaviour, so defects are expected to remain.) No independent reading of the fourth-generation guides as a finished whole was ever made; one session noted that it "was deferred until the code stopped changing".

### 5.2 The three days after

From 22 to 25 November the skill received thirty-five further commits. The last three were made on 25 November, within hours of this run: the corrections of the verification round described in section 10, the installation of the finalize pipeline, and a second change to that pipeline. The commits fall into a few strands, which ran at the same time in different sessions.

**The paper command** was changed most. It was first made to typeset to PDF. The user then asked that sessions writing a paper actually consult real published papers as models of style, and that repeated independent readings verify the likeness; the collection of thirty-two papers under `exemplars/` dates from this. The user next decided that the paper should not mention Lean at all, which undid the design of the previous night. On 23 November, after a run on the parking proof had spent 105 minutes on a plan and four of fifteen sections, the user had the command rethought from the beginning for efficiency at equal quality, and it gained planning from a digest, a brief per section, parallel writing and resumption. On 24 November it gained a short title for running heads, a check for displayed formulas, a narrow repair after the last reading, a way to take up the open findings of a finished run, and the rule that a proof begins with its first step and carries no plan inside it. `runtime/paper.py` went from 1,243 lines at the rebuild to 3,590 by 24 November, and its test module from 693 to 2,599. The finalize pipeline then added to it once more (below).

**What makes a statement formal** was changed five times. On 22 November the index that the program computes from the compiled library was found to fold away axioms that are not elements (a private axiom; the axioms that `native_decide` generates), giving wrong verdicts; that was repaired. The same day a second, independent check was added: at each publication and at `init` the compiled declarations were exported and judged by an external comparator, and a finished root was compared with the first commit (`runtime/judge.py`). On 23 November the formal judge was narrowed to theorems that no other theorem uses, after an `init` on a large canon had spent 46 of its 101 minutes in it. On 24 November the formal judge stopped the runner of the sunflower instance: on Windows the comparator looks for the programs `which`, `git` and `env` before doing anything, and the broker had been started from an environment that lacked them. A repair made the formal judge find those programs itself. The same evening the user decided to remove the formal judge (section 4.4); the removal was committed three hours after the repair, and the program now reads the axiom list from Lean (`Lean.collectAxioms`).

**The cost of the runner and the broker themselves** came into view when the first instance to import Mathlib, on the sunflower conjecture (해바라기, "sunflower"), began to run on 22 November. A waiting command was found to re-read the broker's whole state file four times a second; the broker was found to spend about half its time rewriting that file. Waiting was changed to look only at a job's result file, the state file was written without indentation and later at most every ten seconds, the runner re-read session files only when they changed, and the memory a new set of imports needs was estimated from the size of the compiled files instead of being measured by an extra compile. Later the broker was changed to place cached modules into a build's library folder by hard link instead of by copy, after finished builds had filled ten gigabytes of disk.

**How sessions behave in a real run** produced several repairs. Reviews were ending without their assessment because the review session ended its turn while its three sub-reviewers were still working, and the CLI stopped them ten minutes later; review and organizer sessions are now resumed in the same conversation when their output does not pass the check on its form, the CLI is asked to wait two hours, and the guides describe what happens between the end of a turn and the end of the CLI. A peer's decision to end was found to change the hash of its already prepared candidate. Sub-reviewers were found searching the whole machine for the Mathlib sources, leaving orphaned processes; the guides now say where the imported library lies.

**The guides** were rearranged once as a whole on 22 November: two new documents (`reference/sections.md`, `reference/records.md`) took material out of others, each role's pack was recomposed by what the role actually uses, and the order of a pack was changed so that the root and the assignment come before the guides. Other commits changed the criterion for resolving a question, the guidance on selective imports, and the definition of a question.

**The dependency map** gained the drawing of a refuted root, an interactive page that was tested in a browser by an agent playing several kinds of user, lighter lines out of definitions, and lost its optional layout engine.

**The finalize pipeline** was designed on 24 November in an interview with the user (section 4.11), built overnight by one agent in a copy of the skill outside the repository, tried four times on the small odd-squares instance, and installed on 25 November at 11:42 KST while its fourth trial was still in its paper stage. It brought `runtime/finalize.py` (1,854 lines), `runtime/package.py` (1,470), sixteen files of instructions for its sessions under `finalize/` (one common text and fifteen for the kinds of session), a test module of 1,460 lines, and 1,852 added lines in `runtime/paper.py`: a draft of the paper's statements made before the library is frozen, the stages `resolve` and `coverage`, a main theorem given by the caller, a title page, the launching of sessions separated into a class of its own, and the withholding of the tools by which a session schedules itself. With it `runtime/paper.py` stands at 5,236 lines and its test module at 3,754. Of the pipeline's stages, those after the paper have never run with real sessions (section 8). A second commit, at 12:52 KST, carried into the pipeline what a real submission to the registry had taught: its first stage now writes what the registry's editorial review asks for, its report says that a submission can fail on the version of Lean alone, and every text it writes into a package ends its lines in the same way. A third commit, at 14:04 KST and during this run's first round, made the paper stage end without a person and stopped the documents stage from halting on what it cannot correct (section 6.3).
