**What the run shows about cost.** The suite took half as long again as in the first run. The load on the machine was comparable: the modules that rounds 5 to 8 hardly touched ran as fast or faster. Four modules account for the increase: `test_publication` (four times as long for ten more tests), `test_finalize` and `test_research` (each about two and a half times as long for three more tests), and `test_paper` (seven tenths longer for six more tests). The time of `test_questions` in the first run had been measured under load.

The slowest tests of this run, where the run recorded them. In `test_publication`: `test_a_question_text_that_a_merge_moved_is_not_moved_again` 68 s (added in round 8), `test_the_proof_that_alone_settles_a_statement_is_replaced_only_by_one_under_which_it_stays_settled` 47 s, `test_ended_after_the_event_was_written_and_before_the_canon_was_put_in_place` 37 s, `test_a_candidate_whose_declaration_a_rearrangement_moved_passes_the_review_s_own_compile` 35 s (added in round 8), and four more above 30 s. In `test_research`: `test_a_canon_refused_after_the_directory_was_created_leaves_no_instance` 60 s, `test_a_negation_settled_through_another_declaration_refutes_the_root` 36 s, `test_a_complete_lean_proof_that_names_an_open_declaration_is_formalized_once_what_it_names_is_formal` 33 s, `test_a_formalizer_is_sent_for_each_statement_ready_for_its_formal_proof` 39 s. In `test_questions`: `test_an_informal_or_contested_root_leaves_the_root_question_open` 50 s. In `test_paper`: `test_direct_formal_refutation_is_the_main_result` 29 s, and some twenty tests between 11 and 20 s. The run of `test_finalize` recorded no times for single tests.

What makes each of them slow has not been established. Two changes are candidates and neither has been measured. Round 8 made the tests' publication helper assert the runner's check of a review's output and make the review's compile where a verdict confirms a proof that is complete in Lean (section 16.11); the scenarios that many modules start from are built through that helper. And rounds 5 to 8 added tests that make real builds. The user asked for tests that are simple and worth their cost (section 3). The increase is therefore a defect, and the repairers of these tests look for where a test now pays for a build or a session that what it asserts does not need, keeping every assertion and every real path.

### Changes of round 9 that no run has executed

Round 9 was applied after the run of the tests described above, and nothing it changed in the program or in the tests has been executed. Three of its repairers followed the growth in time that the run had shown. By their reading, the cause in `test_publication` is this: since round 8 the tests' publication helper makes a review's compile wherever a verdict confirms a proof that is complete in Lean, and the candidate that most tests of that module publish carries one such proof. Round 9 gave the verdict `undetermined` in the tests that assert nothing about that compile, had three groups of tests prepare the candidate they share once, and stopped nineteen tests of `test_paper` from copying an instance that they do not change. No time was measured. One correction made the helper that writes a comprehensive review go through the runner's check of a session's output. The judges compared a sample of the forty-one places that call it and not all of them, so those tests are the first to look at when the tests next run.

### Changes of round 10 that no run has executed

Round 10 was applied without a run of the tests, as round 9 was, so nothing that either round changed in the program or in the tests has been executed. Of round 10's changes these would show first in a run. A candidate's receipt records the written proofs it was built on, and the runner compares them before it applies the candidate (`runtime/canon.py`, `runtime/canon_tool.py`, `runtime/runner.py`, `tests/test_publication.py`). A publication flushes the files it places before it records that it is installed (`runtime/canon.py`, `runtime/runner.py`). The broker keeps a finished build in its state for ten minutes and no longer by count, and the test of that pruning sets back the time at which a build finished instead of waiting (`runtime/lean_broker.py`, `tests/test_lean_broker.py`). The runner saves its state through the compact writer and renders the views of the research only when the research changed (`runtime/runner.py`, `runtime/knowledge.py`). `updates` no longer carries the statements of questions (`runtime/coordination.py`). And many tests of `test_paper`, `test_finalize` and `test_context` no longer copy an instance that they only read.

After the round the driving session made two changes at the user's word (section 22). The compaction window is 950,000 tokens in `runtime/launch.py`, in the sentence of `reference/operations.md` that states it, and in the two assertions of `tests/test_context.py`. And `runtime/message_hook.py` exists with one line; no program refers to it yet (section 16.17).

### Changes of round 11 that no run has executed

Round 11 was applied without a run of the tests, as rounds 9 and 10 were. Of round 11's changes these would show first in a run. The restore step of the finalize pipeline refuses an output that puts back a theorem without a proof and checks that a returned file is the earlier file with lines removed (`runtime/package.py`, `runtime/finalize.py`, `tests/test_finalize.py`). The closing pass of the paper command compares once the body of a section that a repair changed, and the report of a pipeline run words its line on the running text by what that comparison recorded (`runtime/paper.py`, `runtime/package.py`, `runtime/finalize.py`, `tests/test_paper.py`, `tests/test_finalize.py`). The final check of a package no longer reads a formula in square brackets followed by parentheses as a link (`runtime/package.py`, `tests/test_finalize.py`). The Git commit of a publication is flushed to disk before the publication is recorded as finished (`runtime/runtime_support.py`, `tests/test_publication.py`), and the runner checks an operator's question request again when its turn in the queue comes (`runtime/runner.py`, `tests/test_questions.py`). `prepare` removes the record of an earlier build before it builds again (`runtime/canon_tool.py`, `tests/test_canon.py`). The check of a producing session's output no longer builds the model of the research (`runtime/check.py`). The task of a review no longer shows the hashes of the candidate's files in the pack (`runtime/context.py`, `tests/test_context.py`). The finalize command loads the broker's module when it starts (`runtime/finalize.py`). And the tests wait for the stand-in compiler at a shorter interval than the programs use in operation (`tests/support.py`), which changes how long they take and nothing they assert.

### Changes of round 12 that no run has executed

Round 12 was applied without a run of the tests, as rounds 9 to 11 were. Of round 12's changes these would show first in a run. A worker's settings hold a hook for a session that receives messages, the host follows the stream of a running turn and sends one message at 800,000 tokens, and the runner has a command `message` (`runtime/message_hook.py`, `runtime/launch.py`, `runtime/ending.py`, `runtime/runner.py`). The runner writes a record of what a session used when it collects the session, and `status` gives the total (`runtime/retention.py`, `runtime/runner.py`). A peer whose resumed turn failed has its output checked before it is replaced, and a review job that closes drops the hashes of its candidate (`runtime/runner.py`). A finding may carry `accepted` (`runtime/check.py`, `runtime/knowledge.py`). The record of a finished build holds fewer keys (`runtime/lean_broker.py`). A read of a file that another program holds is tried again on the error Python really raises, and the runner removes temporary files that an interrupted write left in the canon before it commits (`runtime/runtime_support.py`, `runtime/runner.py`). The time limit of the TeX command ends the programs that command started (`runtime/paper.py`). A finalize run refuses at its start when no TeX command is found or a definition module was changed (`runtime/finalize.py`). The tests of these changes, and seven tests of behaviour no test had guarded, stand in `tests/test_context.py`, `test_ending.py`, `test_research.py`, `test_publication.py`, `test_retention.py`, `test_resilience.py`, `test_lean_broker.py`, `test_paper.py`, `test_finalize.py`, `test_questions.py`, `test_availability.py`, `test_canon.py` and `test_views.py`. Two helpers of the tests were unified in `tests/support.py`: the session record it makes carries the fields the runner writes, and one helper runs the command line of the canon tool for three modules.

### Changes of round 13 that no run has executed

Round 13 was applied without a run of the tests; the run after round 14, described below, executed its changes. Of round 13's changes these were the ones expected to show first in a run. The host's message is a request with an ID, a session can answer it with the basis `handed_over`, a turn that ended at the limit of the window is authorized on `context_limit`, and the runner continues the job in a new session of the role whose task names the earlier folders (`runtime/ending.py`, `runtime/retention.py`, `runtime/runner.py`, `runtime/context.py`). A Claude worker that receives messages is launched with automatic compaction off (`runtime/launch.py`). Three tests start a session host with a launch record of five keys (`tests/test_containment.py`); the correction was read as needing no further key at the end of a first turn, and a run shows whether that holds. A change of a proof marks the concerns that stood on it (`runtime/knowledge.py`, `runtime/canon_tool.py`, `runtime/runner.py`). The archiver writes a second archive for a finished session's raw conversation (`runtime/runtime_support.py`, `runtime/runner.py`). The pack carries the settings, and the first prompt names no file to open (`runtime/context.py`). The runner removes a lock file that an ended Git command left and adds ignored files to a publication's commit, and `remove_tree` waits for a file another process holds (`runtime/runner.py`, `runtime/runtime_support.py`). In the paper command a comparison that was not made is told apart from one that found nothing (`runtime/paper.py`). In the finalize pipeline the take keeps no theorem without a proof for a definition that an attribute keeps, the restore session is given the declarations with an open proof of their own to delete, and the report counts separately the removed sentences that spoke of cited works (`runtime/package.py`, `runtime/finalize.py`). The tests' session record is made by one helper of `tests/support.py` in nine places of five modules, and the test class of the dependency map's published instance stands in `tests/test_views.py`. Section 16.28 was not in the files yet; round 14 brought it in.

### The run after round 14

The tests ran once after round 14 was applied (27 November, about 78 minutes, one module or a few classes at a time, while the research on this machine was compiling). It was the first run since round 8 and executed everything that rounds 9 to 14 changed, so the subsections above headed "Changes of round … that no run has executed" describe changes that have since been run. All 23 modules ran: 1,112 tests, of which 1,107 passed, 3 were skipped for the platform, and 2 ended in an error. No assertion failed.

The two errors. Both are in `tests/test_formal.py`, in `LeanItselfTests`, the class that compiles with Lean itself: `test_a_changed_definition_under_the_root_is_found` and `test_lean_decides_a_claim_of_another_proof_or_a_refutation_and_keeps_what_an_open_proof_names`. Neither failed in its body. Each raised `PermissionError` (`WinError 32`, the file is in use by another process) while the test's temporary folder was being removed, on the file `runner/lean/jobs/<job>/host.log` of the test's instance. The cleanup registered after the folder's own (`stop_broker` in `tests/support.py`) ends the process tree of the instance's broker and waits up to ten seconds for the broker; the host of a compile is a child of the broker and belongs to that tree while the broker lives, and the cleanup waited for the end of the broker only. The two tests took 123 and 24 seconds in that run, and a third test of the class took 125. Run alone afterwards, both passed (153 seconds for the two). The driving session reads this as a compile host that was still alive and holding its log when the folder was removed, under the load of the research's own compiles; that reading was not established further. What was established then is that the removal of a test instance's folder can meet a compile host that still holds a file, and that nothing in the cleanup waited for it. Round 15 confirmed the reading on the two folders that run left behind (the removal had stopped at `host.log` of a job whose result the broker had already recorded, so the host had finished its compile and was ending), and changed the fixture: the folder of a test is now removed by `remove_tree`, which waits for a file that another process holds for a moment.

Time. The whole run took 4,041 seconds, against 4,253 after round 8 with 76 fewer tests. By module, with the time of the run after round 8 in brackets: `test_publication` 259 s (1,006), `test_research` 410 s (633), `test_paper` 529 s (707), `test_questions` 211 s (254), `test_finalize` 768 s (725), `test_formal` 543 s (176), `test_lean_broker` 509 s (258), `test_views`, `test_resilience` and `test_pipeline` together 314 s (149), `test_canon`, `test_context`, `test_ending` and `test_depmap` together 310 s (216), and the nine short modules together 188 s (129). The modules that became faster are the ones whose cost round 9 repaired. The modules that became slower are those that wait for a compile, by Lean itself or by the stand-in; the research was compiling on the same machine during the run, and the run does not separate that load from what the tests added since round 8. The slowest tests: `test_the_settings_are_the_working_file_and_the_research_is_the_commit` 191.8 s (`test_finalize.py`); `test_a_theorem_in_a_module_that_did_not_change_is_compiled_again_and_becomes_formal` 125.3 s and `test_a_changed_definition_under_the_root_is_found` 122.8 s (`test_formal.py`, with Lean itself); `test_full_pipeline_formalizes_and_drains_followups` 86.2 s (`test_pipeline.py`); `test_a_concern_stays_with_the_proof_it_was_raised_about_when_that_proof_is_replaced` 49.4 s (`test_views.py`); `test_a_theorem_in_a_module_that_did_not_change_becomes_formal_when_what_it_rests_on_is_proved` 41.6 s (`test_formal.py`); `test_an_output_that_fails_its_exit_check_is_sent_back_with_what_the_check_found` 36.4 s and `test_a_stopped_run_keeps_its_work_folder_and_continues_at_the_step_that_stopped` 36.2 s (`test_finalize.py`); `test_a_change_that_the_reading_of_the_changes_faults_is_undone_and_its_finding_goes_on_with_the_reason` 33.8 s (`test_paper.py`); `test_a_canon_refused_after_the_directory_was_created_leaves_no_instance` 33.6 s (`test_research.py`); `test_the_page_finds_selects_follows_and_restores` 32.0 s (`test_depmap.py`).

What the run showed of the changes that had been judged by reading only: the tests of the message system, of the hand-over and its continuation, of the record of what a session used, of the marks on concerns and findings, of the archive of raw conversations, of the lock on the definition modules, and of the runner's collection of sessions all passed, and the three tests that start a session host with a launch record of five keys passed. The next run is the last one of this run and follows its last round; corrections of the rounds in between are again accepted on reading.

Round 15 was applied without a run of the tests; the run after the last round executes its changes first. These are the ones to look at first in that run. Every test that stands on `InstanceCase` now has its folder made by `tempfile.mkdtemp` and removed by `remove_tree` (`runtime/tests/support.py`), and `InitializeTests` of `test_research.py` likewise. Seven tests are new: a real Git hook that would refuse a commit, and the files of a frozen candidate on the disk before the review is assigned (both in `test_resilience.py`); a file that an ignore rule names, in the first commit of an instance (`InitializeTests` in `test_research.py`, which runs `init` once more); a replacement with the text the file has, in a canon stored with CRLF (`test_canon.py`); the definition modules of a research whose root was moved to another module (`test_finalize.py`); and two of the paper command, for a new run cut before its kind is on record and for a statement that had no verdict before (`test_paper.py`). Three existing tests assert more than before: that the stage of a publication which changes modules is sent to disk before the build is saved (`test_publication.py`), how much the hook hands over at once (`test_context.py`), and the order in which `updates` returns messages (`test_research.py`). The two corrections on Git rest on behaviour that was read in Git's documentation and not run.

Round 16 was applied without a run of the tests as well. Twelve tests are new: three of `copy_canon` and of the commands that use it (`test_canon.py`); a waiting message whose file another process holds (`test_context.py`, on Windows only); a title and a name written in TeX, and a build whose compile did not run to its end (`test_finalize.py`); a sentence that a pass did not choose (`test_paper.py`); the archive of a review that has no pipeline, work that falls due under the stop, and a request made after the instance finished (`test_research.py`); a caller's `core.safecrlf` (`test_resilience.py`, which first asserts that Git refuses without the option); and what `capture` retains being on the disk (`test_retention.py`). One test of `test_publication.py` was renamed, one of `test_questions.py` was folded into a test of `test_views.py`, and several existing tests assert more. A judge of round 16 named three things it could not follow to the end by reading, and they are the first to look at: whether the scenario of the new test under the stop has a published change that no organizer has taken; whether the new test of the waiting for a lease can miss a regression on Linux, where a file's number and time can repeat; and whether another test leaves a publication at the stage `commit` in the runner's state and then calls `prepare`, which `copy_canon` would now refuse.

Round 17 was applied without a run of the tests as well, so rounds 15, 16 and 17 have been judged by reading alone. Its new tests, by module: a real runner launched through `start` and found by its start record, the wait of `start` for a runner that is slow to write that record (with the process and the clock stood in for), a folder whose `init` ended during its Git commands, and a canon folder whose files are read-only (`test_research.py`); a path longer than 260 characters in a real repository, on Windows only and skipped where the system does not allow such paths (`test_resilience.py`); a host that cannot write its message, and a decision written in a turn that then ended at the limit (`test_ending.py`); the result of a session's reading of its pack, and a message handed over in the middle of a turn (`test_retention.py`); a definition module that Git holds with Windows line endings, a BibTeX field in quotation marks, and the dashes of TeX (`test_finalize.py`); the lines that end a run under an encoding that lacks a character, and a pass over a finished manuscript that leaves typesetting open (`test_paper.py`); and three behaviours of the broker (`test_lean_broker.py`). Several existing tests assert more, and two tests of `test_paper.py` share a starting run they used to build twice. The first to look at is the test that launches a real runner: it has not been run, and it holds the order in which the runner, the archiver and the broker it starts are ended before the fixture removes the folder. Two tests rest on behaviour of Git that was read in its documentation and not executed (a repository without a commit, and `core.longpaths`).

Two changes entered between rounds 17 and 18 without a judge's reading and without a run of the tests (sections 16.32 and 16.33). The change to the broker brought three new tests of `test_lean_broker.py` and changed four: a build that compiles nothing places nothing in its lib, and a build that compiles one module places the cached modules that module reads and no other; a module whose cached import cannot be placed fails with the reason; a cached module that is in the lib is not placed again while the build runs. The agent that wrote them ran none of them. The stand-in compiler checks the files of direct imports only, so the tests that run real Lean (`LeanItselfTests` of `test_formal.py`) are the first to show whether the compiler finds in the lib what a module imports through another. These are among the first to look at in the last run. The change to the guides touched the text of the host's message in `runtime/ending.py`; no test asserts the words that changed.

## 9. Matters that earlier work recorded as open

The document of the second run carried here a long list of defects, doubts and unfinished matters that sessions, the first run and its investigators had written down (`임시/do-math_검증_20261125-2/맥락.md`, section 9, about 37 KB). The four rounds of the second run worked through that list, and most of what it names is repaired. It is not repeated. A repairer who wants to see where earlier work stumbled can read it there, knowing that an entry may describe a defect that is no longer in the file. What follows is what is known to remain, as places to look and not as conclusions of this run.

**Two tests that the second run's judges did not agree on.** Both defects were raised, the corrections proposed for them were rejected, and the defects remain in the files.

- `runtime/tests/test_lean_broker.py`, the assertion that the runner does not start the broker again within ten seconds (near line 2090 at `aa4d34e1`). As written it rests on a fixed sleep of half a second and can pass when the runner does start the broker again. It was raised twice. The replacements proposed would have reported a correct implementation as failing on a loaded machine, and one judge of three rejected each for that.
- `runtime/tests/test_paper.py`, assertions tied to the removed `verification` environment that walk one path again with other values (near line 755 at `aa4d34e1`). One judge rejected the removal for the form of the replacement, a single empty line, which the contract for corrections cannot tell from a deletion.

**Not executed.** After the second run was closed, `runtime/lean_broker.py` was changed once more (commit `893392d5`: the function that starts a broker records what it started, so that a pipeline run that stops can end a broker that has not yet written its start file). The module `test_lean_broker` was then stopped at the user's word after 40 of its 85 tests had passed, so 45 of its tests have not run on the present file. Section 8 gives the state of every module.

**Observations that the second run did not treat as defects in a file and passed to the user in its report.** They bear on what the bulk of the guides costs, and section 13 takes them up with measurements.

- A session's pack was 640 to 740 KB, and the reading tool of the Claude client refuses a file above 256 KB. Of 32 sessions examined, 18 had their first reading of the pack refused and read it in pieces. Commit `f98f3dd4` has since reduced the part of the pack that holds the state of the research; the guides embedded in a pack still come to between 217 and 351 KB.
- The second turn of a session's ending, in which it is resumed to judge again whether it should end, took 39 to 49 percent of the time of attempts, explorations and peers. What those turns did was not read.
- The user's condition that sessions should not waste processor and memory on polling stands only in the standing input of the sunflower instance and in no guide, so a new instance does not receive it. Under this run's freeze document, something the user asked for that no guide carries is an omission, which a correction can supply.

**Documents outside the skill** describe it and are stale in places: `README.md` (the count of tests), `docs/do-math/lean-canon-verification.md` (counts, and two mentions of the removed formal judge), `docs/do-math/running.md` (what `kill-session` ends; the number of the pipeline's stages), `docs/do-math/rebuild-design.md` (a broken link). They are not in scope. A defect that lies wholly inside them is not an item of this run.

**The remaining items of the over-engineering audit of 24 November** are listed in section 4.13. The user did not answer them one by one, and has now asked this run to simplify what is over-engineered, so they are places to look for assignment 15 and for the repairers of the areas concerned. Section 15 says what has become of each.

**Decided, and not to be raised.** How a process outside the runner reads `settings.json`: the first run put it to the user with five options, and the user answered that none is to be done (section 4.13).

## 10. What earlier verification runs of this skill learned

The skill has been through eight verification runs of this kind since 5 November, the last of them on the morning of this run's own day. Their freeze documents, ledgers and final reports are under `임시/` in the folders named `do-math_검증_*`, `do-math-question_검증_20261109` and `do-math-lean-canon_검증_20261122`; the morning's run is `임시/do-math_검증_20261125/`. The freeze document of the present run turns into rules those of their lessons that it means to keep; the rest of this section is history, given so that the same mistakes are recognised. The records of the present run itself, that is its ledger and the proposals and verdicts of its earlier rounds, are closed to repairers, mergers and judges by the run's rules. The earlier runs' folders and the investigation reports of section 12 can be read.

**The kinds of defect that kept returning.** In every run the largest class was disagreement between what a guide says and what the program does, and in the fourth generation it was found in the same place by several repairers at once: the conditions under which a formalizer is dispatched were described wrongly in four guides. The same rule written in two places that had begun to differ was found in every run, in guides (a table of folders duplicated between two references) and in tests (the same refusal asserted in two modules, copies of a helper). Code that nothing used and branches kept for an earlier format were found in every run. In the broker and the session hosts, defects of ordering between processes were uncovered one layer per round. Use of a term before its definition, one word in two senses, and unclear referents were found in prose in every round. And more than once a stand-in used by the tests hid a real defect that surfaced only in a real run.

**Why proposals were rejected.** In the run of 22 November the judges rejected three proposals, all for the same reason: the defect was real, and the replacement text introduced a new one. One changed how a set was computed and dropped a case the original had handled; one moved information out of a place where a later session still needed it; one, rewording a sentence, used "section" in two senses in it. Two were raised again in the next round with a narrower text and accepted; the third was repaired after the run. The lesson the reports draw: when changing a rule that counts or collects, list the cases the present text handles; when rewording, check the new sentence against the shared vocabulary. Other rejections: a proposal that merged tests and lost an assertion; a proposal resting on a false premise about what a guide lacked; proposals that narrowed something the user had decided, which went to the user instead; and a new mechanism to protect against an operator's mistake, which was closed as needless complexity.

**What repairers got wrong about their own task.** Repairers escalated to the user matters that the files in scope and the sources of the run already decided; that a sentence stands in a document does not make it a user decision, and that several repairs are possible is not a reason to ask. Repairers left a defect unreported because they took it to belong to another repairer's area; areas divide the duty to search, not the right to report. And repairers who found one defect did not look for the same pattern elsewhere; an earlier freeze asked that whoever finds a defect search its own area and the whole tree for the same wrong assumption, order of checks or turn of phrase, and report each place.

**What judging without running cannot see.** Judges do not execute anything. In one run three judges unanimously accepted a repair whose premise about a helper was false, and only a run afterwards showed it. Proposals that are each valid can conflict once applied together. This is why the driving session runs the tests at intervals and reads the result, and why a proposal that changes behaviour carries, in the same proposal, the change to the test that asserts that behaviour.

**The run of the morning of 25 November.** It answered the same request as the present run, on 76 files (the paper command was left out and the finalize pipeline did not yet exist), and it was closed after one round at the user's word, "wrap it up with round 1"; the user gave no reason. Seven repairers raised 74 proposals. Twenty-two of them overlapped and were combined, and three judges judged the resulting 62. Fifty-six were corrections that all three accepted, and they were applied, in 39 files (commit `150b61ab`); two were proposals for the user that all three accepted (section 4.13); four were rejected (section 9.9). Six corrected the program: a turn that the AI provider ended with a refusal was not recognised as a refusal; a candidate build that had failed to compile was kept, so that running the same command again did not build again; the broker overwrote a result, or ended, when Windows briefly refused the removal of a queue file; and three smaller ones. Fifteen corrected guides, among them the reviewer's rule for a minor flaw that the user had asked for on 22 November and that had never reached the guides. Seventeen corrected tests: temporary folders left behind after every run, paths that no test went down, assertions that did not fix the reason for a refusal, costs. Eighteen removed leftovers or corrected comments that described an earlier state.

Three things about that round bear on the present run. First, one round is not a measure of what remains: every earlier run of this skill kept finding defects in its second and later rounds, and the closing report of the morning lists observations that repairers made and did not raise (section 9.9). Second, the closing test run did not finish. It was stopped by a guard against low memory after ten of the twenty-two modules, so several corrections to the program and to tests are in the tree without having been executed (section 8). Third, one defect was found independently by four repairers, each of whom wrote a proposal for the user about it. Proposals for the user carry no replacement text, so the procedure did not combine them, and the judges received four. Two were rejected because the option they recommended rested on a final state that the option would not in fact produce, and the two that were accepted recommended the same direction by different means. A repairer who writes a proposal for the user therefore has to follow each option to what it actually leaves in the files, and the matter those four raised is now waiting for the user and is not to be raised again (section 4.13).


**The second run of 25 November.** It answered the same request on 98 files, with the paper command and the newly installed finalize pipeline in scope, and it was closed after its fourth round at the user's word, "finish with round 4". Ten repairers read in each round, eight by area and two across. They raised 96, 97, 53 and 48 proposals; three judges accepted 74, 88, 45 and 48 of those put to them; 33, 84, 36 and 46 corrections were applied, 199 in all (commits `16b81ebf`, `87fb5e5b`, `aa4d34e1`, `a5ce92d0`; closing report `임시/do-math_검증_20261125-2/종료보고.md`). Among them were defects of the running program: a frontier that passed over an accepted step naming a refuted statement, so that work dropped out of what remained; a review session left in the state `publishing` when the recovery of a candidate changed just before publication failed; the pipeline's final check reading `f:\mathbb{N}` in a formula as a drive path. After the run the user answered the three matters its report put forward, and commit `893392d5` carried the answers out together with the corrections that had been approved and not applied.

Four things about that run bear on the present one. First, it did not reduce bulk and was not built to. Its four commits added 1,622 lines and removed 639. Its freeze document told repairers to leave a passage alone when it was not clear whether it was padding, and made every removal from a guide carry a proof; no repairer was assigned to bulk. Section 13 shows what its corrections to the guides typically did. Second, 49 corrections that three judges had accepted were not applied, because another session committed to the same files in the middle of two of the rounds; the next rounds had to find those defects again, and some were put in by hand after the run. The sessions now install only between rounds (section 6.3). Third, two repairers twice wrote the same paragraph for neighbouring gaps of one file. Insertions at different gaps do not overlap, so nothing combined them, and both were accepted; the driving session left out the later of each pair. A repairer who inserts text therefore looks at whether the passage beside the gap already says it. Fourth, a run of this kind does not end because nothing is left: the fourth round still found defects that change behaviour.

## 11. The repository's criteria for judging a test

The repository keeps a distillation of what its earlier work learned about tests (memory notes `L-0001` and `L-0023`). It was written by AI sessions from past experience and is not a set of user decisions, but the earlier freezes used it and it is the measure this run applies together with the user's requirements of section 3. The points that bear most often on this suite:

- **A passing test testifies only to what it actually exercised.** A stand-in, a fixture or a patched name lets through every defect outside what it models. The broker passed all its tests with stand-ins and then failed on the real compiler at three boundaries the stand-ins did not model: state left by an earlier version, the file semantics of the platform, and how an executable is looked up.
- **A stand-in belongs only at the boundary with something outside the program or with time.** The program's own receiving, storing and judging run as real code. A stand-in does not answer the unknown with success, does not infer what the real producer would compute, and does not fill in what is missing.
- **A test of a refusal fixes the reason for the refusal**, not only that something was refused. Its input starts from what the real producer makes and changes the one thing the test is about, so that the failure can be attributed.
- **Passing does not show that a check is strong.** A check that goes over all members is tried by removing a member and seeing that it fails and names the member. Expected values are computed independently of the code under test.
- **A test tied to a past state is a defect.** A branch that tolerates a known old failure counts the failure's return as a pass; an assertion on a line number fails when correct code moves.
- **Right after tests are reworked, compare the number collected**, and confirm that the test guarding a particular repair is still collected. A test once ended up after the block that runs the module and never ran.
- **Cost.** A time budget means something only where overruns are reported. When cutting a test's cost, keep its starting state and the real path it goes down, not only its assertions; a method without an assertion of its own may still check through a helper or through the absence of an exception. A test that prepares and restores real stored state is not cheap whatever it is called.
- **A test that inherits the program's defaults inherits what is right for operation.** On a loaded machine, tests that inherited the default below-normal priority starved; the fixture was given normal priority and the default was kept.
- **A test that failed once under load** is examined for a race in the program before it is called unreliable; one such failure was a real race, reproduced by constructing the state.
- **A test made from an incident asserts the property the contract must keep**, not the incident's particulars; the user asked for exactly this when tests were written for an incident fix.
- **Counts.** Results of runs of different versions are not added together into "all pass". Skipped and not-run are kept apart from passed.

## 13. The guides: where the bulk is and what kind it is

This section is a digest of three investigation reports made on the evening of 25 November against commit `893392d5`: `B1_가이드_세션.md` (the guides that research sessions receive), `B2_가이드_운영_명령.md` (the operator's documents, the method document, the instruction files of the finalize pipeline and the prompts in the programs) and `B5_가이드_사용.md` (what real sessions do with the guides), all in `임시/do-math_검증_20261125-3/조사/`. The reports give every figure with the script that produced it, the instances of each class by line, and worked examples. In this section "the guides" are all the documents of the skill, as the freeze document defines 가이드, and the prompts and sentences that the programs hold for a reader are treated with them. What follows are measurements and investigators' readings. A repairer establishes each in the file before relying on it, and a judge checks it there (section 1).

### 13.1 What the bulk costs, measured on real sessions

From `B5_가이드_사용.md`, which read all 331 session streams of the sunflower instance. Every session there ran on the Claude client; no session of any instance has run on the Codex client, so nothing is known about how that client reads a pack.

**Every guide is read in full at launch.** Summed over all sessions, the reading of `pack.md` left no byte of an embedded guide unread. What sessions skip is the state of the research that follows the guides. The reading is clumsy: the launch prompt says to read the pack in full (`runtime/context.py:141`), and the first attempt was refused in 277 of 331 sessions, 452 times for the client's limit of 25,000 tokens and 209 times for its limit of 256 thousand bytes; sessions then guess sizes over twelve to fifteen calls.

**What the bytes cost.** Guide text measures 2.98 bytes to a token, so the guides of one pack come to about 100,000 to 120,000 tokens. They are 16.5 percent of all input tokens of the sessions' main threads, 30 percent for reviews and organizers and 11 to 15 percent for the producing roles and peers. Each tenth of the guides' bytes removed saves about 1.6 percent of input tokens and about 10,000 tokens of every session's starting context. Most of those tokens are read from cache, so the figure is one of context and not of money. The user's two texts carry 14.8 percent of the guides' tokens and are not changed. Whether a shorter guide is understood better cannot be seen in the streams.

**After a compaction the guides are gone.** A session's context was compacted automatically 160 times, in 157 of the 207 attempt, explore and peer sessions, and 105 of those times in the turn in which a session that ended is resumed to judge again. Reviews, organizers and formalizers were never compacted. After a compaction a session reads back about 6 thousand of the 300 to 360 thousand bytes of guides it had held. No guide, resume text or prompt mentions compaction. Late in a producing session's life a rule therefore reaches it only through the digest the compaction left, the resume text, and what it looks up itself.

**What sessions look up later is narrow.** Most returned to: the sections of `reference/retained-work.md` on ending and on the outcome (62 and 56 sessions, nearly all in the resumed turn, where `resume/common.md` gives the file by its path); `reference/common.md` "Entering your own work" (25); `reference/tools.md` "Checking and ending" (24); the finishing sections of `roles/peer.md`; the two sections of `reference/lean.md` on compiling. Rarely or never returned to: `reference/common.md` "The standing of a statement" (9.3 thousand bytes) and "Seeing what remains: the frontier" (7.2), and four of the eight sections of `reference/lean.md` (about 26 thousand). The largest section of `reference/common.md`, "How you should do the work" (17.7 thousand), is returned to by sixteen sessions. Never opened by any session after launch: `reference/organizer-output.md`, `roles/organizer.md`, `reference/paper-method.md`, `SKILL.md`. That a section is not returned to does not show that it is not used: it was read at launch. What the figures show, in the driving session's reading, is which text has to stay findable and complete in itself for a session that has lost the rest; the freeze document makes that a consideration in judging repetition.

**Where the guides failed in use, and where they were repaired.** Three errors that a guide could have prevented were far commoner than earlier reports had counted: `view` called with element and session exchanged (147 calls in 76 sessions, 106 of them by the sub-reviewers of a review), the build record of `result` read as a list (94 calls in 91 sessions), and `--part` given a capitalised name (26 calls in 22 sessions). After the second run's corrections of 25 November 15:22 KST there was one such error, in a sample of 24 sessions. Two habits persist. Sub-reviewers write scratch files into the shared temporary folder (174 calls since 24 November, 118 by sub-reviewers); they never receive `reference/tools.md`, and the list at `roles/review.md:39` of what a brief to them must carry does not say where scratch files go. And sessions sleep in the shell for long stretches (29 times since 24 November 15:32, mostly reviews waiting for sub-reviewers); `roles/review.md:41` says to wait within the turn and not how, and the user's condition against polling stands only in this instance's standing input (section 9). The line `roles/review.md:39` is itself an example of accretion: one line of 2,951 characters, against 2,435 at commit `162b9e5e`, to which each failure of a sub-reviewer so far has added a clause.

### 13.2 The guides that research sessions receive

From `B1_가이드_세션.md`, whose investigator read all 28 files in full: the fourteen documents of `reference/` that a worker session can receive, the six guides of `roles/` and the eight texts of `resume/`.

**Size.** The 28 files hold 522 thousand bytes. The largest: `reference/common.md` 90,342; `roles/review.md` 51,386; `reference/lean.md` 51,328; `reference/lean-broker.md` 34,090; `roles/peer.md` 33,063; `reference/change-format.md` 29,595; `roles/explore.md` 26,373; `roles/attempt.md` 25,416; `reference/canon.md` 21,691; `reference/records.md` 20,422. Five guides go to all six roles (`common.md`, `writing.md`, `canon.md`, `lean.md`, `tools.md`: 189 thousand bytes), and they are between 51 and 86 percent of a pack.

| Role | Guides in the pack | Without the user's two texts | Its role guide | Its resume text |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| attempt | 300,098 | 251,950 | 25,416 | 3,485 |
| explore | 291,910 | 243,762 | 26,373 | 3,715 |
| formalizer | 220,217 | 220,217 | 15,094 | 2,983 |
| peer | 357,762 | 309,614 | 33,063 | 3,092 |
| review | 369,992 | 321,844 | 51,386 | 1,612 |
| organizer | 311,316 | 263,168 | 13,241 | 1,409 |

**This bulk is not the day's.** These guides held 296 thousand bytes before the rebuild of 22 November and 491 thousand after it, in one commit. They have grown 31 thousand since, of which 25 thousand came with commit `f98f3dd4` on the evening of 25 November (the account of `q0`, section 5.7) and 7 thousand with the six commits of the day's verification rounds. By `git blame`, three quarters of the bytes that stand were last written on 22 November, by two commits.

**How the rounds wrote.** The six round commits of 25 November changed these guides in 29 places. Twenty added words to a paragraph that was kept, three inserted a paragraph, one exchanged words and grew, three exchanged words at equal length, and two made a paragraph shorter. Twenty of the 29 added a fact a session can act on that was missing or wrong. Four added an account that changes no action of the reader. Three times one fact was written into two files in the same commit. Six of the additions carry their fact in about twice the bytes it needs, because the fact comes with the mechanism behind it and the incident that showed it (`change-format.md:140`, `review.md:67`, `canon.md:75`, `review.md:39`, `formalizer.md:29`, `change-format.md:108`). No change removed text that its own addition had made redundant. The pattern is local: a defect is repaired where it was seen, by adding the missing fact and its reason to the paragraph; the fact is often added also to the second document that describes the same thing; nothing is taken away. That explains the longest paragraphs better than it explains the bulk.

**Repetition.** Word for word, little is repeated: shared runs of eight words cover 36 thousand bytes (7 percent), and letting each cluster stand once would take out 21 thousand. Repetition in other words is larger. The investigator's full reading found 42 topics told again, 84 thousand bytes of further tellings in 262 places, and classified each place:

| Class | What it is | Bytes | How the freeze document treats it |
|---|---|---|---|
| a | a rule or criterion restated in the guide of a role that acts on it | 43,608 | Restructured by the design of section 17. What applies the rule to that role's own decisions stays; what tells the common account again is reduced. |
| b | an explanation, an account of a case or of how something works, or an argument, given again with nothing added | 8,205 | Padding: one place is kept. |
| c | the same rule or description again in the same document, or in a second document of the same pack that is not a role guide | 29,533 | Within one document it is padding. Across documents it is restructured by the design of section 17, the audit's twelve items named below included. |
| d | boilerplate that is nearly identical | 2,561 | Padding. |

The third row needs care, because it is where the user's answer of 24 November lies. The audit's notes list the twelve rules it had counted (`.skill-workspace/do-math-review/references-notes.md:81-98`). Each is one rule in two or more documents of one pack that are not role guides: where the imported library lies; `checkout`; the `build` command; a new element in a new module; what a self-description says; that the written proof is the argument of the Lean proof; an approach entry "it was about to take"; that a citation "does not supply" the argument of application; that a section "cannot be withdrawn"; that earlier sessions searched for the same papers; that a failure of execution "is not Lean rejecting the source"; that the session is resumed to "reconsider ending". About 8.4 thousand bytes of class c lie in those twelve. The user answered that item "intended", and the run at first kept each of these twelve, and the statement of any other rule, in every place where it stood. On 26 November the user said they were not sure the answer had applied to these, and asked that repetition be restructured from an understanding of what is written where and why (section 3). The classes and byte counts of this table are therefore a map of where tellings lie and not a rule for what to do with them. Section 17 gives the design.

One role's pack holds 24 to 42 thousand bytes of further tellings, 11 to 14 percent of its guides. The same reader meets a topic in three or more places for 24 to 28 of the 42 topics. The topics told most often: an attempt meets "stating mathematics in Lean from the start" and "the central difficulty; a verified range that only grows" in eleven places each; a review meets "what makes a statement formal" in eleven; a peer meets the account of `q0` in ten. The report lists the twenty largest topics with every place (its section 3.3). The clearest cases of class b: the example of three ranges of `common.md:164` told again at `review.md:147` and `peer.md:53`; the table-lookup case of `common.md:287` told again at `review.md:139`; `common.md:43` restating the reasons of `what-research-is-for.md:45`.

**Padding inside passages**, labelled by reading, 35 thousand bytes (6.8 percent), which the investigator would not trust to better than a quarter of each figure:

- the inside of a program told to a reader who does not act on it, 15,277 bytes, most of it in `lean.md` (5,018) and `retained-work.md` (2,600);
- restatement inside a passage, 8,390;
- how something came to be and the measurements of one machine, 5,311, most of it in `lean.md` and `lean-broker.md`;
- sentences that announce the order of what follows, 4,584 in twenty places. The opening of a guide usually carries the purpose of the text in its first sentence, which a reader needs, and the list of sections after it, which repeats the headings;
- clauses that say which roles receive a document, 1,686.

Densest: `retained-work.md` 20 percent, `lean.md` 17, `tools.md` 12. Besides these, 248 sentences (47 thousand bytes, 9.6 percent of the prose) point to another place and usually summarise it; they are 22 percent of `attempt.md` and 17 percent of `peer.md`. A pointer is useful. The bulk is in the summary that comes with it, which gives the reader a second, shorter telling of what the same pack holds in full.

**Three worked rewrites** (report, section 4.3) kept every detail at 73, 82 and 69 percent of the original length: `review.md:41`, `review.md:67` and `attempt.md:27`. The first comes to 35 percent when it relies on `tools.md:51`, which review's pack holds; one detail would then have to move into that paragraph. They show what is possible and are not approved text.

**What the investigator expects can be reached.** Classes b and d, restatement, announcements and the receiving clauses come to about 25 thousand bytes; class c up to 30 thousand as far as it is open; tightening the pointing sentences perhaps 10 to 15 thousand; moving the inside of programs to where its reader is, up to 15 thousand (out of a pack, that is a proposal for the user). That is 5 to 16 percent of 522 thousand. This estimate counts passages that can go or move. It does not count what rewording at the rate of the worked examples would save across paragraphs that carry no label. A larger reduction needs a change of structure (section 13.4) or a decision of the user.

### 13.3 The operator's documents and the instructions of the two commands

From `B2_가이드_운영_명령.md`. The material is `SKILL.md` and `reference/operations.md` (below, ops), which the operator reads; `reference/paper-method.md` (the method) and the prompts in `runtime/paper.py`, which the sessions of the paper command read; `finalize/common.md` and the fifteen instruction files beside it, of which each session of the finalize pipeline reads the common text and one instruction; and `exemplars/README.md`.

**Size and where it came from.** These twenty guides hold 430 thousand bytes. They held 202 thousand on the morning of 25 November and 62 thousand before the rebuild.

| File | Bytes | Lines | Longest line | At the rebuild | 25 Nov 00:32 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `reference/operations.md` | 154,933 | 689 | 2,734 | 87,057 | 117,090 |
| `reference/paper-method.md` | 80,014 | 275 | 3,041 | 35,919 | 63,963 |
| `SKILL.md` | 21,152 | 84 | 1,179 | 19,602 | 19,932 |
| `finalize/common.md` | 13,719 | 88 | 719 | (new on 25 Nov) | |
| fifteen instructions of `finalize/` | 158,821 | 1,165 | 1,319 | (new on 25 Nov) | |

In this material the bulk came with features. The installation of the finalize pipeline alone wrote 26.5 thousand bytes into ops and 15.3 thousand into the method besides the new files; the six commits of the day's verification rounds changed 48 paragraphs and added 8.4 thousand net. None of those 48 changes replaced standing text by a shorter wording, and sixteen appended a sentence. Ops is the guide touched by the most commits since 5 November, 65 of 100.

`runtime/paper.py` holds 89 thousand bytes of prompt text and 26 thousand of other text for people, besides a module docstring of 15 thousand that `--help` prints. `runtime/finalize.py` and `runtime/package.py` hold 18 and 11 thousand bytes of sentences written for people (messages, the report of a run, text of a package's README). The prompts a paper session receives are 3 to 10 thousand bytes each.

**Repetition is paraphrase.** Word for word, almost nothing is repeated for one reader: shared runs of eight words cover 0.5 percent of ops within itself, 3.7 percent of `SKILL.md` inside ops, and at most 0.7 percent of any instruction inside `finalize/common.md`. A search for duplicates finds nothing. What repeats is the same fact in other words: the investigator listed 32 passages, 12.1 thousand bytes, as a lower bound from one reading. Three facts are each stated four or more times across `SKILL.md` and ops: that a formally settled root and a finished runner are different states, that an input reaches only sessions launched after it, and that a finalize run commits and submits nothing. The rounds have begun to produce such restatements themselves: ops:623 states one exception twice in one paragraph, added by `a5ce92d0` and again by `893392d5`, and the two statements already differ in two clauses.

**What ops is made of.** By the investigator's paragraph map, which is a judgment, ops is 20 percent procedure (what the operator does, asks or tells the user), 33 percent reference (formats, tables, settings, options, exit codes, command syntax), 41 percent account of how the runner and the commands work inside (64 thousand bytes), and 4 percent incidents and measurements. The account of the inside stands mostly in "Waiting, fallback and recovery" (25 thousand), "The paper" with the map viewer (22 thousand), "What the run does" of the finalize section (8 thousand) and "What makes a statement formal" (5 thousand). The stages of the paper command are told in four places in different words: the method's "What is checked after writing" (13 thousand), ops "The paper" (30 thousand), the module docstring of `paper.py` (15 thousand), and the prompts. The map shows where that material is. It does not show that the material can go: what an operator does with knowledge of the inside (explain a status to the user, tell a stall from a wait) is for the repairer to establish passage by passage.

**The classes the investigator found**, with its estimates (the report gives instances by line):

- K1, K6: the inside of a program told to a reader who acts on its outcome, and the order of a whole run told to a session that performs one step. Ops 64 thousand bytes by the map; the method 20 thousand in the sections "What each session works from" and "What is checked after writing"; in the finalize files, passages such as `foldback.md:7-15` and `records-write.md:21`.
- K2: the same fact restated for the same reader in other words. Ops 6.1 thousand, `SKILL.md` 4.4, the method 1.6.
- K3: a pointer that lists what the place pointed to contains. Seventeen sentences of `SKILL.md` (4.4 thousand, a fifth of the file), nine of ops, fifteen of the method.
- K4: accounts of incidents and measurements. Ops 6.6 thousand, the method about 3.9, the finalize files 4.6. A number or a worked case that lets a reader decide a new case is detail. An account of how the text or the command came to be is not. The report sets ops:293-297 (three times an operator's added sentences misled reviewers, to which later rules refer back) against ops:451 ("The command exists because two packages were made by hand before it").
- K5: announcements and closing summaries. About 1.2 thousand bytes in ops; little elsewhere.
- K7: what accretion leaves: a qualifier appended as a new sentence where the sentence before could have carried it, and twin additions.

The fifteen instruction files are close to bare. Their output contracts hold no padding the investigator could find; `selection.md` is 9 thousand bytes of contract under "What you write". Their bulk is the sections that say what a step is for and which earlier failure it prevents, 27 thousand bytes over the fifteen files, which `finalize/common.md:5` announces as the design: "the instruction explains what your step is for and which earlier failure it is there to prevent so that you can decide such a case as the step intends". Within those sections, the part that tells the session how the program proceeds after it can be told apart from the part that gives the standard by which the session judges.

**Three worked rewrites** (report, section 3) kept every detail at 75, 66 and 78 percent of the original length: ops:623, `SKILL.md:24` with `:26`, and method:208. The report lists each detail and where it survives. The saving came from three moves: stating once what stood twice, leaving to the other document what the reader meets there at the moment of action, and folding into a sentence the sentence that restates it. They show what is possible; they are not approved text.

**Where a byte costs most.** In the one long paper run on record (`.skill-workspace/parking-staircase/paper-out-2`, 240 session folders) 171 sessions read the whole method of 80 thousand bytes, among them all 147 repair sessions, of which 23 only repaired LaTeX or wide lines and 30 repaired single passages. `finalize/common.md` gives 6.6 thousand bytes on compiling and on what a Lean file keeps to ten kinds of session that write no Lean.

### 13.4 Structural diagnoses

Each entry gives the structure, what it costs, and the ways the investigators saw to change it with what each way would need. They are leads for the repairers of the areas concerned and for assignment 13. None has been judged. Under the freeze document a way that can be written as corrections to the present files is raised as corrections; a way that needs a file created, removed or renamed is raised as a proposal (the item the contracts call 상신 제안, which carries no replacement text); and a way that changes which guides a research session's pack carries, or takes text out of a pack, is such a proposal and goes to the user. Where an entry says that a way needs no decision or needs the user, that is the investigator's reading of sections 3 and 4. The freeze document decides, and a judge checks it afresh.

Since these entries were written, several of them have been decided or carried out, and an entry is kept below as the record of what was found. The repetition between `common.md` and the role guides, and among the documents every role receives, is handled by the design of section 17, on the user's instruction of 26 November (section 3). The account of `q0` has a document of its own (section 16.3), the lifecycle knowledge has left `retained-work.md` (section 18), and `evidence.md` is gone (section 16.4); the user decided all three. For the operator's documents the entries S1, S2 and S6 of the second report are superseded by the design of section 19, and for the instructions of the two commands the entries S3 to S5 by the design of section 20; where an entry and a design differ, the design is the later reading.

**`common.md` tells every role each role's work, and each role guide tells it again by reference** (B1, S1). `reference/common.md` gives, to all six roles, what each role does and by which criteria; each role guide then gives its own part again and points back: "the common introduction" occurs 56 times in the guides and a quoted heading 123 times. The reader of one pack meets a criterion in `common.md`, again in summary inside a pointer, and again in full in the role guide: 44 thousand bytes of class a and up to 47 thousand of pointing sentences. For whoever corrects the guides it is three places to keep equal, and it is where the rounds wrote one fact twice. Ways: (i) keep both tellings and let a pointer carry the address only. This touches the six role guides and the references, and the quoted headings; it needs no decision. (ii) Let the role guide be the full telling and `common.md` the short one. That moves criteria out of a document that other roles receive (a peer reads review's criteria for questions in `common.md:227` and is sent there by `peer.md:97`), so it changes what a role receives and needs the user. (iii) Order `common.md` so that a term is defined before it is used. The norms of conduct (`:39-97`) come before the canon (`:99`), and sections, the lease and the frontier are used at `:27`, `:51`, `:168` and `:211` before `:186` and `:251` explain them; one round repaired such a use by adding a definition in place. A reordering keeps every byte in the same file and removes that glue. The present order (what the research is for, then how it works) may be the user's wish that purpose come first; nothing the investigator read says who chose it.

**The documents every role receives repeat `common.md` and one another** (B1, S2). `writing.md:43-59` against `records.md:55-59` and `:108`; `tools.md:5-29` against `canon.md:7-28` and `:73-104`; `retained-work.md:26-42` against `lean.md:108-128`; `lean.md:212-230` against `common.md:170-174`; `canon.md:176` against `lean.md:9`; `organizer-output.md:42` and `:68` against `organizer.md:29` and `change-format.md:132-140`. The five guides that go to all six roles can be rearranged among themselves without changing what any role receives; 16.5 thousand bytes of classes b, c and d lie in those five. Nothing in the program is tied to it: `test_context.py:540-544` asserts the order of the guides and not their content. The limit is the statement of a rule (section 13.2).

**The account of `q0` stands in seven files** (B1, S3). Commit `f98f3dd4` wrote most of it: `common.md:225`, `writing.md:57`, `records.md:110-132` (8.9 thousand bytes), `peer.md:77-93` (9.6), `review.md:87-93` (5.3), `explore.md:80-84`, `change-format.md:116`. A peer receives about 21 thousand bytes on it and a reviewer about 17, and `peer.md:81-91` (7.3 thousand) applies only when the question is `q0`, which one exploration at a time holds. Ways: (i) `records.md:116-132` is already the standard that `peer.md:81` and `review.md:91` cite; the parts of `peer.md:85`, `:89`, `review.md:89`, `records.md:118` and `:124` that say again where things are otherwise kept and that nothing is lost (class b, 2.2 thousand) can stand once. (ii) Give the procedure of `peer.md:83-91` only to the peer that needs it, as a reference added to the pack when the task's question is `q0`. That changes `context.py` and what a role receives and needs the user. What in this wording is the user's and what the session's was not determined; the commit's message records the room of 40,000 characters and the instruction to rewrite and not to append.

**Paragraphs built by accretion** (B1, S4). 87 paragraphs exceed 1,000 characters, twenty exceed 1,500 and four exceed 2,000: `review.md:39` (2,951), `tools.md:51` (2,689), `lean-broker.md:35` (2,213), `review.md:89` (2,167). The longest were changed by seven and eight commits each since 22 November. `review.md:39` carries in one paragraph what the briefing holds, the three things every delegation carries, the order of arguments of the canon command with its incident, what a sub-reviewer returns, that it writes no file, independence, model and effort, the three emphases, and what a session on the other client does instead. A session that looks for one of these has to read the paragraph, and a reading tool that cuts a line at 2,000 characters returns it without its end. Dividing such a paragraph by subject saves no bytes and loses none, and has no tie to the program.

**`lean.md` is five documents in one** (B1, S5): why the broker exists, with one machine's measurements (`:11-47`, 10 thousand bytes); commands and results (`:55-161`, 22 thousand); estimating (6 thousand); craft (7.8 thousand); formal acceptance (6.8 thousand). Who receives the file is the user's decision. Inside it, the meaning of statuses and exit codes stands in four places (`:75-77`, `:136`, `:147-149`, `:230`); `:45` repeats `:29`; `:153-159` gives the cache's inside with observations of Lean's versions that `lean-broker.md:89-91` holds for the operator; `:11-29` argues for the broker from measurements of one machine.

**Half of `retained-work.md` is the session's lifecycle as the program sees it** (B1, S6). `:74-80` and `:110-112` (2.6 thousand bytes) describe what the host and the runner record and when the runner launches attempts again. What the session does is at `:61-72` (write the decision with four fields) and `:98-108` (copy the token). The rest informs an operator. Moving it to ops needs a check that the facts are there, and takes text out of four roles' packs, which the freeze document sends to the user as a proposal. Sessions return to this file more than to any other after a compaction (section 13.1), so what a session needs there has to stay complete in itself.

**The pack describes itself three times** (B1, S7): in the preamble that `context.py` writes into every pack (the strings at about `:104-140`), in `common.md:299`, and in `common.md:225`, `:233`, `:235`, `:247`, `tools.md:11`, `:33` and `canon.md:26`. If the preamble changes, `common.md:299` has to follow.

**`evidence.md`** (B1, S8) is 7 thousand bytes that no pack holds, with one link to it (`common.md:249`); `:15-27` restates the standings of `common.md:149-166`. The audit recorded that two of 267 sessions opened it and proposed to delete it or to move `:9-13` into `common.md`; no answer of the user to that finding was found. Removing a file is raised as a proposal (상신 제안).

**Ops serves five occasions as one file with no way in** (B2, S1). An operator who fixes a root needs lines 7 to 288 (37 thousand bytes); one who answers the user about a running instance needs "Read progress and results", "Commands" and parts of "Waiting, fallback and recovery" (about 49 thousand); one who runs the paper command needs "The paper" (30 thousand); one who finalizes needs lines 447 to 556 (21 thousand). The file's order is the order in which features were added. "Commands", the table consulted most, is the ninth of ten sections; "The paper" is 30 thousand bytes under one heading; five lines exceed 2,000 characters (ops:402, :418, :426, :440, :675). The operator opens 155 thousand bytes on every occasion. Ways: (a) keep one file, put a list of occasions with the sections each needs at its head, move "Commands" and the tables of status and exit codes forward, give "The paper" sub-headings, and break the table cell of ops:440 into rows. This touches ops, the sixteen section names that `SKILL.md` quotes, and comments in `settings.py:25`, `runtime_support.py:308`, `test_ending.py:507`, `test_research.py:2251` and `test_resilience.py:270` that name sections of ops. (b) Divide ops into several files of `reference/`, one for each occasion. `context.py:62-63` copies `reference/*.md` by pattern and `test_context.py:566-567` compares against the same pattern, so no program or test changes; six links in `reference/lean-broker.md` (lines 5, 57, 59, 69, 71, 91) move with it. It adds files to every research session's `context/reference/` without changing any pack. (c) Put the operator's files outside `reference/`, as `finalize/` is. That removes them from what a research session can open and needs the user.

**The account of how the commands work inside has no home of its own** (B2, S2). It accumulates in the procedure document, and for the paper command it stands in four places, each in its own words, so that nothing shows when they diverge: commit `a5ce92d0` had to add one fact to ops:430 and to method:259 separately, and ops:440 tells again inside a table cell what ops:402 tells. Ways: (a) in ops, keep for each command what the operator runs, sees and reports (options, files, exit codes, what to tell the user) and point to one account of the stages; for the paper command that account can be the method's section, which the operator already has the user read (ops:408). The sentences of ops that carry an action of the operator inside such paragraphs (ops:404 "Read them with the user", ops:408 "ask the user to read and confirm the document") stay where the action is. (b) Collect the runner's inside (ops:603-666 and the corresponding part of ops:557-573) under one heading named for what it is, to be read when a status needs interpreting.

**The method document is given whole to sessions that apply a part of it** (B2, S3). `paper.py` hands the whole file to nine kinds of session (`planning_kit` at `paper.py:3442`, `kit` `:3451`, `plan_from_draft` `:3531`, `compare_sketch` `:3665`, `repair_session` `:3962`, `compare_section` `:4211`, `resolve_session` `:4365`, `cover_section` `:4461`, `restate` `:4997`) and one section, through `method_part` (`paper.py:369-374`), to the sessions that read. The prompts of the comparing kinds already name the sections that apply; the two named for a comparison come to 7.4 of 80 thousand bytes, and a repair of a wide line needs "The LaTeX of a part" (5.4 thousand). Ways: (a) shorten the sections that no session applies, which lowers the cost for all; (b) give a kind the sections its prompt names, with the existing function. The second needs `paper.py` at those places, the sentence of `METHOD_FILE` (`paper.py:1987-1989`, "Read it in full before anything else"), the tests that assert which files a session is given (`test_paper.py:1089`, `:2512`, `:2981`, `:2988`, `:3254`, `:3795`, `:3920`), and makes each section title used a text found by its exact wording. No statement of the user was found on which paper sessions receive the whole document.

**`finalize/common.md` is one text for sessions of two kinds** (B2, S4). `finalize.py:231-233` builds every prompt as the common text plus one instruction. "Reading the imported library", "Compiling" and "What every Lean file of the library keeps" (6.6 thousand bytes, 48 percent of the common text) concern sessions that write Lean. Five kinds compile (`selection`, `restore`, `simplify`, `arrange`, `foldback`); ten do not, and `common.md:47` says so itself. Six instructions then say again that their session writes no Lean and compiles nothing (`selection-read.md:16`, `records-compare.md:23`, `draft-compare.md:25`, `sources.md:26`, `history-read.md:35`, `package-read.md:31`), and `package-read.md:5` asks its session to read "as if you had not been told" what the common text said. Way: divide the common text into what holds for every session and what holds for sessions that work on Lean, and have the program add the second for the kinds that need it. It needs `finalize.py:231-240`, `test_finalize.py:1106-1118`, and the references to the section "Compiling" in `restore.md:28`, `selection.md:51`, `simplify.md:51` and `foldback.md:62`. `records-write`, `records-compare` and `review` read Lean and need the conventions without the commands, so the division has three parts and not two.

**What the instruction files of `finalize/` say in more than one file** (B2, S5). For one session none of it is repetition; for whoever maintains the files each is a text kept in two places. An opening that says what the library is, in eight files and four wordings. The user's instruction on simplifying with the list "Research under pressure leaves things of the following kinds" in `review.md:13-19` and `simplify.md:7-13`. The form of `part.json` and the rules for `interface` in `simplify.md:18-37` and `foldback.md:27-54`. The instruction to build the whole library once, in `simplify.md:53`, `arrange.md:36` and `foldback.md:62`. The command that parses a record, in `records-write.md:74-76` and `records-compare.md:57-59`. The same incident told to the writing session and to the session that checks it, in six pairs. The two history files share a third of each (the layout, two tables of words, the form of an entry). A shared file given to both, as `records-format.md` is given to the records pair, would hold that once, and needs a new file. One gap was found beside these: `arrange.md:26` tells its session to take up "a simplification in the sense of the review", and that session receives neither `review.md` nor the user's standard for simplifying.

**`SKILL.md` is an entry point and a second, shorter ops** (B2, S6). Its first section defines the vocabulary. Each later section pairs a summary of a section of ops with a pointer that lists that section's contents. Way: keep in `SKILL.md` what only it says (who the operator is, the vocabulary, what to report and in which scope, the conduct at `SKILL.md:70-74`) and replace the paired summaries by one table of occasions and sections. It needs `SKILL.md` alone. The `description` field at line 3 is what makes an agent load the skill and is not part of this.

### 13.5 Texts that must move together

Section 7.2 gives a table of texts that a program, a test or another text finds by their exact wording. The investigations for this run found more. A correction that rewords, moves or renames one side carries the other side in the same correction (freeze document). The lists with line numbers are in the reports; `B2_scripts/out_prompt_ties.txt` lists every assertion on the text of a prompt.

- **Prompt wording asserted by tests.** 96 assertions: 73 in `test_paper.py`, 15 in `test_ending.py`, 8 in `test_finalize.py`. Of the 73, 39 are sentences of the prompt constants of `paper.py` (for example `test_paper.py:1095` "The papers come before the writing."; `:1356` "In the note of a finding that you revise, say which passages you changed for it."). A prompt reworded alone fails its test. Whether a test should assert the wording of a prompt at all is a question for the repairers of the tests (section 14).
- **The installed instruction files.** `test_finalize.py:1106-1118` reads `finalize/common.md` and every installed instruction: placeholders filled, the prompt begins with the common text and contains the Python path, the broker's path, `--expect-mb 64`, and "There is none" from `finalize.py:238`. The other tests of that module write stand-in instruction files.
- **Headings and phrases that a program writes and a guide quotes.** `finalize.py:1797` "Stage 4: what the paper lost so that it says no more than the library" (ops:499); `finalize.py:2035` "What the reader of the package found and the run did not correct" (ops:509); `finalize.py:2058` "What to do" (ops:529); `package.py:1653-1656` "Solved problems" (ops:511, :535); `package.py:1404` "Where the result comes from" (ops:549); `finalize.py:529` "the packaging of a refutation is not built" (ops:455); `runtime_support.py:53` "Runner memory reserve released; dispatch will wait for recovery." (ops:673); the words of the map `proof on a separate card`, `proof`, `another proof` (ops:384; `test_depmap.py:392`); the three headings of the timeline at `package.py:1304` (`history-write.md:75-94`, `history-read.md:52-71`; `test_finalize.py:895`); the values of `why` that `package.py` writes (`records-write.md:33`, `records-compare.md:13`; `test_finalize.py:556`, `:568`, `:1777`).
- **Numbers.** The constants at `paper.py:219-230` (`PLAN_ATTEMPTS`, `READING_ROUNDS`, `CHOSEN_PAPERS`, `SECTION_PAPERS`, `PARALLEL`, `LIKENESS_PAPERS`, `LIKENESS_ROUNDS`, `LIKENESS_CLEAN`, `LIMIT_WAITS`) are written out as words in ops:408, :410, :418, :440, :483 and in method:13, :206, :208, :265. Three prompts say "four research articles" and two say "two of the plan's papers" as fixed text (`paper.py:2243`, `:2249`, `:2464`, `:2756`, `:2842`).
- **Section names in quotation marks.** Ninety quoted names resolve today. `SKILL.md` names sixteen headings of ops and "Judging from the text itself" (`reference/common.md:71`). The method quotes its own headings in about twenty places. The strings of `paper.py` name eleven sections of the method in 28 places.
- **Headings quoted in the session guides** (B1). 46 headings are quoted 123 times. Renaming or removing one leaves a sentence that sends its reader nowhere, and nothing reports it. Quoted most: "Seeing work that is still underway: the board" (7), "Turning a session's work into the canon" (6), "The standing of a statement", "Building on what already exists" and "Stating mathematics in Lean as you work" (5 each). Two quoted names are bold labels of paragraphs and not headings ("Whether a new proof takes the place of the old one" and "What you think of a division", `review-output.md:68`, `:76`; the labels at `review.md:71`, `:73`). "What makes a statement formal", quoted at `lean-broker.md:91`, is a heading of ops.
- **Sentences that state which roles receive a document** (B1): `common.md:35`, `:113`, `:141`, `:263`, `:279`; `canon.md:5`; `writing.md:45`; `records.md:3`; `retained-work.md:5`; `sections.md:3`; `tools.md:3`; `explore.md:82`. They state `context.py:18-25`, and `test_context.py:523-544` asserts the same table.
- **Links in a guide of a pack** (B1). `rebase_links` in `context.py` resolves every Markdown link of an embedded guide against the session's `context/` folder, and a target outside it raises `ValueError('context reference escapes its source area')`, which stops every launch of the roles that receive the guide. `test_context.py:136-146` tests the function on made-up links and not on the guides. A passage that moves to another guide takes its links with it, rewritten for the new place.
- **Output of the program that a guide shows** (B1): `canon_tool.py:104` "written in the record; its Lean proof ends in sorry" and `:178` "(read one with --part, all with --full; --part source prints the Lean text)" in the example view at `canon.md:124`, `:136` and the table at `:143`; the words of the view's lines (`canon.md:139-160`), the standing words of the frontier example (`common.md:192-199`) and the states of the root (`common.md:209`), which `canon_tool.py` and `knowledge.py` produce; "source changed after submission" (`lean.md:92`; `test_lean_broker.py:593`).
- **Numbers in the session guides** (B1): "about the first 160 characters" (`review-output.md:72`; `knowledge.py:559`); "twenty unless `--last`" (`canon.md:98`, `formalizer.md:21`; `canon_tool.py:430`); "the first twelve" (`canon.md:150`; `canon_tool.py:126`); "`--timeout 50`" (`lean.md:87`, `change-format.md:130`; `context.py:77`, `:81`, `canon_tool.py:436`); "up to twenty seconds" (`lean.md:51`, `lean-broker.md:69`, `:75`); "ten seconds" (`lean-broker.md:73-75`; `lean_broker.py:46`); the table of defaults at `lean-broker.md:45-55` with "three times" and "two by default" (`lean.md:49`, `:182`) against `settings.py`; "four by default" (`organizer-output.md:15`). "40,000 characters" (`records.md:120`) is in no program.
