- **Names a session copies from a guide into a command or a file** (B1): the keys of `commands` in `input.json` (`context.py:71-87`), used as `commands.<name>` throughout; the fields of `task`; `settings.acceptance.decline_reducible_proofs` and `decline_uncomprehended_computation` (`review.md:117`; `settings.py:40-41`); `all_unresolved_work_occupied` (`retained-work.md:102`; `availability.py:83`); `output/end-decision.json` and its four fields (`retained-work.md:63-72`, `resume/common.md:7`; `ending.py:50-66`); the headings of a record and of an approach entry (`records.md:47-53`, `:106`; `canon.py:33-35`).
- **The worked candidate of `change-format.md:156-277`** (B1) is the same mathematics as the fixture of the tests (`tests/support.py:85-480` and the modules that use it). No test reads the guide, so the two can drift apart without a failure.
- **Tests that use the installed guides as data** (B1): `tests/support.py:202` copies `reference/`, `roles/` and `resume/` into the skill a test runs on; `test_ending.py:619-659` builds every role's resume text from the installed files and fails on an empty file or a missing placeholder; `test_ending.py:184`, `:227` and `:229` assert one sentence each of `resume/common.md:3`, `resume/output.md:3` and `resume/review.md:1`. A guide that is removed or emptied breaks these; a reworded one does not, except in those three sentences.
- **Words and paths by which a session finds text after a compaction** (B5). Sessions search for `end-decision`, `outcome.json`, `checkout` and `lean_broker`, search the pack for the markers that begin and end each embedded guide, and open `context/reference/retained-work.md` by the path that `resume/common.md` gives (149 of 151 later readings of that file). No test asserts those words or that path, so renaming them breaks the look-ups without a sign.
- **Messages of the program that a guide quotes** (B3). Rewording the guide breaks no test and leaves the guide quoting a message the program does not print.

Sections 14 and 15 list the further ties that concern tests and program alone.

## 14. The tests: bulk, cost and gaps

This section is a digest of the investigation report `임시/do-math_검증_20261125-3/조사/B3_시험.md`, made on the evening of 25 November against commit `893392d5`. Its scripts read the test files and the logs of the second run's test runs; no test was run for it. The report gives every table with its script, thirty cases of overlap, the twenty most expensive tests and fixtures, and the branches it found untested. What follows are measurements and an investigator's readings, to be established in the files before anything rests on them (section 1). Sections 7.4 and 8 describe the suite and what has run; section 11 gives the criteria for judging a test.

**Where the time goes.** The one complete run on record (section 8) took about forty-three minutes on a loaded machine. Four modules took two thirds of it: `test_paper` 504 seconds, `test_finalize` 444, `test_publication` 436 and `test_research` 305. The time is spent on starting real processes along the real path, not on assertions. A test of `test_finalize` that runs the pipeline takes 19 to 27 seconds, because each starts the pipeline at its first step with the run's own broker. A test of `test_publication` takes 8.2 seconds on average, for two real builds by the broker. A test of `test_paper` that runs the command takes about 7 seconds, because each runs the whole command with about a dozen stand-in session processes. No timing of single tests exists anywhere: the logs carry seconds per module or per group of classes only, and no run on record was made on an idle machine, so only relative weights are reliable.

**The cost comes from structure.** Repairing tests one at a time would not remove it.

- Each module runs in its own process, so each of the fourteen modules that use the instance fixture builds the `init` template again. The scenario of nine statements named `ways` is built in four modules, and in one of them twice for a single test.
- `test_finalize` and `test_paper` start their command from its beginning in every test, although both commands can continue a stopped run. A run stopped at a step boundary, built once and copied for each test, could serve the tests whose subject lies after that step. That would change those tests from a fresh run to a continued run. Whether the starting state and the real path of each test survive that change is exactly what the freeze document's rule on tests asks a correction to show.
- `TransportCheckTests` makes real builds by the broker inside loops of subtests in order to test checks of form, and publishes operator questions in `setUp` for each of its eighteen tests.
- Running the stand-in sessions inside the test process would save process starts and would remove the real launching path. Under the user's requirement that a cheaper test keeps its real path (section 3), that way is closed.

**The bulk is spread out.** The tests hold 1,581 thousand bytes against 1,222 of program (1,207 without `exemplars/fetch.py`); by subsystem the ratio runs from 0.63 (the finalize pipeline) to 2.48 (the runner and the research). Eighty percent of the bytes are the bodies of tests. Comment lines are 153 thousand bytes; in a sample of thirty blocks they mostly state the property asserted, and 25 lines in the suite describe history. Lines copied between places account for about 56 thousand bytes. True overlap is rare: of 194 pairs of tests that make the same calls into the program, eight share most of their assertions. The repetition that exists is of three kinds: the whole sequence of sessions asserted again in 33 tests of `test_paper`; siblings that differ by a value and are written as separate methods, which could become subtests and would save lines and not time; and starting states built again for each test.

**Gaps are in branches for failure.** By name the coverage is saturated: 701 of 702 functions are named in a test or reached from one. Of twelve branches the investigator checked by reading, those on paths that matter to a running research or a publication and that no test reaches are: the two refusals of `restart-broker` (`runner.py:1601`, `:1609`); the catch-all of a finalize step for a defect of the program (`finalize.py:214`); the restart of the pipeline's broker when it dies during a run (`watch_broker`, `finalize.py:352-359`); a failed move of the package into `solved/`, and continuing after the move (`finalize.py:2131`, `:2069`); the failure path of the history thread; and `terminate_process` refusing a process ID that has been reused (`runtime_support.py:319-338`). A mechanical pass lists a further 49 `except` handlers and 158 early refusals with no sign of a test; three of the branches checked by hand were false signs, so that list is one of candidates.

**What the second run already repaired** and need not be looked for again (report, section 4.6): the temporary folders left behind; the instance copies made and thrown away; the copy of the guides made for every test (now made only when used); `kinds()` defined five times; unchecked `assertRaises(paper.Stop)`, down from nineteen to seven; the options `--model`, `--effort` and `--title-page` without a test; the Lake stand-in answering for names it does not know.

**Still open by the same report:** 21 checks in `test_finalize` that what a session was given contains certain files, where the exact set is not asserted; 16 assertions that `paper.pdf` exists, which cannot fail with the TeX stand-in; a test without an assertion at `test_canon.py:292`; and the test of the ten-second window at `test_lean_broker.py:2160`, which depends on the clock and which the second run raised and rejected twice (section 9).

**Not run together.** The 952 tests now in the tree have never run together on one version. Since the complete run, `f98f3dd4` changed `test_questions`, `test_research` and `test_views`, and `893392d5` changed `test_finalize` and `test_paper`, each with the program modules it touches; on the last code `test_lean_broker` ran 40 of its 85 tests (section 8).

**Texts that must move together, beyond the table of section 7.2** (report, section 7): the hand-written table of roles and guides at `test_context.py:521-536`, which fails when a guide file is renamed, merged or split, and not when a passage moves between files; `INSTRUCTIONS` at `test_finalize.py:178-180`, which also generates the stand-in instruction files (`:291-298`), so that a new or merged file of `finalize/` is neither checked nor stood in for; the exact lists of given files that name `paper-method.md`, `user-writing-preferences.md`, `records.md` and `records-format.md`; and the string `method, section "The implementation does not show"`, asserted at `test_paper.py:755`, `:772` and `:1310`. Messages of the program that a guide quotes are a tie in the other direction: rewording the guide breaks no test and leaves the guide quoting a message the program does not print.

## 15. The program: what is more than its job needs, and where it is fragile

This section is a digest of the investigation report `임시/do-math_검증_20261125-3/조사/B4_프로그램.md`, made on the evening of 25 November against commit `893392d5` by reading, by `ast` scripts and from Git; nothing was run. The report gives every figure with its script and every instance with its line. What follows are measurements and an investigator's readings, to be established in the files before anything rests on them (section 1).

**Size and composition.** The program is 21 files and 1,222 thousand bytes, 20,485 lines. It held 583 thousand at the rebuild of 22 November and 826 thousand on the morning of 25 November; of the day's growth, 238 thousand is the two new modules of the finalize pipeline and 147 thousand is `paper.py`. `paper.py` alone is 389 thousand bytes: the class `Paper` 128, the other functions 126, 85 module-level string constants (the prompts) 82, and a module docstring of 15 that tells the stages again which `reference/operations.md` and `reference/paper-method.md` also tell. Across the program, docstrings are 15 percent of the bytes and comments 6 percent; twenty-five docstrings of more than a thousand bytes hold 53 thousand. The prompts and the sentences the programs write for people are text that a reader reads, and section 13 treats them with the guides.

**What is not used.** Little. The second run removed most of the unused names that earlier investigations had listed. An `ast` sweep of the whole skill leaves one parameter that nothing passes (`paper.act_on_open(gate=)`) and five names that only tests use. What remains of this kind is written and never read: files, state fields and keys that the program produces and nothing consumes (report, section 2).

**The same thing implemented in more than one place** (report, section 3), in places with different behaviour:

- three writers of files. The runtime's writer shares one module-level buffer of 64 KiB and is not safe for threads (`runtime_support.py:7-8`, `:131-157`), so the threaded paper and finalize commands wrap it in a lock (`paper.py:315-330`) or use a third writer (`package.py:52-74`), which creates folders, turns CRLF into LF, does not `fsync`, and gives up on a Windows sharing error after 2.5 seconds where the runtime's waits 60;
- two parsers of the import header of a Lean file (`canon.header_scan`, `lean_broker.read_header`);
- two lists of the allowed axioms (`settings.AXIOMS`, `package.PERMITTED_AXIOMS`);
- three functions named `ensure_broker`;
- two launchers of AI sessions, the research session host and the one in `paper.py` that the finalize pipeline also uses. Only the first recognises a provider's refusal, resumes after the limit on output tokens, and copies transcripts;
- two helpers for operating-system locks;
- the name `ROLES` for two different sets.

**The over-engineering audit of 24 November, item by item** (report, section 4.1). Three of its findings about the program were changed on the user's answer: the broker's state file is written at most every ten seconds, ELK is gone, and the sub-reviewers' report files are gone. Three the user kept (section 4.6). The rest stands in the code as the audit found it. About ten of those were put to the user and not answered (section 4.13), among them leases on questions, the table of candidate hashes, the gate on attempts with its tokens, the parser of the Codex client's rate-limit sentences, `--open-findings`, `--typeset-only` and the machinery that undoes a narrow repair of the paper. About fifteen were never put to the user, among them the GO gate, the request ID of an end decision, and a refusal that is detected in two places. The report says which is which. A correction that removes one of the items the user kept, or the request ID of an end decision, which the freeze document protects, is not open; the others are places to look, within the freeze document's rules on what a running instance depends on.

**The same shapes in code written since the audit** (report, section 4.2). The finalize pipeline hashes again every product a step reads and stops when one differs, and stops when the hash of an accepted session output has changed (`finalize.py:179-215`, `:254-267`). Both guard a state that only an edit from outside the run produces, which the operator's guide tells the operator not to make. Nobody has decided it.

**Where the program is fragile** (report, section 5; the report gives a census by module and the ten instances it judges most likely to fail in a real run). The first five:

- Publication moves records and question files with `shutil.move`, without the retry on a Windows sharing error that the runner's own writer has (`runner.py:673`, `:886`, `canon.py:1272`). A sharing error there ends the runner.
- `finalize.finish` moves the package into `solved/` with `shutil.move` (`finalize.py:1931`). When the rename fails it copies and deletes, which can leave both copies, and the next start then refuses because `solved/<name>` exists. A retrying `rename_directory` exists (`runtime_support.py:178-193`) and is not used there.
- Nothing prevents two finalize runs on one work folder.
- Lake, Git and the map command are run without a time limit (`package.py:114`, `:140-155`, `:821-828`, `finalize.py:1759`, `runtime_support.py:364`), where the probe of Lean's version has 300 seconds.
- Exception tuples that include `KeyError`, `TypeError` and `AttributeError` turn a defect of the program into a verdict on a session's output, in the transport check (`check.py:422`) and in publication (`runner.py:766-767`). Finished work is then discarded or charged to the candidate.

These are conditions of real operation. They are to be kept apart from safeguards for a state that only the program's own defect could produce, which the user refused (section 4.7); the report lists the places it reads as such safeguards in its section 5.3.

**Structure** (report, section 6). Six causes that repairs of single places would not remove:

- S1: `paper.py` carries the session launcher of two commands besides the paper pipeline; `finalize.py` imports `Sessions`, `Stop`, `Unusable`, `produce`, `WRITING` and helpers from it.
- S2: one global buffer is the reason for three file writers. A buffer for each call or each thread would remove the cause and two of the writers. Any call from a pipeline thread into the roughly eighty functions that write through the shared buffer has to remember the lock; two call sites do today (`finalize.py:435`, `paper.py:3261`).
- S3: the two launchers of AI sessions differ in what they detect.
- S4: four rules on which two processes must agree are each written in two places.
- S5: the shape of each shared state file exists only in the code that reads and writes it.
- S6: text for people sits inside program logic, in docstrings that tell again what the guides tell.

For each the report says what a solution would need and what it could break in the instance that is running from these files. One caution it draws: the sunflower instance's broker runs the program it loaded when it was last started (section 6.1), so a correction to one of the two header parsers alone would make the instance's broker and its other processes disagree until the broker is restarted.

**Texts that must move together, beyond the table of section 7.2** (report, section 7): the keys of a record's header and the names of its sections (`canon.py:31-36`); the choices of `--part` (`canon_tool.py:408`); the four exact fields and two values of an end decision (`ending.py:29-36`); `disposition: no_research` (`availability.py:59-64`); the names of review output that are refused (`check.py:332-333`); each prompt of the paper command and the reader of its output, which stand about two thousand lines apart in the same file; and wording that comes from outside the skill, the tool names of the Claude client in `SELF_SCHEDULING` and the usage-limit sentence of the Codex client.

## 16. Structural directions the run has adopted

A structural problem is one whose cause is how material is divided and arranged across several files, so that repairing its instances one by one would not remove it. The freeze document gives a repairer two ways to raise a solution. One that can be written as corrections to the files as they stand is raised as corrections and judged like any other. One that cannot (it needs a file created, removed or renamed, or it is too large for one repairer to write out in one round) is raised as a proposal, the item the contracts call 상신 제안.

This section is where a proposal of the second kind is carried forward. When the judges have accepted such a proposal, and it neither changes a decision of the user nor touches what a running instance depends on, the driving session enters its direction here before the next round, creates, removes or renames the files concerned, and enters them in the freeze document's list of files in scope. A file it creates begins with one line that says what the file is to hold: an empty file of `reference/` would be copied into every new session's folder, and an empty file of `resume/` fails a test. The repairers of later rounds then carry it out by corrections, each judged like any other. An entry states the structure to be reached, the files concerned and what has to move together. It does not prescribe wording, and it does not decide whether a particular correction keeps every detail: the judges decide that for each correction. An entry is the driving session's adoption under the user's request for this run (section 3). It is not a decision of the user, and a repairer who finds, in the files, that carrying an entry out as written would lose a detail or break a decision of section 4 does not carry it out that way: it takes the course the files support and says in the item what it departed from and why, or, where nothing can be raised, says so in its record (freeze document, 「무엇을 찾는가」, last paragraph).

Proposals that change a decision of the user or what a running instance depends on are not entered here. They go to the user.

Thirty-two directions have been entered so far. The first two came from proposals that the three judges of round 1 accepted, and round 2 carried both out. The next three are decisions of the user of 26 November, and all three have been carried out: the second in round 2, the other two in round 3. The sixth came from a proposal that the judges of round 2 accepted, and round 3 carried it out. The seventh came from a proposal that the judges of round 3 accepted, and round 4 carried it out. The eighth is a decision of the user on a proposal of round 4, and round 5 carried it out. Directions 16.9 to 16.11 were entered after round 7 and 16.12 to 16.14 after round 8; five of the six are decisions of the user or follow from them, and one is a proposal that the driving session adopted. Rounds 8 and 9 carried out all of them but 16.12, which round 11 carried out. Directions 16.15 and 16.17 are decisions of the user of 26 November and were entered after round 10; round 11 carried out 16.15. The number 16.16 is not in use: it was a direction the user decided and withdrew the same day, and the end of this section records it. Directions 16.18 to 16.24 were entered after round 11: five are decisions of the user (16.18, 16.19, 16.22, 16.23, 16.24) and two are proposals of round 11 that the driving session adopted (16.20, 16.21). Round 12 carried out 16.17 to 16.23. Directions 16.25 to 16.28 were entered after round 12, and all four are decisions of the user of 27 November: three on proposals of round 12, and one an instruction that reached this run from the session that operates the running instance. Round 13 carried out 16.24 to 16.27. Directions 16.29 to 16.31 were entered after round 13. They are decisions of the driving session, made under the user's instruction of 27 November that it settle what is open for the rest of the run (section 3, the last subsection). They are not decisions of the user: each entry says what the user had decided beside it, and the user can reverse each. A repairer treats them as it treats the driving session's other designs in this section, and does not raise the same matter again as a proposal. Round 14 carried out 16.28 to 16.31. No direction is outstanding, and from round 15 no repairer is handed one. Directions 16.32 and 16.33 are decisions of the user of 27 November. Neither was handed to a repairer: at the user's instruction each was carried out by one agent between rounds 17 and 18, and no judge read the result before it entered the tree, so round 18 examines both as it examines the rest of the product. What rounds 12 to 14 brought in under these directions (messages to a running session, the hand-over of a task to a session that continues it, the record of what a session used, the lock on the modules of the root's definitions, the marks on concerns and findings, the archive of raw conversations, the rule on choosing work, the peer that loses its host) is part of the product now and is examined like everything else in it. After the directions stand the proposals that were considered and not adopted. Where a direction has been carried out, the end of its entry says what later rounds still have to do. Line numbers are left out because the files change from round to round; the names are what to search for.

### 16.1 The session runner of the two commands has a module of its own

Carried out in round 2. `runtime/command_sessions.py` holds the runner that launches a session of the paper command or of the finalize pipeline, waits out a usage limit, reads what the session wrote and runs sessions side by side (the class `Sessions`, with the exceptions, constants and helpers it uses), and `runtime/paper.py` and `runtime/finalize.py` import it from there. The tests put their stand-ins in the place of names of that module. One step of the direction is still open and can be a correction of its own: `runtime/package.py` has a `read_text` with the same body as the `read_file` of the new module, written a second time when the runner still stood in the paper module. Moving the prompt constants out of `paper.py` is not part of the direction.

### 16.2 The stand-ins of the paper command's and the pipeline's tests have files of their own

Carried out in round 2. `tests/paper_cli.py` and `tests/finalize_cli.py` hold the stand-ins that `tests/test_paper.py` and `tests/test_finalize.py` launch in the place of a session, of TeX, of Lake and of the command that reports the version of Lean, so that a stand-in process no longer compiles a whole test module. Joining the stand-ins of the two modules into one function is not part of the direction.

### 16.3 The account of the problem as a whole gets a document of its own

Carried out in round 3; the last paragraph says what is left. This direction is a decision of the user, given on 26 November in two sentences: "yeah taking out q0 account procedure from peer.md into a separate thing would be better." (15:46:47Z) and, when the driving session had reported that the standard to which the account is written is the other half of the same matter, "yeah move the standard into the same document too" (16:40:37Z). The file `reference/q0-account.md` was created before round 2 with a title and one sentence. The full account of what moves and how the other documents stand to it is section 6 of the report `임시/do-math_검증_20261125-3/조사/C1_가이드_설계.md`; what follows gives its substance.

The structure to reach: one document holds what a session needs in order to write or to judge the account of `q0`. It holds the standard (who reads the account and what each of them needs from it, the room of 40,000 characters and why there is one, what the account holds that no other place of the canon can, how a text is brought within its room) and the procedure by which a peer writes the account. The pack of a peer carries the document when the peer's assignment names `q0` as its question, and the pack of a review carries it when the candidate it judges carries the account; no other pack carries it. Every other session can open it by a link, since every file of `reference/` is copied into a session's folder.

What moves, with nothing lost and nothing reworded beyond what the move requires:

- from `roles/peer.md`, section "The understanding so far of a question": the six paragraphs that begin "When the question is `q0`, this text is the account of the problem as a whole" and end with the paragraph that begins "Tell review what you did to the account". The paragraph before them (how an understanding text is written, for every question) and the paragraph after them stay. In their place `peer.md` keeps one sentence: that when the question is `q0` the text is the account of the problem as a whole, and that how it is written is in the document, which a peer in that case receives.
- from `reference/records.md`: the part "The account of the problem as a whole". `records.md` keeps the format of an understanding text. Its sentence "Only the text of `q0` has a fixed room, for the reason the next part gives" stays with its address changed.
- from `reference/common.md`, the paragraph that says what the account is: the sentence "For that reason the account has a fixed room, and each peer that replaces it answers for the whole text within that room; it does not append to the text it found" is the peer's rule and goes to the document. That the account has a fixed room stays there as a fact.

The document also tells the peer one thing no guide tells it now: that the assessment review writes beneath the current account is addressed to the peer who next replaces it (`roles/review.md` tells the reviewer so).

What has to change together:

- `runtime/context.py` builds the list of documents of a pack from the role. For a peer the runner sets `task['question']` to the question its producer held, or to `None`, and a replacement peer receives the same field; the root question is the constant `ROOT_QUESTION` of `runtime/knowledge.py`. A review's assignment has no such field; it holds `candidate`, the folder of the candidate, which exists when the review is launched, so the presence of `questions/q0.md` in it shows that the review judges an account. How a review's assignment is built for a comprehensive review was not read by the designer; the repairer reads it. `tests/test_context.py` asserts the list of documents for each role with an empty assignment; the two new cases need tests of their own, made in the same correction.
- Every place that names `records.md` or `peer.md` for the standard or the procedure names the document: `reference/writing.md` (two sentences), `reference/change-format.md` (one), `roles/review.md` (where it judges the account), `roles/explore.md` (which names the standard as a copy in the session folder, since an explorer's pack does not hold it; the same holds for the document). The heading "The account of the problem as a whole" of `records.md` is quoted by `roles/explore.md`, `roles/peer.md`, `roles/review.md` and `reference/writing.md`; each quotation follows the text.
- `roles/review.md`, where it speaks of the list a peer names in its description: that list is made only for `q0`, and the sentence says so once the paragraph that asks for the list has left `peer.md`.
- A link inside a moved paragraph is rewritten for its new place; a link that leaves the files a session is given stops the launch of every session whose pack carries it (section 6.1).
- The sentences of `reference/common.md`, `reference/writing.md` and elsewhere that say which roles receive which document, as far as they speak of the standard.
- The pipeline's record sessions receive `records.md` under another name (`finalize/records-write.md`, `finalize/records-compare.md`) and are told to read two of its sections, "The record" and "Weight: units and steps". Neither moves.

Round 3 made the move in one correction, joined with the move of section 16.5 because the two met in one line of `reference/writing.md`. `reference/q0-account.md` now holds the standard and the procedure, and `runtime/context.py` puts the document into the pack of a peer whose assignment names `q0` and of a review whose candidate carries `questions/q0.md`; `tests/test_context.py` asserts both cases. Three things are left, each for the repairer whose area holds `reference/q0-account.md`. The document does not yet tell the peer the one thing named above, that the assessment review writes beneath the current account is addressed to the peer who next replaces it. The moved paragraphs were moved without rewording, so that a judge could hold them against the original; they can now be tightened where they stand, like any other paragraph of a guide. And the direction named two sentences of `reference/writing.md` that point to the standard, of which the correction changed one; the repairer checks whether any place still names `records.md` or `peer.md` for the standard or the procedure.

### 16.4 `reference/evidence.md` gave what it alone said to the guides every role receives, and is gone

A decision of the user (section 3: "yeah do the evidence.md plan"). The document told a session how to judge whether an element of the canon gives the premise its own work needs. No pack carried it, and four of 331 sessions opened it. Round 2 carried the ten points that no pack told into `reference/common.md` (at the end of "Reading the canon", where a statement without a proof is introduced, and where concerns and findings are told) and into `reference/canon.md` (where the form by which a proof names what it uses is given), and removed the link to the document. The driving session removed the file before round 3. `tests/test_context.py` still uses the name `evidence.md` in a made-up link, which needs no file.

### 16.5 "Writing the texts of the canon" moves from `reference/writing.md` to `reference/records.md`

Carried out in round 3; the last paragraph says what is left. This direction is a decision of the user. The design of section 17 had found that the section "Writing the texts of the canon" of `reference/writing.md` (about 4 thousand bytes) says how the peer writes records, approach entries and understanding texts, and that it nonetheless goes to all six roles; the designer put the matter to the user and did not make it part of the design. The driving session reported this, and the user answered: "I want it moved" (26 November, 16:51:40Z on 25 November UTC). The round that was running when the answer came worked under the rules as they stood, so the direction holds from the round after it.

The structure to reach: the section stands in `reference/records.md`, which the roles that write and judge those texts receive (peer, review, organizer), and no longer in `reference/writing.md`. `records.md` then holds, for each written text of the canon, its format and how it is written, in one place. An attempt, an explorer and a formalizer no longer receive the section. What such a session has to supply so that its peer can write those texts is told to it in its own documents: `reference/retained-work.md` (how each part of the report becomes a part of the candidate), and the passages of `roles/attempt.md`, `roles/explore.md` and `roles/formalizer.md` on what to leave for the peer. The repairer who carries the move out checks, sentence by sentence, that nothing in the section which a producing session acts on stands only there; anything that does goes into `retained-work.md` in the same correction.

What this changes in the design of section 17: where the report names `writing.md` as the home of how a record's parts are written (matter M8), of how an approach entry reads (M15) and of the two sentences on what kind of text an understanding text is (M16), and where it says that the peer would be given, in that section, the reason why the written proof is the argument of the Lean proof (M10), the home is the section in its new place. The sentences of `records.md` that say again how a part is written then stand in the same document as the section and are joined with it.

What has to change together:

- The sentence near the head of `writing.md` that says the section is "particular to the texts a peer writes" and that a producing session reads it to know what its report should supply.
- The sentence of `records.md` that sends its reader to `writing.md` for how each text is written, and the first paragraph of the section itself, which names `records.md` as the place of the formats.
- The links in `roles/peer.md` that point to the section, and any other pointer to it by name; the heading "Writing the texts of the canon" is quoted by `writing.md` itself.
- Links inside the moved paragraphs, rewritten for the new place.
- The sessions of the finalize pipeline that write and compare records receive `records.md` whole, under another name, and are told to read two of its sections; their own instruction files tell them how each part of a record is written. The move puts the section into the file they receive. The repairer says, in the correction, whether those sessions are thereby told one thing in two wordings, and if so carries the change to `finalize/records-write.md` and `finalize/records-compare.md` (for instance by naming the sections they are to read, as those files already do).

Round 3 made the move. Three things are left. The sentences of `reference/records.md` that say again how a part of a record is written now stand in the same document as the section, and the repairer whose area holds `records.md` joins them with it. The reason why the written proof has to be the argument of the Lean proof, which no guide gives the peer (section 17, what the designer found besides), has its place in the moved section, and the same repairer puts it there. And the statement that this entry asks for on the pipeline's record sessions was not made: the repairer whose area holds `finalize/records-write.md` and `finalize/records-compare.md` reads the section in its new place against those two files, says whether those sessions are now told one thing in two wordings, and carries the correction if one is needed.

### 16.6 The fixture of the broker's tests has a file of its own

Carried out in round 3, from a proposal that the three judges of round 2 accepted. `tests/broker_fixture.py` holds the fixture that runs a Lean broker inside the test process (`FAKE`, `source` and the class `BrokerFixture`), with the bodies it had at the head of `tests/test_lean_broker.py`, and `tests/test_lean_broker.py`, `tests/test_baseline_estimate.py`, `tests/test_sampling.py` and `tests/test_state_save.py` import it from there. A change to a test of `test_lean_broker.py` therefore no longer reopens the results of the other three modules. The fixture was not put into `tests/support.py`, because that file imports the runner, and the broker's four test modules would then be reached by changes to six program files they do not touch now.

### 16.7 How a review judges the account of `q0` moves into the same document

Carried out in round 4. From a proposal that the three judges of round 3 accepted, adopted by the driving session inside the direction of section 16.3; it is not a further decision of the user. `roles/review.md`, in its section "The state the change attaches to statements and questions", holds a paragraph of about 1.6 thousand bytes that begins "When the question is `q0`, the text is the account of the problem as a whole": to judge the new account by the standard of the document, to read it by itself before comparing it with the old one, what to ask of it, that a shorter account is no ground for concern, and that the reviewer counts the characters of the text because no program checks the room. Every review receives the paragraph. Only a review whose candidate replaces the account acts on it, and that review receives `reference/q0-account.md` (section 16.3).

The structure to reach: the paragraph stands in `reference/q0-account.md`, as the part of the document written for the review, and `roles/review.md` keeps one sentence: that when the candidate replaces the account of `q0`, how the account is judged is in the document, which a review in that case receives. The paragraph's last sentence, on what the sub-reviewers are given, speaks of every replacing text and stays in `roles/review.md`. The sentences in the paragraph before it that begin "When the question is `q0`, the description of the change says what the peer did to the text" concern the same case; whether they travel with the paragraph is the repairer's to decide from what a review of another candidate needs. Nothing is reworded beyond what the move requires.

Round 4 made the move: `reference/q0-account.md` has the section "How a review judges the account", and the two sentences on the list a peer names travelled with the paragraph.

### 16.8 A session of the two commands that a provider refuses is waited for and launched again

Carried out in round 5. A decision of the user (section 3: "wait and relaunch"), on a proposal that the three judges of round 4 accepted. The runner of the two commands' sessions, `Sessions` in `runtime/command_sessions.py`, waits out a usage limit and then launches the session again. It does not recognise the other refusals that concern the model, a provider that has no capacity and a provider that asks for credit, although `runtime/retention.py` holds the rule that tells them (`refusal`, which the runner of the research uses). Such a session is launched again twice at once and then counted as one that left no usable output: the run stops with exit code 2 and a report that does not name the provider as the cause, and in the pipeline's steps that simplify, arrange and fold back, `Run.discardable` of `runtime/finalize.py` takes that stop as a ground to discard the part for good.

The structure to reach: `Sessions.launch` treats a refusal that concerns the model as it treats a usage limit whose reset time it does not know. It waits, by the same `retry_max_seconds` and at most the same twelve times, and launches the same session again with the same model. The user chose this over going on to the next model, which would let different models write the parts of one paper or of one library, and did not choose the variant that stops at once on a demand for credit. A part is not discarded because a provider refused.

What has to change together, in one correction by the repairer whose area holds `runtime/command_sessions.py`: the runner, and the place where `finalize.py` decides what may be discarded; the tests that assert the waiting, with the stand-in session of `tests/paper_cli.py` (they replace the function that detects a usage limit), and for the pipeline a test that a refused session of a step whose part may be discarded is waited for and its part kept; the two paragraphs of `reference/operations.md` that say what a run does at a usage limit, one under "The paper" and one under "Finalizing a finished research"; and the `session` event of the run's log, which needs a key of its own for the reason of a wait. The key `usage_limit_until` keeps its meaning, since a guide has told the operator what it holds (freeze document, the invariant on what is running).

Round 5 made the change. `Sessions.launch` tells a usage limit, a provider without capacity and a demand for credit apart by `retention.refusal`, waits and launches the same session again with the same model, and stops with `Refused` when the waits are used up; the `session` event carries `refused` and `refused_until`. `Run.discardable` now takes only a session that left unusable output (`Unmade`) as a ground to discard a part, so neither a refusal nor a fault of the program discards one.

### 16.9 The account name is looked for only inside paths of a home folder

A decision of the user (section 3: "C'"), on a proposal of round 6 that the three judges accepted. The finalize pipeline looked for the name of the account that runs it as a bare word (`runtime/package.py`, `account_pattern`): the archive replaced each occurrence by `<account>`, and the final check stopped the run when the word stood anywhere in the package, in a file or in a path. For an account whose name is an ordinary word the run could not end: with the account `lean` every package stopped at the final check because the library lies under `lean/`, and with `root` a declaration named `OddSquares.root` was rewritten in the archive before the check stopped the run.

The direction: the name counts as the account name only where it stands inside a path of a home folder, which is the full path of the home folder with either separator and the path component that follows `Users` or `home`. There the archive replaces it by `<account>`, and there the final check stops the run when it finds it. An occurrence as a bare word is neither replaced nor a ground to stop. The final check collects the files in which the name stands as a bare word, and `report.md` lists them, so that the user judges them; the run goes on to its end. The approved agreement of the pipeline says "no account name or personal path appears" (section 4.11); the decision narrows what the check stops for and keeps every occurrence visible.

What moves together, as one correction raised by the repairer of the pipeline's program: `account_pattern`, `knowledge_archive`, `standalone_problems` and the sentence of the archive's `MANIFEST.md` in `runtime/package.py`; the report in `runtime/finalize.py`, with the list of files and its sentence; the tests of `tests/test_finalize.py` that use a word as the account name (`ArchiveTests`, `DocumentTests`), and a test of the list in the report; the two passages of `reference/operations.md` on the archive and on the final check; the sentences of `finalize/history-write.md` and `finalize/history-read.md` that tell a session of `<account>`. Finding F6 of the evaluation (section 21) is this matter.

### 16.10 The sessions that compare a section with its elements receive the method document as the other kinds do

A decision of the user (section 3: "give those sessions full"). Round 3 gave the kinds of session that compare the statements of a section with their elements only the sections of the method document that their prompts name (section 20, "Level 2"). The design study had advised against it: those sections point into sections such a session would not have, and they use words that the first two sections define. The user was told so and decided that those sessions receive the full document again.

"Full" is what every other kind that receives the method document as a file receives: the document without the closing part that the program withholds from every session (`method_before_checks` in `runtime/paper.py`). That reading is the driving session's, and the user was told of it. The correction is raised by the repairer of the paper command's program as one item: the places of `runtime/paper.py` that compose the document for those kinds, the sentences of their prompts that name the sections given, and the tests of `tests/test_paper.py` that assert what those kinds receive. Which kinds are concerned is read in the copy.

### 16.11 The tests' publication helper goes through the runner's check of a review's output

A proposal of round 7 (from finding T10 of the evaluation), accepted by the three judges and adopted by the driving session, since it changes no decision of the user and touches nothing a running instance depends on. `support.InstanceCase.publish` writes a review's assessment by hand and queues the publication without the check that `Runner.finish` applies to a review's output (`check_session`). A scenario can therefore start from a state the runner would never produce; one test of `tests/test_research.py` publishes `elements: []` for a candidate that replaces a proof.

The direction is the second option of the proposal. `publish` writes the review session's `input.json` and asserts, before it queues the publication, that `check_session` returns no objection, as `organize` already does. Where a verdict confirms a proof that is complete in Lean, the helper makes the review's compile and carries `formal_check`; a verdict of `undetermined` on a complete proof, which tests whose subject is not the formal check already use, needs no compile. Raised by the repairer of the test suite as a whole (14): the two helpers of `tests/support.py`, and the tests whose present assessments the check refuses. Which tests those are, beyond the one named, shows only when the tests run. The run after round 8 shows them, and the round after it repairs them.

### 16.12 Text that a repair writes during the closing pass is compared once

A proposal of round 8 (from finding P3 of the evaluation), accepted by the three judges and adopted by the driving session, because the choice follows from two decisions the user has made (section 4.11): what the elements do not cover is cut, and the run goes on to its end without a person. In the closing pass of the paper command, after a passage has been removed or a statement restated, `settle` may start a repair session, and what that session writes is compared with nothing.

The direction is the first option of the proposal. At the end of the closing pass, the body of each section that a repair session changed during the pass is compared once more with its elements, and what the comparison reports is entered in `closed` and not repaired; this is what the pass already does for a numbered statement that a repair changed. The run does not stop. Raised by the repairer of the paper command's program as one item, with everything that reads `closed`: the closing pass in `runtime/paper.py` and the sentence of `REMOVE_PROMPT` on it, `package.closed_lines` and the report in `runtime/finalize.py`, the passage of `reference/operations.md` on what the closing pass leaves, and the tests of both commands.

Carried out in round 11. A first correction, of round 9, had been rejected because the report of a pipeline run (`report.md`, which `runtime/finalize.py` writes) said without a condition that the running text is covered by declarations, so that a run in which the new comparison recorded uncovered passages would have reported both. The correction of round 11 words that line by what the closing pass recorded. It also closed a case beside it: a statement restated in the closing pass was not entered in `closed` when the run stopped before its comparison had ended.

### 16.13 An attempt may take on work outside the frontier when the frontier is fully held

Carried out in round 9. A decision of the user (section 3: "if an attempt can find something new to work on outside frontier, good on them"), on a proposal of round 7. `reference/common.md` leaves to an attempt's judgment whether work on a statement outside the frontier serves the settling of the root, and `roles/attempt.md` told an attempt that finds all the work that remains covered by other sessions to end with the outcome `no_research`. The user decided that the first holds in that case too.

The direction: `roles/attempt.md` says that the judgment "there is nothing I can take on" includes having looked at what lies outside the frontier, and that an attempt which finds work there that serves the root takes it on like any other; ending with `no_research` remains for an attempt that found none. `reference/retained-work.md` says, where it describes the report that all the work that remains is occupied, that the report speaks of the work the frontier shows. The runner is not changed: while such a report is accepted it launches no further attempt, as now. Raised by the repairer of the role guides (2) as one item with the pieces in `reference/retained-work.md`; the passages are guides of research sessions, so the sentences give the reason and leave the judgment to the attempt.

### 16.14 The literature a paper needs is in the research before the paper is written

Carried out in round 9 as far as the guides go; the last paragraph says what was done and what was not settled. A decision of the user (section 3: "let's focus on ensuring that missing citation will not occur later down the line"), on a proposal of round 8. When the canon has no bibliography, the paper cites nothing, and the reading of the manuscript beside real papers reports the absence of citations and of an account of earlier work every time; no repair can remove it, because a writing session may say of the literature only what was read and may not add a work from memory (`reference/paper-method.md`). The user decided against silencing the finding and against a step of the paper stage that looks for citations. The cause is to be removed upstream.

What is known. A source enters `canon/bibliography.bib` when a research session uses it (`reference/literature.md`), and the sources step of the finalize pipeline finds, opens and enters sources of results that the library proves and that are known under a name (`finalize/sources.md`). Nothing in the guides secures that the works which pose the problem, or the earlier results a paper would be placed among, are in the canon: not the operator's steps when the root is fixed with the user, not the guide of the account of the problem as a whole, not the guides of the roles that read the literature. A research has reached the paper stage with an empty bibliography (the paper run of section 6.2; the verification record and memory note `M-0749` record the observation).

The direction is given as a case to trace, and not as a text to write. The repairer of the guides as a whole (13) takes it as the case it follows in round 9: a research begins, runs and reaches its paper; at each place where a reader of the guides (the operator who fixes the root, an explorer or an attempt that reads the literature, the peer who writes the account of the problem, the session that plans the paper) could have brought the problem's literature into the canon, what does the guide tell it. Where no sentence can be pointed to, that is the defect, and the repairer raises the corrections, with their pieces in the documents concerned, or a proposal where a file would have to be created. Two limits hold. The system relies on what the guides tell sessions and the operator, and no check or refusal is added to a program for this (freeze document, 「결함이 아닌 것」). And the passages are for research sessions and the operator: they give the reason (a paper without the literature of its problem cannot be placed among earlier work, and nothing later can supply what nobody read) and leave the judgment of what is worth reading to the reader.

Round 9 traced the case and applied three corrections. `reference/literature.md` now tells a session that reads the literature to bring the work that poses the problem, and the earlier results on it, into the canon, and `reference/records.md` widens what the `## Literature` part of a record holds accordingly. `roles/explore.md` says the same to the explorer that holds `q0`. `reference/operations.md` says how the source of a proposition taken from the literature is put into the canon folder when the root is fixed, and no longer reads as asking for the `source` key on a proposition that was posed without a proof; with that key the paper command takes the root as cited and not as proved. One thing was not followed to its end: whether, in an instance whose root is settled through another declaration, the `## Literature` part of the root's record reaches the sessions of the paper command.

### 16.15 A restore session removes the declarations that point at a theorem without a proof

Carried out in round 11; the last paragraph says what the correction did. A decision of the user (26 November, 10:33:11Z: "yeah do option 1"), on a proposal of round 9. A finished research holds, beside the proof of its root, theorems that were never proved. The root's proof does not rest on them, since the root is proved from the allowed axioms. `take` of `runtime/package.py` leaves such theorems out and keeps every declaration that is no element. Where a declaration of that kind, a private lemma for instance, names a theorem that was left out and stands in a module that stays, the module no longer compiles and a restore session is started. `finalize/restore.md` tells that session to put back what the build needs, so it puts the theorem back. `restored_files` and `Run.take` accept the result on the build alone. The check of the merged library (`index_problems`) then stops the run at `2.4-merge`, and at the same place every time the command is given again. The restore session reads two passages that point different ways in this case: `finalize/restore.md` (restore what the build needs) and `finalize/common.md` (no file a session returns may hold an open proof).

What the user chose is the first option as the driving session had put it to the user: "The restore session does not put such a theorem back; it deletes the private declarations that point at it. The program refuses output that restores an unproved theorem and checks that only lines were removed, kept elements are intact, and the build passes." That sentence is the decision. What follows spells it out and is the driving session's.

A restore session does not put back a theorem that is not proved from the allowed axioms. It deletes, whole, each declaration that is no element and names such a theorem, and with it each declaration that is no element and uses one of those. The program checks three things. An output in which such a theorem has come back is refused. A returned file is the file as it stood before the take with lines removed and none added, and the text of every element that stays or was put back is intact. And the library builds, as now. What was deleted is entered in `decisions.md`. If something that stays did use a deleted declaration, the build fails and the run stops at `2.1-take` with the cause, which is the earliest place it can stop.

What moves together, as one item raised by the repairer of the pipeline's program (10): `take`, which is to report the names of the theorems it left out for want of a proof so that the restore step knows which they are, and `restored_files`, in `runtime/package.py`; `Run.take` in `runtime/finalize.py`; the passages of `finalize/restore.md` on what is put back, so that this instruction and `finalize/common.md` say one thing for the case (either file may be the one that changes); and the tests of the restore step in `runtime/tests/test_finalize.py`, with two new cases (a theorem without a proof that the build asks for is not put back and the declarations naming it are gone; an output that puts such a theorem back is refused), together with the stand-in session of `runtime/tests/finalize_cli.py` as far as those cases need it.

The user was told what this rests on: the program cannot verify that exactly the right declarations were deleted, only that nothing was added, that nothing kept was altered, and that the library builds. The other way of closing the case, in which the take script removes those declarations itself, was put to the user beside this one and was not chosen. It would have needed the place of each such declaration in its file, which the index does not record. It is not raised again.

Round 11 applied the correction. `take` reports the theorems it left out for want of a proof; `restored_files` refuses an output in which one of them has come back and accepts a returned file only as the earlier file with lines removed; `finalize/restore.md` tells the session to delete the declarations that name such a theorem and those that use them; and what was deleted is entered in `decisions.md`. One case beside it was seen and not raised: a declaration that is no element and has an open proof of its own. It has been handed to the repairer of the pipeline's program to verify (section 21).

### 16.17 A message can be sent into a session while its turn is running, and the host sends one before a compaction

Decisions of the user of 26 November. Carried out in round 12; the last paragraph of this section says what the correction did. Round 11 had written it out first, and that item was rejected for one test (below, "What round 11 wrote, and why it was not applied").

After round 11 the user decided what follows the message: a session whose task is not done hands it over to a new session of its role, and automatic compaction is switched off (section 16.24). That is built on this direction in round 13. This direction is the part that stands by itself: the way to send a message, the worker's hook, the operator's command, and the host's message at 800,000 tokens.

The user asked whether a session can be prompted automatically, at a certain count of tokens, "to wrap up its session before autocompact" (11:06:25Z). The driving session answered that the host can speak to a session only between turns, and named three ways of reaching a session inside a turn: A, a hook in the worker's own settings; B, feeding the CLI a stream of input; C, ending the CLI and resuming the conversation. It recommended A, after a trial on a real session. The user decided:

> "let's go with A', but expanded: a generalized message system that can be used to send any message to the running session. also, disable all project hooks that the worker does not need so they don't hamper" (11:13:18Z)

and then set two values: "let's fire at 850k and raise autocompact to 950k" (11:17:14Z), corrected a minute later to "hmm, no 800k would be more safe for threshold" (11:18:21Z).

In this section a *message* is the new thing: a text put where the hook finds it and handed to a session inside a turn. What sessions send one another today, and the runner's notice that the canon changed, are called coordination messages; a session receives those when it asks for updates, and this direction leaves them as they are.

**What is the user's, and what is not.** Four things are decided by the user:

- there is a general way to send a message, any message, to a session that is running, and it is built on a hook of the worker's own (the way called A);
- the hooks of the operator's project and account do not run in a worker. The user's words are "all project hooks that the worker does not need"; a worker needs none of them, so the driving session reads this as: all are kept out;
- when a session's context reaches 800,000 tokens, it is sent a message that directs it to wrap up before the compaction;
- the compaction window is 950,000 tokens. The driving session made that change before round 11, in `runtime/launch.py`, in the sentence of `reference/operations.md` that states the value and in the two assertions of `tests/test_context.py`. The user has since decided to switch automatic compaction off (section 16.24). Until that direction is carried out the window stays as it is, and this item does not change it.

The user had also decided, earlier the same day, that a worker's system prompt should state what a compaction does and tell a session to write a finished part down, and withdrew both when the message system stood beside them ("yes, drop it entirely", 12:35:25Z; the end of this section 16 records it). So nothing is added to a worker's system prompt, by this direction or by another.

Everything else in this section is the driving session's design for carrying those four out: which event the hook is attached to, where messages wait and in what form, who the senders are and how the operator sends, and how the wrap-up fits the way a session ends. A repairer who finds a better course in the files takes it and says why (freeze document, 「무엇을 찾는가」, last paragraph). Three settings of the driving session were told to the user in so many words: the coordination messages keep their present delivery; the message at 800,000 tokens is sent once in the life of a session; and a Codex worker receives a message only if its documentation shows how. These three are the driving session's as well, and a repairer may depart from them in the same way.

**What is known.** A worker session is one call of the CLI for each turn, and its host can speak to it only between turns (`runtime/ending.py`, `supervise` and `run_turn`). A first turn runs for hours, and a compaction that falls inside it removes reasoning the session has not yet written down (section 22). The CLI runs hooks, commands of the operator's choosing at fixed events, and inserts what a hook returns into the conversation. Workers are launched with hooks switched off, so that the hooks of the operator's project and account do not run inside them (`runtime/launch.py`). The driving session tried the mechanism on real sessions twice; the record of both trials is `임시/do-math_검증_20261125-3/조사/E2_메시지_훅_시험.md`, with the scripts beside it. Together they established seven things:

- A hook named in the worker's own settings file runs under `--setting-sources ''`, and a hook of the project's `.claude/settings.json` does not. What keeps the project's and the account's hooks out is that flag, which the worker's command already carries.
- Text that a hook of the event `PostToolBatch` returns as `additionalContext` reaches the session with the results of that batch of tool calls, before its next request to the model, once. The session sees it introduced by "PostToolBatch hook additional context:". A hook that prints nothing adds nothing.
- The same hook runs inside a sub-agent, and there its input carries `agent_id` and `agent_type`; in the session itself it carries neither.
- The stream the host records (`cli.stdout.jsonl`) shows neither the hook nor the delivered text.
- A hook given as a program and its arguments (`command` with `args`, which the CLI starts without a shell) works on this machine, and a message was handed over once and moved to where delivered messages are kept.
- A hook that ends with exit code 1 does not disturb the session: the turn goes on as if the hook had printed nothing.
- **A hook whose program file is missing ends the turn.** The interpreter then ends with exit code 2, and the CLI treats exit code 2 at this event as "stop before the next request to the model". The turn ended after its first tool call, and the CLI reported it as a success: a `result` event of subtype `success` with `stop_reason` `tool_use`, and exit code 0. A host sees nothing wrong.

These cannot be followed in the files of the skill, since they are the behaviour of the CLI. A correction may rest on them as the record states them, and the freeze document tells a judge how to treat them (영역 배분, 「아직 수행되지 않은 방향」).

One more fact is needed and comes from the streams the running instance retains, where anyone can read it (`.skill-workspace/sunflower-20261122/sessions/<session>/cli.stdout.jsonl`). An event of type `assistant` carries `message.usage`, and for a request of the session itself (an event without `parent_tool_use_id`) the sum of `input_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens` and `cache_read_input_tokens` is the size of the context of that request. On one such stream that sum stood at 862,618 just before a compaction whose `pre_tokens` was 885,564.

**What the last of them asks of the correction.** Once workers carry the hook, anything that makes the hook's command end with exit code 2 ends every turn of every Claude research session after one tool call, silently. The skill's folder being replaced or moved while sessions run would do it, since the command names a file of the skill. The correction has to close this, and a test has to hold it closed. Two ways were seen, and the repairer chooses or finds a third: the command does not end with exit code 2 when the program cannot be opened or fails (a short program text given to the interpreter on its command line can open the hook program, and end with 0 or 1 whatever goes wrong); or the hook program a session runs is a copy in that session's own folder, written when the session's folders are created. The first keeps one program for all sessions and depends only on the interpreter; the second leaves a session untouched by a change of the skill, as its settings file already is. Whatever is chosen, the hook program itself ends with exit code 2 on no path. What the CLI does when the hook's command cannot be started at all, the interpreter being gone, was not tried.

**The structure to reach.** It has five parts.

*Where messages wait.* Each session is to have a place under its own folder where the messages addressed to it wait, one file for each. A message is written whole, so that it is never read half-written, and messages are handed over in the order in which they were written. A message that has been handed over is kept, apart from those that still wait, because nothing else records what a session was told. Each message says who sent it, so that the session can tell: the runner, which is the word a session's guides use for the programs that run it and covers what the host sends, or the operator.

*The worker's hook.* The settings file of a worker that receives messages is to name one hook, at `PostToolBatch`, in place of the key that switches hooks off. A worker that receives none keeps that key: a session whose runner made no place for messages, and a session of the two commands. The hook is a program of the skill, `runtime/message_hook.py`; the file exists with one line that says what it is to hold, and nothing refers to it yet. When its input carries no `agent_id`, the hook hands over the messages that wait for the session and moves them to where delivered messages are kept. In a sub-agent, and when nothing waits, it prints nothing. It reads its input to the end, since the input carries the results of the tool calls and can be large. It is started after every batch of tool calls of every session, so it loads nothing it does not need. A failure of its own must not disturb the session: it leaves the messages where they wait. `--setting-sources ''` stays, and a test asserts that a worker's command carries it whenever the worker's settings hold a hook, because that flag is then the only thing between a worker and the hooks of the operator's project. `launch.worker_command` also builds the command of the sessions of the paper command and of the finalize pipeline (`runtime/command_sessions.py`); those sessions receive no hook.

*Who sends.* The senders use one function of the runtime that writes a message for a session.

- The host, while a turn runs. Today it looks at the session every tenth of a second to see whether it has ended or is to be cancelled (`run_turn`). It is also to read, from the session's stream, how large the context of the session's own requests has become, leaving out the requests of sub-agents, and when that reaches 800,000 tokens it is to send the session one message, once in the life of the session, whatever its role. The figure is a constant beside the compaction window in `runtime/launch.py`.
- The operator, by a command that sends a text of the operator's own to a session that is running. `reference/operations.md` describes it where it describes what the operator can do with a running research, and says how the operator sees whether a message was handed over. A session launched before this correction has no hook; a message written for it waits and is never handed over, and the documentation says so.

*What the session is told.* A session should take a message as the runner's or the operator's and not as text of unknown origin. The CLI's documentation warns that text "framed as out-of-band system commands can trigger Claude's prompt-injection defenses", and advises statements of fact. Two things serve this: the message itself says who sent it and states its facts first, and the guide that describes a session's folder (`reference/tools.md`) says that the runner can hand a session a message while it works and how one appears.

The message the host sends at 800,000 tokens is prose a session reads and is held to what the freeze document asks of a guide. It states the facts first: how full the context is, and at about what size the client will replace the conversation with a summary. For what a summary does not keep it names the guide's section on a compaction and does not restate it. From that it asks the session to wrap up: to bring its files to the state from which its work can be taken over, and to end its turn in the way its role ends one. Asking this once, when the context is nearly full, is not the instruction the user withdrew, which would have told a session to write each finished part down as it works. The user's words for it are "wrap up its session before autocompact". What wrapping up consists of differs by role, and the guides of each role already say how that role ends; the message points there and does not restate it. One more thing bears on its wording. In five retained streams of the running instance the provider's safeguard stopped a response, and two of those stops were classified as extraction of reasoning, one of them right after a compaction. A text that reads as asking a session to put its reasoning into words may meet the same refusal. The message therefore asks for the products of the work (a statement settled on, a proof that closed, the result of a computation, a line of attack that failed and where it stopped) and not for the session's reasoning.

*How this fits the way a session ends.* The freeze document keeps the occasions on which a session may end narrow, and keeps the host's second look: a session that ends its turn is resumed, up to `max_end_resumptions` times, to judge its ending again (불변식 「연구를 계속하게 하는 장치를 약화하지 않는다」). The user's decision gives a session no new ground for giving up research. The message tells a session that its conversation is about to be replaced and asks it to put its work into its files first, so that the work goes on from the files. The freeze document says, in that invariant, that a session which ends its turn on this message has not been given a wider ground for ending. This item leaves the resumption, its limit and the request to judge the ending again exactly as they are: `supervise` is not changed. Round 11 had put a paragraph in front of that request for a turn that ended at 800,000 tokens or more; it is not written again, because the user has decided what follows the message (section 16.24) and the next round writes that. The text of the message stands in one place in the program, since the next round changes it.

**What round 11 wrote, and why it was not applied.** The repairer of standpoint 19 wrote the whole direction as one item of twenty-five pieces. It was not applied, for this reason: the item added a test to `runtime/tests/test_context.py` that dispatches an attempt and a review through the runner and asserts that the attempt runs on Codex and the review on Claude. In that module's instance the settings come from `fixture_settings` of `runtime/tests/support.py`, which gives `roles` as an empty mapping; `settings.validate` merges defaults only into `cli`, `lean` and `acceptance`, so `roles` stays empty, `Runner.selection` then gives every role the first entry of `models`, and that entry is a Codex model. The review was therefore launched on Codex too, received no folder for messages, and every assertion after the first would have failed. A test of this path has to give the review a Claude model itself; `runtime/tests/test_research.py` shows how a module restores the role defaults.

What that item did is given here because the repairer of round 12 does not see it, and it is a record in the sense of section 1: the course is the earlier repairer's, and the files decide.

- `runtime/message_hook.py`: the names of the two folders (`messages` for what waits, `delivered` for what was handed over), the two senders, a function that says whether a session receives messages (its folder `messages/` exists), a function that returns the hook's entry for the worker's settings (the interpreter as `command`, the hook program and the session's folder as `args`), a function that writes a message whole under a name that sorts by time, a function that hands the waiting messages over, and `main`, which reads the event from its input and hands nothing over when the event carries `agent_id`.
- `runtime/launch.py`: a constant of 800,000 beside the compaction window, and the worker's settings holding the hook when the session receives messages and `disableAllHooks` otherwise.
- `runtime/ending.py`: a function that reads from one line of the stream the size of the context of a request of the session itself (the sum of `input_tokens`, `cache_creation_input_tokens` and `cache_read_input_tokens` of an `assistant` event without `parent_tool_use_id`); `run_turn` following the stream as it waits and sending the message once; and a paragraph in front of the request to judge the ending again, which this round's item leaves out. On a real stream the figure read this way stood at 862,618 just before a compaction whose `pre_tokens` was 885,564.
- `runtime/runner.py`: `dispatch` creates the folder `messages/` for a session launched on Claude and for no other, and a command `message <session> --file <file>` for the operator.
- Tests of the hook program run as a process (a waiting message handed over once; an event of a sub-agent; nothing waiting; a path with a space in it), of the worker's settings and command, and of the host; the passages of `reference/operations.md` and one paragraph of `reference/tools.md`.

Two observations that other repairers of round 11 wrote into their records, because the functions were reserved for this direction, are recorded here. `run_turn` catches `OSError` around the start of the CLI and around the writing of `process.json` alike and records both as `launch_error`, which the runner counts against the model. This one lies in lines the item changes. Where it holds, the item may carry its repair and says so in its 영향 칸; where it holds and the item does not carry it, or where it does not hold, the repairer writes into its record what it found. The other concerns `supervise`, which this item leaves alone: the test of `runtime/tests/test_ending.py` whose name says that it keeps a decision error creates no decision error, because the stand-in CLI leaves before it writes a decision in the turn that ends at the output limit, so that no test passes the two places of `supervise` that join the two problems. It is handed on with section 16.24, which changes `supervise`.

**How it is raised.** By the repairer of standpoint 19, as one item. If it cannot be written out in one round, the repairer raises what stands by itself and writes in its record what is left; it raises no proposal for the remainder, since the direction is decided. Section 16.19 gives a second direction that concerns what a session's stream holds; it is raised by another repairer and is kept out of `runtime/ending.py` so that the two share no line.

**What moves together** in this item:

- `runtime/launch.py`: the worker's settings (the hook in, the key that switches hooks off out, for the sessions that receive messages) and the constant for the message.
- `runtime/message_hook.py`.
- `runtime/ending.py`: the watching of the stream during a turn and the sending, in `run_turn`. `supervise` is not changed.
- The function that writes a message, the operator's command, and the place where the runner creates a session's folders. A correction cannot create a file; if the repairer finds that a new test module is needed, it says so in its record and the driving session creates it before the next round.
- The tests: of the worker's command and settings (`tests/test_context.py`, `tests/test_cli_adapter.py`), of the host (`tests/test_ending.py`), a test that runs the hook program as a process with an event on its input, and a test that the hook's command, as the worker's settings give it, does not end with exit code 2 when the hook program cannot be opened.
- `reference/operations.md`: what a worker is launched with ("Context, continuation and acceptance settings"), the operator's command, and where the operator reads what a session was sent.
- `reference/tools.md`, where it describes a session's folder.

**Codex.** No Codex session has run in the instance on this machine, and every compaction that was measured was in a Claude worker. The repository holds hooks of its own for Codex (`.codex/hooks.json`), and nothing has shown that a Codex worker with hooks switched on would be kept from them. So `runtime/launch.py` goes on disabling hooks for a Codex worker, the host sends no message to a Codex session, and `reference/operations.md` says that messages reach Claude workers only. The repairer of round 11 read the documentation of Codex that the investigation fetched (`임시/do-math_검증_20261125-3/조사/E1_scripts/out/docs_codex/learn_hooks.md`) and found no way there to run the worker's hook while keeping the repository's hooks out.

**What the trials did not establish** is listed in their record. Four points bear on the correction. No session with a worker's own model received a message, so what a research session does with one is known only once real sessions run with it. A resumed turn was not tried; the documentation says that text a hook inserted is kept in the transcript and shown again on `--resume`. Every path in the trials was ASCII without spaces. And the trials did not try a hook whose command cannot be started. The item names in its 영향 칸 what it relies on that the trials left open.

A session that is already running keeps the settings its host wrote when it was launched and is not affected. Sessions receive the hook only when a runner started after the correction launches them, since the runner creates the folder in which messages wait.

**What round 12 applied.** The repairer of standpoint 19 wrote the direction as one item of twenty-three pieces, and the three judges accepted it. `runtime/message_hook.py` hands the messages that wait in a session's folder `messages/` to the session, once each, and moves them to `messages/delivered/`; in a sub-agent it hands nothing over. The worker's settings name it at `PostToolBatch` for a session that receives messages, which is a session whose folder `messages/` exists; the runner makes that folder when it dispatches a session on Claude. Every other session keeps the key that switches hooks off.
