- The paragraph of `reference/operations.md` that begins "The comparison reads the index and not the Lean sources" ends by saying what the comparison does not see: a declaration that is no element, and "a definition whose unchanged text Lean now reads as another term". That clause names no cause. The replacement said that inside the definition modules the comparison of the files covers "an import under which Lean would read the unchanged text of a definition as another term", and so narrowed the cause to an import. A change of Lean or of a pinned library can also make Lean read an unchanged file as something else, the same document says so where it describes changing Lean, no comparison of files sees it, and this section leaves that case outside the decision. The reservation an operator uses when reporting `unchanged` to the user was therefore lost for the definition modules.
- The docstring of `statement_differences` in `runtime/canon.py` gained the sentence "For the modules of the root's definitions the check of the root covers both, because it compares their files", which is untrue in the same case.

What the judges of round 13 found is told here so that the repairer knows what to keep. It is no ground for a judge of this round: the pieces are new text against a new copy, and a judge checks every gate in the files as for any item.

**What round 14's item was asked.** To carry the direction out in the files as they then stood. The account above is a record and no approved text: the repairer may take that design over where the copy still supports it, and writes every piece against this round's copy, whose lines have moved. That paragraph of `reference/operations.md` and that docstring keep the reservation, whatever they say the comparison of files adds: a definition whose unchanged text Lean reads as another term because Lean or a pinned library changed is seen by neither comparison. A judge of round 13 noted besides that the punctuation at the end of the two entries added to the lists of `reference/change-format.md` and `reference/organizer-output.md` did not fit their place in the list.

One sentence of this section was wrong and is corrected here. It had described the comparison at the end as comparing the files as they stand "as the finalize command does". The finalize command does not compare working files: it takes both commits from Git with the conversion of line endings switched off (`package.git_members`) and compares those. Two repairers of round 13 pointed out that comparing a working file with the bytes of the first commit reports an unchanged file as changed on a machine whose Git converts line endings, which the Git of this machine does. Round 13's item avoided that by letting Git make the comparison. A correction keeps that property, however it compares.

Repairers of round 13 saw three things in the places this direction changes and did not raise them. They are handed on with it, and the repairer's file under `임시/do-math_검증_20261125-3/평가/` has them: `Runner.check_root` writes its request for a comprehensive review before it saves the state; `root_check` keeps the record of an earlier settlement after the root has lost its formal settlement; and two passages that sessions receive, in `reference/lean.md` and `reference/change-format.md`, describe the behaviour this direction changes. Where the repair of one of these lies in lines the item of the direction rewrites, it is carried in that item and named apart in its 영향 칸; an item of its own would cross that one and go to a merger. Where it touches no such line, it is an item of its own.

What moves together: the refusal where a candidate is applied, for a peer's change and for an organizer's rearrangement; the comparison in the check of the root; tests through the real path (a candidate that replaces a theorem standing in a definition module; a rearrangement that rewrites one; an instance in which the file of a definition module was changed, which `status` then reports as changed; a theorem in a module outside the definition modules that is still replaced in place); `reference/operations.md`, in "The root as posed" and where the root is fixed in Lean; one entry in each of the two lists, in `reference/change-format.md` and `reference/organizer-output.md`, that tell a session what the preparation refuses; and the paragraph of `reference/lean.md` that begins "A statement in an untouched module, and the root as posed", if with the lock in place it tells a session something that no longer holds. The parts need each other where they share a function or a sentence, and those are one item. A part that leaves the files consistent when it is applied alone may be an item of its own, so that one lost sentence does not undo the whole; two items that need each other are not raised, because a merger would have to join them.

The item states in its 영향 칸 what a judge cannot read off the pieces: how the definition modules are obtained when a candidate is prepared, and at what cost for each preparation; what happens in an instance whose first commit holds the index in the earlier form; that nothing published so far in the instance on this machine would be refused; and what a session is told when the refusal reaches it. The refusal is a new one for a research that is running; the user decided it, so it is not raised as a proposal.

**What round 14 applied.** One item of twenty-three pieces, which the three judges accepted. `runtime/canon.py` has `definition_modules` and `check_definition_modules`. `build_candidate` in `runtime/canon_tool.py` and `Runner.advance_build` in `runtime/runner.py` refuse right after a candidate has been applied and before any build. `Runner.check_root` asks Git which of the definition modules differ from the first commit and adds a sentence for each to `differences`. The tests use a variant of the tests' canon, `layered`, and stand in `runtime/tests/test_formal.py` (`DefinitionModuleTests`, and one test of `RootCheckTests`). The paragraph of `reference/operations.md` that begins "The comparison reads the index and not the Lean sources" keeps its reservation, and the docstring of `statement_differences` was left as it was. The lists of `reference/change-format.md` and `reference/organizer-output.md` have one entry each.

**What is left.** By the repairer's record, the runner's refusal at publication is tested for a peer's candidate only; an organizer's rearrangement relies there on the same function. One judge noted two things that did not change its verdict. A preparation that reuses the state an earlier preparation kept skips the new check, and the runner then refuses at publication. And in an instance whose definition module is stored with CRLF line endings, `apply_lean` rewrites the file with LF, so a replacement with identical text is refused as well. The two observations on `check_root` that were handed on with the direction were examined and not raised; the repairer's reasons are in its record. These are handed to the same area for the next round. Round 15 examined the judge's two. The first was not raised: `build_candidate` writes the state it keeps only after the check has passed, so the check is skipped only for a state that a preparation wrote before the lock existed. The second was repaired: `apply_lean` no longer writes again a file whose text the replacement leaves as it is.

### 16.29 An attempt that has chosen its work registers it, also while it does not yet see a way in

Carried out in round 14; the last paragraph of this section says how. A decision of the driving session, under the user's instruction of 27 November that it settle what is open for the rest of the run (section 3, the last subsection). It is not a decision of the user, and the user can reverse it. It settles two of the five matters that section 16.27 left open. The other three are settled as "no change" and are recorded at the end of this entry, so that they are not raised again. It changes guides and no program.

What stands behind it. The session that operates the running instance reported that two attempts of 26 November ended without registering a section. One wrote that a section cannot be withdrawn and that it did not want to occupy a statement on which it had no approach, and then gave its time to work off the frontier. The user asked that session whether having such an attempt register is the one thing that could be changed, and decided nothing. The repairer of round 13 traced what the guides and the program say today; the repairer of this round confirms each point in the copy.

- `reference/sections.md`, "Breadth, and the moment of registration", says: "An attempt registers its section once it has decided what to pursue, and then does its research inside that section, because work that is not registered is invisible to other sessions, which may repeat it without knowing." The same paragraph names the cost of registering early, a boundary that follows what looks manageable, and says that when the shape of the difficulty is not yet clear "a breadth set by the approach you intend serves the research better". A registered section cannot be withdrawn, and the part stays held until a publication or until a review decides that there is nothing to publish.
- `roles/attempt.md` says that a part on which no approach is visible yet is work an attempt can take on and that finding the way is its research, and, under "Finish on the mathematics", that when the means are exhausted "an honest and useful account of that limit is the contribution you leave for the peer and later researchers".
- No sentence says whether an attempt registers while it is still looking for a way in. The attempt of 26 November read the facts above, that a section is not withdrawn and that its breadth follows the approach, as a reason not to register.
- For an obstacle that several statements share, the guides give three ways. A session registers a statement of its own on the one element it is aimed at ("Stating a section" in `reference/sections.md`, which also says that two attempts aiming at the same intermediate statement then see each other on the board). One session can hold several sections. And what has no form as a statement is a question, which a peer proposes and an explorer takes. `roles/attempt.md`, under "Holding on to the problem and continuing the research", ties staying power to a statement: "Stay with the statement you set out to settle and the blocked argument".

The decision.

1. Not yet seeing an approach is no reason to work unregistered. An attempt that has chosen what it works on registers it, at the breadth of what it actually attempts. The reasons are the ones the guides already give: work that is not registered is invisible to the other sessions, and a section that ends with an honest account of the approaches that failed has done what a section is for. That a section cannot be withdrawn does not count against registering, because holding the part one is working on is what a section means.
2. When the obstacle a session has chosen is shared by several statements, the session registers a statement of its own that isolates the obstacle, on the element it bears on most directly. That is the form of a section the guides and the program already have; nothing is added to the program. What has no form as a statement stays a question.

What a guide is to say. Two or three sentences in all, placed where the choice and the registration are described. In "Breadth, and the moment of registration" of `reference/sections.md`: that the moment to register is when the work is chosen and not when a way in is seen, with the reason. In `roles/attempt.md`, where it speaks of staying with "the statement you set out to settle": that this statement can be one of the session's own that isolates an obstacle several elements share. The sentences give the reason and leave the judgment to the session, as the rest of those guides do. What registration means, that a section is not withdrawn, what a section fixes as done, and what a session does about a part another session holds stay as they are: the driving session did not change them, and a correction that would is outside this direction. The user's rule of section 16.27 stands as round 13 wrote it, and these sentences add no emphasis to it.

Settled as "no change", and not raised again as proposals:

- *Many sessions at one obstacle.* Tolerated, with no limit. The existing rules cover it: one holder for one part, and taking on another statement that has been proved equivalent is no intrusion. A limit on the sessions at one obstacle, or a mechanism that knows obstacles, would be a capability do-math does not have, and is not raised as pieces either.
- *What a session leaves when it ends without a result.* The row of the report "Approaches and where they stopped", the basis `no_way_forward` and the peer's entry among the approaches tried already carry it. With the two points above, such an account has a statement to attach to.
- *How to tell whether the rule worked.* Nothing is added to the skill for it. The driving session reads it off an instance once a runner has been restarted: whether the elements on which attempts registered were on the frontier when they registered, how many attempts ended without a section, and how much of the new volume stands under statements of the frontier.

What moves together, as one item raised by the repairer of the area of coordination, questions and the producing roles (6): the sentences in `reference/sections.md` and `roles/attempt.md`. If the copy shows that another text an attempt receives describes the moment of registration (the text a resumed attempt receives, or "Ending before research starts" in `reference/retained-work.md`), it is brought into agreement in the same item. No program changes, and a test moves only if it asserts a sentence that is reworded.

**What round 14 applied.** Two sentences in "Breadth, and the moment of registration" of `reference/sections.md`, and one in `roles/attempt.md` under "Holding on to the problem and continuing the research". The repairer confirmed in the copy the four things this section reports of the guides, and found that no other text an attempt receives speaks of the moment of registration.

### 16.30 A finding published before a proof was replaced is shown as about the earlier proof

Carried out in round 14; the last paragraph of this section says how. A decision of the driving session, under the user's instruction of 27 November (section 3, the last subsection), on a proposal of round 13 that the three judges accepted. It is not a decision of the user, and the user can reverse it. It applies to findings what the user decided for concerns in section 16.25.

What stands behind it. The view of an element shows, for each finding of a later review that touches the element, a line `later review  <publication> (<status>): <reason>` (`view` in `runtime/canon_tool.py`; `settle` in `runtime/knowledge.py` attaches a finding to the elements it names). `apply_findings` records a finding with its source, reason, status, elements and questions, and nothing that says when it was published. When a comprehensive review withdraws the confirmation of a proof and a later candidate replaces that proof, the view shows the new verdict and under it the finding of the review that had withdrawn the old one, unmarked. Neither line carries a revision, and `log` does not list reviews. A session therefore reads an accepted proof with a later judgment of failure attached, which is the reading the user removed for concerns. Round 13's correction marks the concern in that view and leaves the finding beside it unmarked. At a retirement the mismatch runs the other way: the verdict moves to the statement's name together with its `later_review`, while the finding's `elements` keep the name that no longer exists, so the line appears in no view. The repairer of round 13 followed this in the program; it has not been seen in the instance on this machine.

The decision. A finding is treated as a concern is treated since round 13. When the proof of an element changes, the findings that stand on the element at that moment are from then on shown in its view as about an earlier proof, and at a retirement the finding's elements move with the verdict. The record stays in the view and nothing is removed. Round 13 marks a concern at the event that changes the proof (`proof_changed`) and does not compare two sequences, because that comparison gives a wrong answer at a retirement (section 16.25, last paragraph); the repairer follows the same mechanism for findings unless the files show a reason against it. The driving session chose this among three options (leave it as it is; mark it; drop the line from the view) because it applies to the neighbouring line what the user chose for concerns, and loses no record. What is given up is what the user accepted for concerns: if the new proof has the same flaw, the old finding no longer points at it; that proof was judged by the review of the candidate that brought it in.

What moves together, as one item raised by the repairer of the area of the canon, the knowledge model and publication (3): in `runtime/knowledge.py`, what `apply_findings` records for a finding and what `proof_changed` and `organization_event` do with the findings that stand at that moment; the line of the element view in `runtime/canon_tool.py`; the sentence of `reference/canon.md` that explains that line, and any other sentence a session or the operator receives that describes it; and tests through the real path, beside the two that round 13 added for concerns. Three things the item settles from the files and states in its 영향 칸: what `knowledge/state.json` gains, since a key that is added is read by a session's command and by a runner that has not been restarted; what a finding published before this correction shows; and whether `commands.updates` or the frontier view say anything of such a finding that would then disagree with the element view.

**What round 14 applied.** One item of thirteen pieces. `proof_changed` marks concerns and findings in the same step, `apply_findings` records `earlier` on a finding, and the element view shows `about an earlier proof` on the line of a later review. Three sentences of guides follow (`reference/canon.md`, `reference/common.md`, `reference/operations.md`), and the two tests round 13 added for concerns assert the same of findings. Two things beyond this section were put into the item and stated in it: a finding that reaches an element only through a concern already marked as about an earlier proof is marked from the start, and the line `Later review` beside the root question leaves out an element that stands under `earlier`.

### 16.31 A peer whose host is lost before its turn of judging its ending again has ended keeps the candidate its first turn prepared

Carried out in round 14; the last paragraph of this section says how. A decision of the driving session, under the user's instruction of 27 November (section 3, the last subsection), on an observation that a repairer of round 13 recorded and did not raise, because it lay in lines that were reserved for another direction. It is not a decision of the user, and the user can reverse it. It extends the user's decision of section 16.22 to the neighbouring case. No judge has seen the observation yet, so the repairer establishes the case in the copy before it writes anything, and if the copy does not show it, says so in its record and raises nothing.

What was observed. A session whose first turn ended normally is resumed to judge its ending again. Before that turn starts, its host saves the request and waits (`wait_to_resume` in `runtime/ending.py`) until `control/STOP` is gone and no block of the provider stands. If the host ends during that wait, which happens to every host when the machine is restarted, no `exit.json` is written. The next runner's `Runner.finish` then records the authorization `interrupted`. The condition by which section 16.22 keeps a peer's candidate asks that a resumption was made and that the host wrote its exit, so it is false, and the work goes to a replacement peer. The candidate that the first turn prepared never reaches the check of the peer's output. An operator who gives `stop`, waits for the running turns to end and restarts the machine therefore loses the prepared candidate of every peer that was waiting, and each replacement does the stage again; section 16.22 records two such replacements that ran for 191 and 373 minutes. `reference/operations.md` describes the behaviour as it is ("Host loss is recorded as an interruption", and that interrupted peers, reviewers and organizers receive fresh replacement sessions), so the program and the guide agree with each other.

The decision. A peer whose first turn ended normally, and whose host was lost before the turn of judging its ending again had ended, is treated as section 16.22 treats a peer whose resumed turn failed. That covers a host lost during the wait described above and a host lost while that turn runs; in both, the host wrote no exit. Unless the operator cancelled the session, its `output/` goes to the check of a peer's output; if it passes, the candidate is frozen and goes to review, and if it does not, a replacement peer takes over as now. The user's decision of section 16.22 was the third of four options, "for any failure of that turn except an operator's cancellation"; the driving session reads a host that is lost before or during that turn as such a failure. What is given up is the same as there: that peer does not look at its ending a second time, and review judges the candidate as it judges any. This does not weaken what makes a session judge its ending again (freeze document, 불변식 「연구를 계속하게 하는 장치를 약화하지 않는다」): the session cannot be asked, because its host is gone, and what happens today in its place is not a second judgment by that session but a replacement peer that does the stage again.

What moves together, as one item raised by the repairer of the area "the life of one session" (5): the condition in `Runner.finish`, and what it reads to tell this case from a first turn that was itself cut off, which has prepared nothing that can be relied on and stays an interruption; a test through the real path, beside the tests of section 16.22; and the sentences of `reference/operations.md` that say what happens to an interrupted peer. `runtime/ending.py` is not changed unless the files show that the runner cannot tell the case apart without it. The same repairer has, as an observation handed on, a second case in the same function: a producing session whose host ended before it launched the CLI is tried again as if the model had failed, and a block is placed on the model. A repairer of round 13 recorded it and no judge has seen it. If the copy shows it, it needs no decision of anyone; as the driving session reads it, the repair is that no block falls on a model whose CLI was never launched, as round 13 repaired the same kind of thing in `run_turn`. Where its repair lies in lines the item of this direction rewrites, it is carried in that item and named apart in its 영향 칸; an item of its own would cross that one and go to a merger. Where it touches no such line, it is an item of its own.

**What round 14 applied.** The repairer established the case in the copy first. `Runner.finish` tells it apart by what the host had recorded for the turn of judging the ending again (the count of resumptions and the round of the request it saved), and `runtime/ending.py` was not changed. The same item repaired the observation that was handed on: a producing session whose host left no exit code places no block on its model. That is wider than the case observed, since it includes a host that vanished while the CLI ran and left nothing. One case was left as it is: a peer whose host vanished after the CLI of its first turn had ended and before the host recorded the request is still replaced.

### 16.32 The broker places in a build's lib only the cached modules that a compile of the build reads

**The user's decision (27 November).** A repairer of round 17 raised a proposal, and the three judges passed it for a decision. When the broker registers a build, it places, for every module it takes from the cache, links to that module's five emitted files in the build's `lib` folder. The only readers of that folder are the compiles of the same build, and a compile reads the modules its own module imports and no others; in a build whose modules all come from the cache nothing reads the folder. The repairer measured, in the log of the instance on this machine, that registering a build of about 1,100 modules took eleven seconds at the median, and that the broker, which has one loop, measured no compile and started none meanwhile. Three options were put: place only what a compile reads; leave out the placing only in a build that compiles nothing; leave it as it is. The user chose the first, in the form the repairer recommended:

> "1, and dispatch a small subagent whose sole task is to implement it now, so that it can be judged in the round 18 for any potential issues too"

So the decision has two parts. The broker does not place the files of every cached module when a build is registered: it places the files of the cached modules that a module reads when it makes that module's compile job. And where those files cannot be placed, the module that was to read them is closed as failed with the reason, at the place where that failure is handled today; before, the cached module was compiled in that case.

**How it was carried out.** At the user's instruction it was not handed to a repairer as an item. One agent wrote it between rounds 17 and 18 on copies of the files, and the driving session installed the copies after checking that both Python files compile and that the static check of the skill finds nothing. No judge has read it and no test has been run on it. The last run of the tests, after round 18, is the first to execute it.

**What changed.** In `runtime/lean_broker.py`, `Broker.advance_build` records a module it finds in the cache as `cached`, with the identities of the cache's entry and with `in_lib` false in its row, and places nothing. When it gives a module a compile job, it goes through that module's `needs`, and each cached module among them whose row says it is not in the lib is placed through `restore_module` and its row marked. A module is placed once in a build. A row without the mark is read as placed: such a row is that of a build that a broker before this change registered, which placed every cached module then. A module whose cached import cannot be placed is closed as `failed`, without a job and without a key, the log gets `restore_failed`, and the build fails. `restore_module` places the files and returns nothing, and it raises when the cache no longer holds the complete entry. In `runtime/tests/test_lean_broker.py` three tests are new (a build that compiles nothing places nothing and a build that compiles one module places what that module reads and no other; a module whose cached import cannot be placed fails with the reason; a cached module that is in the lib is not placed again while the build runs) and four were changed in their starting state or in what they assert. In `reference/lean-broker.md` the paragraph on what stands in a build's lib was rewritten.

**What is the user's, and what is design.** The two parts of the decision above are the user's and are not reopened: no correction or proposal returns to placing every cached module, and none restores the compile of a cached module that could not be placed. Everything else is the agent's design, and a repairer who shows a better way in the files may raise it: that the mark stands in the row and is not read off the files in the lib, that a module is placed once in a build, that a row without the mark counts as placed, that a row closed for this failure carries no key, what `restore_module` leaves in the lib when it fails part of the way, the wording of the docstrings and of the guide, and the tests. A correction to what this change brought in, the mark in the rows of the broker's state among it, lies within the change the user decided, so it is raised as pieces and not as a proposal; it has to keep a broker with the change able to take over a build that a broker before the change registered.

**What the agent could not establish, and what it saw and left.** Its account says so itself. The stand-in compiler of the tests checks the files of direct imports only, so whether the real compiler finds the files of indirect imports in the lib is first shown by the tests that run real Lean. It does not know how real Lean opens an `.olean` on Windows, and wrote the design so that it does not depend on that. It did not measure how much the registration of a build gains. If a broker of the program before this change takes over a running build that a broker with this change registered, its compiles do not find the cached modules; that can happen only when the installation is taken back while a broker runs. And it noted two things outside its task: `read_header` reads one import from a line, so a second import on the same line is missing from `needs` and from the key, and after this change the compile of such a module may not find that import in the lib (whether Lean accepts such a line it did not establish); and a publication's build that fails for a reason that is not the candidate's is handed to a replacing peer like any failed build. The second is behaviour the user left standing (section 4.6) and is not for a correction; it is named here because the failure to place a cached module is now one more such reason.

**Where its account is.** `임시/do-math_검증_20261125-3/조사/F1_브로커_lib_구현_보고.md` holds the agent's own account: what it changed function by function, what it traced (the completeness of `needs`, two compiles that read one cached module, `requeue`, `adopt`, a request registered a second time), each test and what it catches, and where a reader should look first. It is a lead and not a verdict.

**The instance.** The broker of the instance on this machine holds the program as round 15 left it (section 6.1). This change runs there from the next start of a broker. The functions of `lean_broker.py` that a session's commands and the runner call were not changed.

### 16.33 The guides say how to read a record of failures, and that needed work is not given up for being hard or long

**The user's decision (27 November).** The user gave the research on this machine two further inputs after the rule of section 16.27, and had one agent apply them to the guides (section 3 quotes the inputs and the instruction). The first is about the approaches recorded on the statements of the frontier and the places where they stopped: a session takes them in calmly as information, uses them as helpers that tell it what to avoid in pursuing the work, and does not let them dissuade it from pursuing the subject at all. The second is about staying with work: a session does not give up because the work is hard or because it would be a lot of work; if the work is needed, it does it; and a task can be continued beyond the limit of one session's context, so that limit is not to weigh on it. The three inputs, with that of section 16.27, concern one matter: a session does not turn away from hard work.

**How it was carried out.** At the user's instruction it was not handed to a repairer as an item. One agent wrote it between rounds 17 and 18 on copies of the files, and the driving session installed the copies after checking that the Python file compiles and that the static check of the skill finds nothing. No judge has read it. The agent was asked for what the user asked for: a change that holds for every role that meets the situation and after a hand-over or a compaction, that adds no more than it needs, that is written into the text as it stands in the guides' own voice, and that has the intended effect at the moment a session makes the judgment.

**What changed.** Eight passages in four files, with no new section and no new paragraph. In `reference/common.md`: at the end of the paragraph that carries the rule of 16.27 under "Working directly on the central difficulty", three sentences on a long record of approaches that failed; and in the paragraph on the judgment that a session cannot carry its work further, the words "in this session" were removed and two sentences added, that needed work is done however hard it is and however long it takes, and that a task goes on past a conversation that fills (with the link to "Handing a task over to a new session" in `reference/retained-work.md`). In `roles/attempt.md`: where the entries of approaches are introduced, that they are there to help pursue the statement and that a long list of them, or finding one's own first ideas among them, is no reason to pass the statement by; where the guide says what is not the exhaustion of means, "the failure of one or two approaches" became the failure of approaches "however many and whether yours or recorded before you", with what a stopped approach shows; and a clause that a conversation that fills is no limit on the task. In `resume/common.md`, the text of the turn in which a session judges its ending again: that the places where approaches stopped show what a further approach has to avoid and not that none exists; "in this conversation" was removed; "substantial" became "takes a great deal of work"; and a direction that needs more room than the conversation has left is still pursued, since a task goes on past a conversation that fills. In `runtime/ending.py`, the host's message at 800,000 tokens gained "however hard or long that direction is".

**What the places were chosen from.** The agent read the end decisions of the instance on this machine. Of 315, 151 were on the ground `no_way_forward`, and all but one decision of any kind were written in the first turn in which the host asked the session to judge its ending again, so the text of that turn (`resume/common.md`) weighs most. No decision named the context as its ground, but 45 narrowed the judgment to "in this session" or "in this conversation", words two sentences of the guides used. The grounds sessions gave fell into three kinds: passing a statement several sessions had worked on for one nobody had; their own approaches having stopped where the record already had approaches stopping; and the size of what remained. Two sessions received the sentence of round 13 that difficulty is no reason and still ended in that turn, on the second and third kinds; the new sentences speak to those two.

**What is the user's, and what is design.** That the guides of the producing roles say these things is the user's and is not reopened; no correction or proposal removes them or turns them into something a program enforces, and what section 16.27 keeps as the user set it (a soft rule, not applied by review, no rule of what matters, what peers publish and reviews accept unchanged) holds for them too. Where each is said and in which words is the agent's design. A repairer who reads the changed passages in the place where a session meets them and finds a detail lost, two passages that now disagree, a sentence a session would read otherwise than the user's words say, the same thing said twice to one reader, or more text than the change needs, raises it as any defect is raised. The freeze document's rule for this round on reading a guide (목적 진술, 「가이드를 읽어서 판단한다」) applies to these passages as to any other, with the limit it states for what the user decided.

**What the agent left, and what it could not establish.** It found no sentence pulling the other way in `roles/explore.md`, `roles/formalizer.md`, `roles/peer.md`, `reference/retained-work.md` and `resume/attempt.md`, and changed none of them; the documents that only peer, review and organizer receive it searched by word and did not read through. It left two sentences as they are because changing them would go beyond the two inputs: in `resume/common.md`, that more of the same activity is not a direction that remains (at least five of the sixteen decisions it read in full used it to end), and in `roles/attempt.md`, "what you can now pursue". It says of "however many" that it changes the wording of a criterion for ending without widening when a session may end, and that a later reader may want to look at it. No session has read the new text yet, so whether it changes what sessions do is not established; in the instance on this machine a new session also receives the inputs themselves, so a change seen there cannot be put down to the guides alone.

**Where its account is.** `임시/do-math_검증_20261125-3/조사/F2_입력_4_5_적용_보고.md` holds each passage before and after with the reason, the sessions it read, the trace of what a session of each role meets in each of the three situations after the change, and six values by which the effect can be looked for later. It is a lead and not a verdict.

### Proposals that were considered and not adopted

A repairer does not see what earlier rounds decided. A proposal of the structural kind that the driving session did not adopt is therefore recorded here with its reason, and is not raised again unless the files show that the reason no longer holds.

- **Moving the section "Reporting that all the work that remains is occupied" from `reference/retained-work.md` into `roles/attempt.md`.** Proposed in round 3 and again in round 5, on the ground that only an attempt writes that report and that explorers, formalizers and peers receive the section without use (about 2 thousand bytes). Not adopted. An attempt writes the report when it ends; sessions of that role lose their guides to a compaction more often than not (section 13.1), and a session that decides about its ending is sent to `retained-work.md` by its path. Moved into the role guide, the form of the report would no longer stand in the document the session has open at that moment. The driving session told the user so on 26 November, and the user did not ask for the move.

- **Rewriting or comparing the records of declarations that the pipeline does not change.** Proposed in round 8: a package carries the records of unchanged declarations as the research wrote them, and some of them speak of the research's sessions, of revisions or of files the package does not hold. The options were to rewrite and compare every record, or to give the unchanged records to the comparing sessions only (one more session for every forty records). Put to the user, who decided to leave it as it is (section 3). It is not raised again.
- **A request to the pipeline's sources step for the earlier work on the problem itself.** Considered when the user asked whether a missing citation could simply be added (section 3). The step would have to find, open and enter works that no record cites, and something would have to hold what was read of them. The user chose to secure the literature upstream instead (section 16.14). It is not raised as a proposal unless the tracing of section 16.14 shows that nothing upstream can do it.
- **Stating the facts of a compaction in a worker's system prompt, and telling a session there to write a finished part of its work down when it is finished.** Decided by the user on 26 November (11:00:58Z and 11:02:40Z), entered as direction 16.16, and withdrawn by the user the same day, when the message system of section 16.17 stood beside it: "what about getting rid of the system prompt thing telling them to write down work? the message system seem better imo" (12:34:08Z), and then "yes, drop it entirely" (12:35:25Z). The losses the investigation found were finished pieces that existed only in thought when a compaction fell inside a first turn, and the message at 800,000 tokens addresses that at the moment the session still has its reasoning in view; one mechanism is kept and not two. Nothing is added to a worker's system prompt, neither the facts nor the instruction nor a sentence about messages, and no guide is given the instruction either. What round 10 put into the guides was not withdrawn with it: the section of `reference/retained-work.md` on what a compaction leaves and the sentence of `resume/common.md`, which state facts and set no rhythm of work. Section 16.24 now turns that section into the passage on the hand-over. The withdrawn direction itself is not raised again, as pieces or as a proposal.
- **A rule by which the broker deletes the folders of finished builds and jobs.** One of three options of a proposal of round 11 on the volume of the broker's records (section 16.20 carries out another). It needs a decision on when a record may go, and that decision is the user's; the user has been told and has not asked for it. It is not raised again unless the user asks.
- **Giving a session its pack in the first prompt, and not as a file it reads.** Proposed in round 14; the three judges accepted it as a real cost. A session reads `pack.md` through about twelve calls of its reading tool. On 21 live sessions of the instance on this machine that took a median of 13 requests, with a median of 1,359,744 tokens read from the cache, before the session began its work; the pack itself is about 150 thousand tokens. The proposal puts the text of the pack into the first prompt and keeps `pack.md` as a file. Two things cannot be established from the files: whether the Codex CLI accepts a prompt of that size on its standard input, and how a session treats a pack it received in that way, at its start and after a hand-over. The user left such decisions to the driving session for the rest of the run, and it did not adopt this one, because it changes how every session of a running research is started and rests on behaviour of the CLI that no session has shown. It is put to the user with the figures when the run ends. It is not raised again in this run, as a proposal or as pieces.

## 17. A design of the guides that research sessions receive

On 26 November the user said how repetition among the guides is to be restructured (section 3): not by counting how often something is told where, but by reading and understanding what is written where, how and why, together with an understanding of the roles, of what each document should contain and of what each pack should contain through its composition. This section gives that understanding in outline. It was made by an agent that read, in full, every document a research session can receive (the thirteen documents of `reference/` that are not the user's, the user's two texts, the six role guides, the eight resume texts) and the programs that assemble a pack and a resume text, on the tree at `5a298b8e`; none of those files was changed by the commit of section 6.3. Its report is `임시/do-math_검증_20261125-3/조사/C1_가이드_설계.md` (112 thousand bytes). **The report is the design; this section is its outline.** A repairer whose area holds guides for research sessions reads the report's "At a glance", its parts 1 to 3 as far as they concern the area's documents and roles, and in part 4 every matter that has a place in the area's documents; the repairer of assignment 13 reads its "At a glance" and, of the rest, what concerns the pack it follows in the round. A judge reads the matter that a correction cites.

The report describes the tree at `5a298b8e`. Round 2 carried out many of its arrangements, so a repairer reads the present copy, and where a place already stands as an arrangement has it, nothing is to be done there. The design is the driving session's adoption, made on the user's instruction. It is not the user's words, and it is not approved text. A repairer reads the places of a matter to their end in the files before acting on it, and where carrying an arrangement out would lose a detail or leave a role without something it needs, does not carry it out there and says why. A judge does not approve a correction because the design names it; the judge checks in the files that every detail of a telling that goes stands in the place that remains, and that every role which needs the matter receives that place.

### Four words

The **home** of a matter is the one place where it is told in full, with its reasons and cases. An **address** is a pointer that names a document and a section and says nothing of what stands there. An **application** is a telling that holds facts of one role's situation which the general telling does not hold: what this role looks at, at which point of its work, and what it does then. A **second copy** is the same statement given again to a reader who has it, at a place where it adds no fact.

### What decides whether two tellings repeat each other

Who reads both, when, and what the second one does there. Reading the documents with the roles in mind gives five kinds of telling, and only the last is repetition to remove:

1. The explanation of the system, once, in `reference/common.md`, for the roles that have to understand it.
2. The application in a role guide.
3. The contract in the one reference that is open when the session acts: a format, a command, what a check requires.
4. The telling at a moment when the rest is gone: a resume text, the ending sections of `reference/retained-work.md`, "Checking and ending" of `reference/tools.md`, and the late sections of the guides of the three roles whose context is compacted.
5. The second copy.

Real use divides the roles in two (section 13.1). An attempt, an explorer or a peer lost its guides to a compaction in 157 of 207 sessions, at a median of under two hours; a review, an organizer or a formalizer never did. So in `roles/review.md`, `roles/organizer.md` and `roles/formalizer.md` a passage that states again what `common.md` explained reaches a reader who still holds the explanation, and what does work there is the application. In `roles/attempt.md`, `roles/explore.md` and `roles/peer.md` the same holds for the sections a session uses at its start (choosing work, taking a question, reading the producer's work) and does not hold for the sections it uses late (holding on, leaving the work, making the change, finishing), where a clause that carries the norm being applied does work. The measurement is of one instance on one client, with few formalizers; the report says so.

### What each document is for

A passage can be held against the purpose of its document.

| Document | Purpose | Who receives it |
|---|---|---|
| `reference/common.md` | Explains the research as a cooperation to every role: the root, the canon, an element, a standing, the frontier, a question, the board, what each role does for the others, and the norms of the work with their reasons. A passage belongs when at least two roles need it to understand their own work or one another's, or when it fixes a word other guides use. | all six |
| a role guide (`roles/<role>.md`) | The role's own work in the order in which it is done: the judgments that are the role's, with the reasons and cases by which they are made, the fields of its assignment, the commands no other role uses. A passage belongs when it holds something of this role's situation. | its role |
| `reference/writing.md` | How to write what another session will read without the conversation that produced it; how each text of the canon reads to the session that opens it. | all six |
| `reference/canon.md` | Where the canon and the views derived from it are, the form of a Lean file of the library, the reading commands, what a view shows. | all six |
| `reference/lean.md` | What a session needs in order to compile and to read what a compile shows: the commands, their results, the estimate a submission carries, how to write Lean that compiles cheaply, what makes a statement formal. | all six |
| `reference/tools.md` | The session's own folders, how a command of `input.json` is called, the coordination commands, other computations and the literature server, delegation, the check before ending. | all six |
| `reference/literature.md` | How to search the literature, what to keep of a search, how a result taken from a source enters the canon. | attempt, explore, peer, review |
| `reference/sections.md` | How a section is stated, how broad it is, when it is registered, the four commands and their request file. | attempt, peer |
| `reference/retained-work.md` | What a producing session leaves (the report, the Lean files, the outcome), and how a session ends (the end decision, the outcome without research, the availability report). The second has to be complete here: a resumed session is sent to this file by its path at the moment it decides. | attempt, explore, formalizer, peer |
| `reference/records.md` | The formats of the written texts of the canon and the criteria for the weight of an element. | peer, review, organizer |
| `reference/change-format.md` | The form of a candidate, what each kind of change requires, the command that prepares it. | peer, review, organizer |
| `reference/review-output.md`, `organizer-output.md` | The two output files and what the transport check holds each to. | review; organizer |
| the resume texts | At a resumption: the state the session is in and the standard by which it decides to go on or to end; for a review and an organizer, what the check found and what is lost with the CLI process. For an attempt, an explorer and a peer this text and what it sends them to may be all they hold of the guides. | the resumed session |

Part 2 of the report lists, for each document, the passages that do not belong in it by its purpose and what it gains; part 3 goes through each pack as its reader meets it and says which shared parts a role has no use for and what a role is not told.

### The main lines of the arrangement

- **A criterion that one role applies and others anticipate has its home in `common.md`; the applying role's guide keeps its application and loses its copy of the criterion.** The admission and the resolution of questions, which proof takes the place of which, the criterion for a division, what follows the weight, the three kinds of thing an attempt takes on, the kinds of assertion that can be stated.
- **A matter that only one role acts on leaves `common.md` for that role's guide, and `common.md` keeps the sentence the other roles need.** That `q0` is taken first and the exception of a merge go to `roles/explore.md` and `roles/organizer.md`; what `common.md` says of one role's outcome and of what the check requires of each role goes to `retained-work.md` and to `tools.md`.
- **A contract stands in one reference.** How a record and an approach entry are written: `writing.md`; their format: `records.md`. How an understanding text is written: `roles/peer.md`. Where the canon is and how it is read: `canon.md`, which takes over what `tools.md` tells a second time. The copy of the canon and the build against it: `lean.md`; `retained-work.md` keeps what the producing session writes in its copy and hands on. Where the imported library lies: `lean.md`. Delegation: `tools.md`, with the design of the three sub-reviews in `roles/review.md`.
- **A rule of one sentence stays at the point of action.** That a new element goes into a new module, that a section cannot be withdrawn, that a replacement needs a section, the three kinds of answer to a question: each stands in the document a session has open when it acts, in one clause, and an address would be longer than the clause. The reason stands once, in `common.md`. Matter M49 of the report gives the table.
- **The twelve rules the audit of 24 November counted** (section 13.2). By this reading four stay in every place: a new element in a new module, the written proof as the argument of the Lean proof, a citation that does not supply the argument of application, a section that cannot be withdrawn. For the other eight one of the places is a second copy for a reader who holds the first, and the design names a home.

### The matters

Part 4 of the report treats 49 matters, each with every place where it is told, what each place says and in what form, why it is there, and the arrangement: the home, what each other place keeps, and the details that have to travel to the place that remains. The list below names each matter and its home so that a repairer can find the matters of a document; the arrangement itself is in the report. Line numbers are left to the report.

A. The norms of the work. M1 what moves the research forward, a range that only grows (`common.md` "What moves the research forward"). M2 working on the central difficulty (`common.md`; the observation on the lower levels of the frontier to `attempt.md`). M3 persistence and the standard for ending (the last sections of each role guide; the resume texts stay whole). M4 building on earlier work, the reading before taking on a statement, a paper an earlier session retrieved (`common.md`; the retained copy in `literature.md`. The report would also take from `tools.md` the fact that the literature server's store is shared by every session; that part is not followed, because a formalizer and an organizer receive `tools.md` and not `literature.md`, and would lose the fact). M5 judging from the text itself (`common.md`). M6 stating mathematics in Lean (`common.md`; the role guides keep where the role's unstated findings go). M7 applying a condition to the cases it covers, the table lookup (two tellings with two uses; both stay and describe the table alike).

B. The canon and what enters it. M8 the parts of a record and how each is written (the section "Writing the texts of the canon", which section 16.5 moves into `records.md`; format in `records.md`). M9 the weight and what follows it, the folder of a module (`records.md`; the folder in `canon.md`). M10 the written proof and the Lean proof are one argument (the role guides stay). M11 naming in Lean what an argument uses (`canon.md`). M12 a statement never changes in place, a new element goes into a new module (reason in `common.md`; the short places stay). M13 which proof takes the place of which (`common.md`). M14 a statement that supersedes another (`change-format.md`). M15 approaches tried (`common.md` what an entry is; `records.md` the format; how an entry reads in the section that section 16.5 moves). M16 the understanding so far of a question (`common.md` what it is; `peer.md` how it is written). M17 the account of the problem as a whole (section 16.3). M18 review is a judgment and not a gate (`common.md`). M19 who reads a session's originals (`common.md`, told twice in that document).

C. Standing, acceptance, formality. M20 the standings, a proof held back (`common.md`). M21 the two considerations, where the hold is placed (`common.md` what they are; `review.md` their development, which stays). M22 what makes a statement formal, the axioms (`common.md` the concept; `lean.md` the evidence, with one definition where it has three). M23 review's own compile (`common.md` why; `review-output.md` and `change-format.md` how). M24 the outcomes of a compile that are kept apart (`lean.md`). M25 concerns, findings, the comprehensive review (`common.md`; fields of the assessment in `review-output.md`; fields of the assignment in `review.md`).

D. Choosing and coordinating work. M26 what an attempt can take on (`common.md`). M27 a division should follow the mathematics (`common.md`). M28 the lease, registration and occupancy (`common.md`; the commands in `sections.md`). M29 questions: admission (`common.md`). M30 questions: resolution and the remainder (`common.md`; the one-sentence tellings stay). M31 how an explorer occupies a question, `q0` first, a merge (`explore.md`, `organizer.md`; `common.md` keeps what the other roles need). M32 the organizer's work and its change (`organizer.md`, `organizer-output.md`). M33 ending without research, the availability report (`retained-work.md`). M34 where later sessions come upon what is left (`common.md`).

E. Tools and mechanics. M35 where the canon and the derived views are, reading and not writing (`canon.md`). M36 the copy of the canon and the build against it (`lean.md`). M37 where the imported library lies (`lean.md`). M38 `commands.prepare` and `commands.verify` (`change-format.md`). M39 delegation (`tools.md`). M40 the transport check (`tools.md`). M41 updates, and conditions the user gives later (`tools.md`). M42 the pack describes itself (the pack's own opening; `input.json` in `tools.md`). M43 compiling at natural points (`lean.md`).

F. The session's life, and what stays. M44 being resumed to reconsider ending (`retained-work.md` and the resume text; section 18 decides the sentences on what the system records). M45 an interruption, saving work as it goes (`retained-work.md` and each producing guide; section 18). M46 what a peer does with a producing session's work (`common.md`). M47 who receives which document (a clause stays where its reader acts on it). M48 a result taken from the literature (`literature.md`). M49 rules of one sentence that stay where they stand.

### Who raises a matter

A second copy that only has to go is removed by the repairer whose area holds it. A matter whose arrangement moves text from one document into another is raised by the repairer whose area holds the document the text leaves, with the pieces for the receiving document in the same correction (freeze document, 영역 배분, 「범위」). Where text leaves the documents of two areas for one place, one repairer raises the whole and the other raises nothing of it: the account of `q0` (M17) by the repairer of `roles/peer.md` (section 16.3); M35, the second telling of `canon.md` in `tools.md`, by the repairer of `tools.md`, including the pointer of `canon.md` that sends its reader to `retained-work.md` for `checkout`; M36, the copy of the canon and the build, by the repairer of `retained-work.md`; the section of `writing.md` (section 16.5) by the repairer of `writing.md`.

### Where this section meets sections 16 and 18

The account of `q0` (M17) and `reference/evidence.md` are section 16.3 and 16.4. Where a paragraph holds both a second copy and knowledge of the system that sessions do not need (the end of `common.md`, the ending sections of `retained-work.md`, "Checking and ending" of `tools.md`, parts of `lean.md`), section 18 and its report decide which sentences leave because a session does not act on them, and this section decides where what remains has its home.

### What the designer found besides, and what it left open

Not repetition, and for the repairers of the documents concerned: `common.md`, where it speaks of a session that ends without research, names two of the four roles that receive `retained-work.md`; `canon.md` sends its reader to `retained-work.md` for `checkout`, a document that reviews and organizers do not receive; `common.md` and `roles/review.md` describe the table of the looked-up case differently (one "the research already had", one "that an existing paper had already computed"; the paragraph after this one says which is right); no guide gives the peer the reason why the written proof has to be the argument of the Lean proof, which the formalizer and the reviewer are given; `reference/lean-broker.md` sends its reader to `lean.md` for the entries of the calibration table.

On the table of the looked-up case, a judge of round 4 opened the standing condition in which the user gave the case: the input whose name begins with `1795252086657333900` under `solved/parking-unimodality/history/sources/later-instance/control/inputs/`. It calls the table one that the paper had already computed. So `roles/review.md` has it right, and `reference/common.md` is the place to correct, in the same words. "Already published" does not serve: the guides use "published" for what has entered the canon, and two corrections of round 4 that used it were rejected for that reason. Section 4.5 gives the case.

Left open by the designer, in part 8 of its report: whether a compacted session would miss a clause that the design takes from a role guide (the measurement behind the division of the roles is one instance); in matters M13, M21 and M29 a reader who holds that only the reviewer needs the criterion would put it in `roles/review.md` and shorten `common.md` instead, and the design keeps it in `common.md` because the peer's, the explorer's and the attempt's guides send their readers there for it; in matter M36, whether "Working Lean files" of `retained-work.md` still serves a compacted session once the two commands are described only in `lean.md` (the report says what to do in either case). One proposal the designer put to the user and did not make part of the design: whether "Writing the texts of the canon" in `writing.md`, which is written for the peer, stays in the packs of the three producing roles. The user has since decided that it moves (section 16.5), and round 3 moved it. Where the report names `writing.md` as the home of a matter because of that section (M8, M10, M15, M16), the home is the section in its new place.

## 18. System knowledge that research sessions do not need

On 26 November the user decided that two kinds of knowledge need not be told to research sessions (section 3 gives the words): "system-level lifecycle knowledge", and "broker scheduling and cache compile knowledge". The first was said of the passage of `reference/retained-work.md` that describes what the session host and the runner record about a session's turns and endings and when the runner launches attempts again. The second was said of the account in `reference/lean.md` of how the Lean broker schedules compiles and how its cache of compiled modules works inside. The freeze document turns the two sentences into a rule (목적 진술, 「세션이 알 필요가 없는 지식」). This section says what an investigator found when the guides were read for both kinds, sentence by sentence, on the tree at `5a298b8e`. The full account, with every passage quoted, is the report `임시/do-math_검증_20261125-3/조사/C2_시스템_지식.md`; a repairer who corrects one of the documents named here reads the report's entries for that document. The report is a lead. A repairer establishes each fact in the file, and a judge checks it there. Round 2 took most of these passages out, so the lines the report gives no longer hold; a repairer reads the present copy for what is left of either kind.

### The line

A session acts on what it has to do or decide: which command to call and with which arguments, what a result or an exit code means for its next step, what it has to write and in which form, how to estimate what a compile will need when the command asks for an estimate, what will be lost if it ends now, what it can rely on and what it cannot. That stays. A session does not act on how the system brings these about: what the host and the runner record and in which files, when and by which rule the runner launches or relaunches sessions, how the broker orders and admits compiles, how it measures and calibrates memory, how its cache is keyed and when an entry is reused. That leaves. Where one sentence carries both, the part the session uses stays. Where a description of the system is the reason for something the session is asked to do, the reason stays as far as the session needs it to apply the request to a case the text does not name, and the mechanism behind it leaves. Sessions go back to `retained-work.md` more than to any other document after a compaction, so what a session does at its ending stays complete in that document.

### What leaves, and from where

The investigator found 8,481 bytes in 40 places: 4,647 of lifecycle knowledge and 3,834 of scheduling and cache knowledge.

| Document | Lifecycle | Scheduling and cache | Largest passages (lines at `5a298b8e`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `reference/retained-work.md` | 2,462 | 0 | what the runner and the host record about an ending (`:74`, `:76`); dispatch stops and interruptions (`:80`); when attempts are launched again after a report of full occupancy (`:112`, all but its first sentence) |
| `reference/lean.md` | 447 | 3,590 | "Why a compile is waiting" (`:41`, its first five sentences); who starts the broker (`:43`, three sentences); the file-level account of the cache and the measurements of two Lean versions (`:145` to `:153`, in part); how baselines are calibrated (`:163`, two sentences) |
| `reference/tools.md` | 533 | 0 | the CLI's waiting limit in past sessions (`:51`, one sentence); what happens to an interrupted session's stage (`:57`, two sentences) |
| `roles/review.md` | 502 | 0 | that the host resumes a review whose output fails the check (`:41`, one clause); where the reports of delegated agents are kept (`:45`, from the colon on) |
| `reference/change-format.md` | 75 | 244 | why the extraction compile is compiled again (`:152`, one sentence) |
| `reference/common.md` | 254 | 0 | clauses of `:311` and `:313` |
| `reference/organizer-output.md` | 243 | 0 | when the automatic organizer is launched (`:15`, two sentences) |
| `reference/review-output.md`, `roles/attempt.md` | 131 | 0 | one sentence and one clause |

Nothing leaves `reference/sections.md`, `reference/canon.md` or the eight resume texts. One role's pack loses between 5.4 and 7.6 thousand bytes.

### What the operator's documents lack

Every fact that leaves has to remain with the operator. Most are already in `reference/operations.md` or `reference/lean-broker.md`; the report gives the line for each. Ten are not, and a correction that removes one of them from a session guide carries it into the operator's document in the same correction.

| Fact | Leaves from | Belongs in |
|---|---|---|
| A session's declaration is the file `declaration.json` in its folder | `retained-work.md` "The outcome" | `operations.md`, where it speaks of a session's declaration |
| A valid decision of the session is recorded with `source: worker` | `retained-work.md` "Continuing …" | `operations.md`, the account of the host's records |
| An ending at the resumption limit is an operational limit and not the session's claim of completion or of an impasse | the same | `operations.md`, where the limit is described |
| The host moves `output/end-decision.json` out of `output/` at the end of the turn into that turn's `submitted-end-decision.json`, and keeps its check of it there | the same | `operations.md`, the account of `turns/` |
| The snapshot behind an availability token is kept in the session's `availability/` folder | `retained-work.md` "Reporting that all the work that remains is occupied" | `operations.md`, where the token is described |
| Reports of delegated agents are in the CLI stream of the turn that received them, and their conversations under `transcript-<id>/subagents/` | `roles/review.md` | `operations.md`, where retention is described |
| `status` shows for a queued job what it waits for and how often it has been passed | `lean.md` "Why a compile is waiting" | `lean-broker.md`, where the broker's `status` is described |
| Inside a build a module starts after its imports compiled with exit code 0 or came from the cache, and independent modules start side by side | `lean.md` "Building against the canon …" | `lean-broker.md`, where a build's folder is described |
| How often real proofs leave the public part of a module unchanged is not known | `lean.md`, the cache | `lean-broker.md`, beside the measurements of the two Lean versions |
| The builds of `commands.prepare`, `commands.verify` and the runner carry one compile, `DoMathExtract`, that extracts the index; it reads the changed modules as a plain file and so is compiled again whenever one of them changed | `change-format.md` (the name and the purpose stay there) | `lean-broker.md`, where the runner's and review's builds are described |

The operator's document differs from the session guide on one point: `retained-work.md` says that "the runner records" the end authorization, `operations.md` that "the host issues" it, and `runtime/ending.py` shows that the host writes the record with the value `runner` in its `source` field. `operations.md` is the accurate one.

### What is left has to read as a whole

A passage taken out can leave behind a sentence that refers to what is gone, and the correction that takes the passage out carries the rewording. The report lists thirteen such points. The four that matter most:

- **"host".** The only definition a session receives is the clause of `retained-work.md` that leaves ("the separate process through which the runner launched your CLI and which stays alive for the whole session"). The word stays in use in `retained-work.md` and in `tools.md`, and it is used before that definition in the order of every pack; the packs of review and organizer do not hold the definition at all. It needs one short definition at the first place that keeps the word, or the remaining sentences are said without it.
- **"end authorization"** in `retained-work.md` and at the end of `common.md` names a record whose account leaves. Both sentences are to be said of the ending itself.
- **The handling of interruptions.** `common.md` and `roles/formalizer.md` send the reader to `retained-work.md` for it, and the paragraph they mean is the one that leaves. Either one sentence stays there (an interruption ends the session without a reconsideration turn, and what it retained goes on to a peer), or the two pointers are corrected. The investigator concludes the first, because sessions come back to that document for that section.
- **"public part", "private part" and "reservation"** in `lean.md` lose the sentences that define them.

### Ties

Nothing that leaves is asserted by a test or quoted as the name of a section. Three pointers constrain what stays: `resume/common.md` gives the path of `retained-work.md` for the format of the decision, which stays there; `operations.md` says that the protocol "is described in" `retained-work.md`, a sentence that has to change once that document describes the session's side only; and `lean-broker.md` relies on `lean.md` for the description of an entry of the calibration table, which stays. Three numbers that section 13.5 lists as written in two places leave the session guides and then stand in the operator's documents and the program only: "three times" for the broker's passes, "four by default" for the organizer, and "ten minutes" for the CLI's wait.

### What the investigator left open

- Whether a peer is meant to read the record of the producer's ending. No guide tells a peer to open it, and the report treats the sentences about it as leaving. If a judge finds that a peer does act on them, they belong in `roles/peer.md`.
- Whether the two sentences of `lean.md` on the public and the private part of a compiled module are the reason a session needs for the convention that a module of the canon keeps its proofs apart from what other modules read. If they stay, 339 fewer bytes leave.
- One clause of `tools.md`, that a compile submitted by a delegated agent "shares your session's memory and job accounting" at the broker. The investigator found no accounting of memory by session in the scheduler and could not establish whether the clause is accurate.
- The six role guides and seven reference documents were searched for the names of the mechanisms and read around each hit, not read through. A statement of either kind that uses none of the usual words would not be in the report.

### Other system knowledge, which the user's two sentences do not cover

The report's part 6 lists eleven further groups of statements about how the system works: how Lean and the operating system account memory (`lean.md`), what the runner does with a session's output on the way to publication, when formalizers and organizers are launched, who computes the axioms and the comparison of the root, what the CLI does, what is fixed at launch, and others. The user has not decided on these. They are treated as the freeze document treats any account of a program's inside that stands in a pack: not removed on the ground that the reader does not use it, and moved out of a pack only by a proposal for the user.

On one of these groups the user has since spoken. Asked about the paragraph of `reference/lean.md` on how the operating systems account the memory of a Lean process, the user said what that part of the guide is for: "the goal of that part is to direct the sessions toward behaviors that save memory usage effectively" (26 November, section 3). This is a standard for the whole part of `lean.md` that speaks of memory, and not for that paragraph alone. A sentence there stays when a session uses it to spend less memory or to estimate what a compile will need: what costs memory, what a session can do about it, what goes into an estimate. A sentence that describes a mechanism without bearing on what the session does (how a system discards and re-reads pages, what becomes of an allocation beyond the limit, a measurement that gives no sense of size) can go to `reference/lean-broker.md`, by a correction that names where it remains. The proposal that raised the paragraph had measured it by what a session uses for an estimate; the user's measure is the wider one, and it is the one that holds.

## 19. A design of the operator's documents

Rounds 1 and 2 showed that rewording gains about one percent on `reference/operations.md`, and the repairer of that document said after round 3 that what is left to gain lies in how the document is arranged. The driving session therefore had the operator's documents read as section 17 had the session guides read: by an agent that read `SKILL.md`, `reference/operations.md`, `reference/lean-broker.md` and `exemplars/README.md` in full, with the passages of the session guides and of the method document that they tell again and the parts of the programs that print what an operator reads, on the tree at `bde1628a`. Its report is `임시/do-math_검증_20261125-3/조사/D1_운영자_문서_설계.md` (120 thousand bytes). **The report is the design; this section is its outline.** The repairers of `reference/operations.md` and of `SKILL.md` and `reference/lean-broker.md` read the report whole; the repairer of assignment 13 reads its "At a glance" and, of the rest, what concerns the occasion it follows in the round. A judge reads the matter that a correction cites.

The design is the driving session's adoption under the user's request for this run (section 3). It is not the user's words and it is not approved text. The report describes the tree at `bde1628a` and gives every place with the heading it stands under or with its first words, because lines move; a repairer reads the present copy, and where a place already stands as an arrangement has it, nothing is to be done there. A repairer reads the places of a matter to their end before acting on it, and where carrying an arrangement out would lose a detail or leave the operator without something a step needs, does not carry it out there and says why. A judge does not approve a correction because the design names it.

### Six words

An **occasion** is one visit of the operator to the documents with one purpose. The **home** of a matter is the one place where it is told in full. An **address** is a pointer that names a document and a section and says nothing of what stands there. An **application** is a telling at another place that holds what the operator needs for the step taken there and that the home does not hold, or the one clause without which that step cannot be taken. A **second copy** is the same statement given again to the operator on the same occasion. An **inside account** is a passage that says how a program reaches a result, as opposed to what the operator runs, decides, reads or tells the user.

### The reader and its ten occasions

The operator is an AI session that has `SKILL.md` in its context whenever the skill is invoked and holds nothing from the last time. It opens `operations.md` by section, through the section names that `SKILL.md` quotes (21 quotations of 17 headings), and `lean-broker.md` from `operations.md`. The report's part 1 gives, for each occasion, what the operator does, decides, reads and tells the user:

O1 fixing the proposition with the user; O2 preparing the settings and the environment; O3 creating, starting and handing over, after which the operator's part of the conversation ends; O4 answering the user about an instance; O5 putting something into a running research (an input, questions, a request); O6 stopping, restarting, diagnosing and recovering; O7 drawing the dependency map; O8 having the paper written; O9 turning a finished research into a package; O10 setting and reading the broker's limits.

The sections of `operations.md` match the occasions for O1, O7, O8 and O9. For O4, O5 and O6 what the operator needs is spread over three to five places, and the section names that `SKILL.md` gives do not lead to all of them.

### Where the bulk is

Word for word almost nothing is repeated. What these documents hold more than once, or in the wrong place for the reader, is of three kinds. `SKILL.md` tells in summary what the section of `operations.md` it sends the operator to tells in full: 5 to 6 of its 17.9 thousand bytes. `operations.md` tells a fact in the section in which it was written and not in the section of the occasion on which the operator acts on it: what the output of `status` means stands in 19 places of `operations.md` and five of `lean-broker.md` and in no table, how a session ends in two sections, how to place a question in three places, what an input reaches in five. And inside accounts stand between the steps, so an operator who needs a step reads through them, and one who needs the account has to know under which step it was written.

### What decides whether two tellings repeat each other

Five relations occur, and they are judged differently.

1. `SKILL.md` and the section of `operations.md` that it names: one reader reads both on one occasion. A second telling in `SKILL.md` is a second copy unless it is vocabulary, or a duty that no program enforces and that the operator can break before the section is open.
2. Two sections of `operations.md` that serve two occasions: a clause that the step needs, with the address, is an application. A re-explanation is a second copy when the same paragraph sends the reader to the home anyway.
3. `operations.md` and `lean-broker.md`: one reader, and each names the other. Either can be the home; the other keeps what is its own.
4. The operator's documents and a text written for sessions (a session guide, the method document, an instruction file): two readers, so the same content is not repetition, and what matters is that the two agree.
5. The operator's documents and what the program prints or writes for a person: a passage that only says again what the output says at that moment is a second copy of it. A passage that prepares the operator before the command is run, or says what the output does not, is not.

### The lines of the arrangement

- **Each occasion has its sections, and a section holds what the operator does there, in the order of the work.** Seven passages stand in the section of another occasion and move (R1 to R7 below).
